Technical report‡ · Interactive edition

A Categorical Atlas of Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning

Cones, Orbits, and Gauges†

Read a fine-tuning method as a map that lands on the pretrained point. Its image is what it can say, its fibres are how it learns, and base change is how it travels.

Xiaoyu Li University of New South Wales

Zhizhou Sha University of Texas at Austin

Chiwun Yang City University of Hong Kong

Dai Shi University of Cambridge

with Claude Opus 5.5 (Anthropic), in the spirit of Grothendieck's rising sea

‡ This is a technical report rather than a journal or conference submission. We share it, with its proofs, catalogue and interactive companion, as an open resource for everyone working on parameter-efficient fine-tuning, and we welcome corrections and suggestions for future versions.

† The title pays homage to Michael M. Bronstein, Joan Bruna, Taco Cohen and Petar Veličković, Geometric Deep Learning: Grids, Groups, Graphs, Geodesics, and Gauges (2021), whose programme of reading architectures through their symmetries this atlas extends to fine-tuning.

Overview

Fine-tuning moves a point

The argument on one page, nine checkable results and the conventions used later.

The right generality turns a hard theorem into a remark, and the remark into a design rule.

after Grothendieck

Abstract

Parameter-efficient fine-tuning has hundreds of methods, usually compared by parameter count and benchmark score. This site compares them as maps. A pretrained checkpoint \(\theta_0\) is a base point in weight space \(\Theta\), and a weight-space PEFT method is a pointed smooth map \(\rho:(Q,q_0)\to(\Theta,\theta_0)\) from a small parameter space. These maps form the pointed slice category \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\), where the frozen model is initial and full fine-tuning terminal, so every method pointed at \(\theta_0\) sits in the interval between them.

Credit\(\mathbf{Para}\) and the lens reading of backpropagation are due to Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras and Cruttwell et al., 2021. The closest earlier unified frame for PEFT is He et al., 2022.

Model referees (see How this atlas was made) checked all 41 numbered results against their proofs, the literature and the Hugging Face PEFT source. They repaired 27, a second review narrowed many of those, and only the narrowed forms appear. Some results are new, such as the HRA apex; many are known and re-derived with credit.

Three invariants

Image germ · what a method can say

Near \(\theta_0\), the weights a method reaches form a linear space, a cone, a group orbit, or a torus saturation or meet of these. Its first-order image \(S_1\), expressive dimension \(d(M)\), min-cut rank bound and base-point type are read off this germ. Zero-init LoRA starts at the apex of the cone \(\theta_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), where first steps miss directions it reaches later; a PiSSA-style split starts at a smooth point (Exposés II.A, II.B and II.C).

Fibres and metric · how it learns

Many parameter values give the same weight; for LoRA these fibres are \(GL_r\) gauge orbits. The optimizer's rates push forward to a cometric \(K\) on weight space, so isomorphic methods can train differently. With per-block rates, the compact half of LoRA's gauge acts by isometries and the non-compact half yields charges conserved under gradient flow. Through the backpropagation lens, GaLore without weight decay or full-gradient clipping is one-sided LoRA with a moving frozen factor and carried-over optimizer state (Prop IV.2; Exposés III and IV).

CreditThe GaLore identity is due to Torroba-Hennigen et al., 2025.

Base change · how it travels

Splitting initialisations, quantisation, restarts and merging move the base point. QLoRA is LoRA pointed at the quantised weights \(\kappa\theta_0\), with defect \(e=\kappa\theta_0-\theta_0\), and its image misses \(\theta_0\) whenever \(\operatorname{rank}e\gt r\), the generic case (Prop V.2). Merge-and-restart with a fixed shape reaches the orbit of the monoid one cycle generates; for ReLoRA, every update of rank at most \(Kr\) after \(K\) cycles (Thm V.3; Exposé V).

Adapters, prefixes and ReFT extend the network; seven local rewrite rules with side conditions certify when they merge back (Exposé VI).

Things you can check in your browser

  1. LoRA+ is \(\alpha\) in disguise. For zero-init LoRA under Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\), no weight decay or clipping, one schedule for both groups and the same data order and dropout masks, LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) follows, in exact arithmetic, the same weight trajectory as LoRA with \(\alpha\) times \(\lambda\). Thm III.10 · Cor III.11 · orbit explorer
  2. No initialisation buys rank above \(2r\). No exact pointing of \(\mathrm{LoRA}_r\), zero-init or split, reaches an update of rank above \(2r\). For \(r\lt\min(m,n)\), its full-rank pointings form three strata, classified as a set by \(\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\sqcup\mathrm{Gr}(r,m)\sqcup\mathcal M_r\). Thm II.1 · Thm II.2 · apex and smooth point
  3. Two apexes. Zero-init LoRA misses \(r(n-r)\) first-order directions. HRA without Gram–Schmidt, at HF PEFT's default paired initialisation, is an apex too when \(W_0\) has full column rank and \(r\) is even with \(2\le r\le n-2\). Thm II.2 · Thm II.7 · apex · Gram lab
  4. Orthogonal methods keep the spectrum. If every merged weight is \(W_0R\) with \(R\) exactly orthogonal (exact-Cayley OFT, HRA, and GOFT and ETHER as in their papers), the neuron Gram matrix \(WW^\top\) and the singular values never change in exact arithmetic, through any number of merges and restarts. HF PEFT's default Cayley–Neumann OFT is only approximately orthogonal. Cor II.6 · Thm V.3 · Gram lab
  5. Rank is a cut, dimension is a volume. At equal budget, with \(2r\le\min(m,n)\), \(r^2\le\min(m,n)\) and \(2r^2\lt m+n-1\), \(\mathrm{LoHa}_{r,r}\) reaches at least \((m+n-1)-2r^2\) fewer dimensions than \(\mathrm{LoRA}_{2r}\), buying rank up to \(r^2\) for \(r\ge3\) and strictly dominated for \(r\le2\). GraLoRA keeps LoRA's dimension and lifts the maximum rank to \(\min(kr,m,n)\), but caps each block at rank \(r/k\); for \(k\ge2\) and \(kr\le\min(m,n)\) the images are incomparable. Prop II.12 · rank is not volume
  6. Kronecker sums cut the other way. With equal square splits of a \(d\times d\) weight, a generic rank-one update has the largest Kronecker rank, \(d\), while the identity has Kronecker rank one, so zero-init \(\mathrm{LoRA}_r\) and unconstrained \(k\)-term Kronecker sums, for \(r,k\lt d\), have incomparable images. Thm II.11 · cut the tensor
  7. A conserved charge. With constant per-block rates, LoRA's charge \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\) is conserved by every continuous-time update \(\dot q=-\Lambda D\rho^\top\gamma\), gradient or not, including minibatches, global-norm clipping and DoRA's detached norm. Discrete steps make the charge drift, with relative drift of second order in the step size; Adam and momentum are not covered. Thm III.5 · Noether hyperbolas
  8. Adam sees a finite shadow of the gauge. On LoRA's full-rank locus, with channel-uniform hyperparameters, the largest part of \(GL_r\) an update rule respects is \(O(r)\) for SGD, the \(2^rr!\) signed permutations for Adam and AdamW, and all of \(GL_r\) for undamped scaled GD. Thm III.12 · gauge lab
  9. The activation decides the merge. (IA)\(^3\)'s feed-forward scale always folds into \(W_{\rm down}\). It folds into \(W_{\rm up}\) under SwiGLU for every scale and under ReLU for nonnegative ones, while under GELU only entries \(0\) and \(1\) fold. Thm VI.3 · merge game
CreditItem 1 for LoRA+ (Hayou et al., 2024) under Adam is due to Schulman et al., 2025; the general torus and the SGD law are added here. The \(2r\) bound underlies the PiSSA-to-LoRA conversion of Meng et al., 2024. Balancedness goes back to Du, Hu and Lee, 2018 and Arora et al., 2018, and the two-part conservation law is Zhao et al., 2023. The Kronecker rearrangement is Van Loan and Pitsianis, 1993; scaled GD is Tong, Ma and Chi and Zhang and Pilanci, 2024. Liu et al., 2022 note the (IA)\(^3\) merge into \(W_{\rm down}\), and SmoothQuant and AWQ already migrate scales through activations and meet the GELU obstruction.

How to read the labels

LabelWhat it means here
Theorem, Proposition, Lemma, CorollaryProved here, with the proof under the statement. Known marks a result of the authors in the margin, re-derived for PEFT.
Observation, DefinitionAn easy truth kept because it organises; a definition, with an ochre rule.
[Num]Checked in float64 by the project's scripts. Figures recompute their numbers in your browser.
[Sketch], [Conj], [Pred]A proof sketch only; a conjecture; a falsifiable prediction, with its optimizer scope.
AnalogyLanguage with no theorem behind it, in a dashed box. Noether, Erlangen, gauge and moduli are meant this way beyond what is proved.
verified, repairedReferee status; repaired results were corrected or narrowed, and only that form is shown.

Notation

Papers that store \(W^\top\) swap input and output; we translate every method into this convention.

SymbolMeaning
\(W\)A linear box \(y=Wx\), \(W\in\mathbb R^{m\times n}\), with \(m=d_{\rm out}\) and \(n=d_{\rm in}\). Row \(i\) is output neuron \(i\).
\(\Theta\)Weight space, a real vector space of adapted and frozen boxes.
\(\theta_0\), \(W_0\)The pretrained point; \(W_0\) for one box.
\(\rho\), \((Q,q_0)\)The method, a pointed smooth map from trainable parameters \(Q\) started at \(q_0\). For LoRA, \(\rho(B,A)=W_0+sBA\) with \(s=\alpha/r\).
\(\mathsf{Meth}\)The pointed slice \(\mathbf{Diff}_*/(\Theta,\theta_0)\). A morphism \(h:M\to N\) is a pointed smooth map with \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\), read “\(N\) simulates \(M\)”.
\(\lvert M\rvert\)The budget, \(\dim Q\).
\(d(M)\)The expressive dimension \(\dim\operatorname{Im}M=\max_q\operatorname{rank}d\rho_q\). The gap \(\lvert M\rvert-d(M)\) is gauge waste.
\(S_1\)The first-order image \(d\rho_{q_0}(T_{q_0}Q)\). The base point is regular if \(\dim S_1=d(M)\), an apex if smaller.
\(K\)The induced cometric \(D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\), with \(\Lambda\succ0\) the learning rates. Gradient flow is \(\dot\theta=-K\nabla_\theta L\).
\(\mathcal M_{\le r}\), \(\mathcal M_r\)Matrices of rank at most \(r\); the smooth stratum of rank exactly \(r\), of dimension \(r(m+n-r)\).
\(G\), \(GL_r\)The weight gradient \(\nabla_WL\); the LoRA gauge, \(g\cdot(B,A)=(Bg^{-1},gA)\).

Readers new to category theory or Lie groups can start with the appendix, which collects the background the site assumes.

Exposé 0–I

A method is an arrow

We model a weight-space PEFT method as a pointed map into the pretrained weights. The frozen model and full fine-tuning sit at the two ends, and sums and products of such maps already give methods in use.

Look at the arrow, and at the point where it lands. The objects will then explain themselves.

after Grothendieck
In one minute
  • A weight-space PEFT method is a pointed smooth map \(\rho:(Q,q_0)\to(\Theta,\theta_0)\) sending the initialisation to the pretrained weights. \(N\) simulates \(M\) when a pointed smooth \(h\) satisfies \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\).
  • The frozen model is initial and full fine-tuning terminal, with the merge as the arrow into it. Every weight-space method that starts at \(\theta_0\) sits between them.
  • Parameter count is a poor measure. LoRA with \(r=16\) on a \(4096\times4096\) matrix has 131,072 parameters and a 130,816-dimensional image, yet from zero initialisation its first steps use only 65,536 directions.
  • Adding updates gives a symmetric monoidal structure in which RoSA, GraLoRA and concatenated LoRAs are sums. The product of the two one-sided LoRAs with frozen frames is LoRA-XS.

The thesis

Fine-tuning papers usually compare methods by their number of trainable parameters. That number does not say what a method can reach, how it trains, or what merging does. Those are properties of a map.

A pretrained checkpoint \(\theta_0\) is a point in weight space \(\Theta\), and fine-tuning moves it. A parameter-efficient method chooses where the point may move, through a pointed map

\[\rho:(Q,q_0)\longrightarrow(\Theta,\theta_0)\]

from a small space of trainable parameters into weight space, which sends the initialisation \(q_0\) to the checkpoint. LoRA is the map \((B,A)\mapsto W_0+sBA\) on each adapted matrix. Orthogonal fine-tuning (OFT) is \(Q\mapsto W_0\,C(Q)\), where \(C\) is the Cayley map of a skew matrix. Full fine-tuning is the identity of \(\Theta\), and the frozen model is the inclusion of the single point \(\theta_0\). Figure 1 shows where each catalogued method sits relative to these two.

Following Grothendieck, we study a morphism over a base, here the pretrained point. Three invariants of \(\rho\) do most of the work, with one further layer for architectures.

CreditThe first unification of adapters, prefix tuning and LoRA is He et al., 2022, who treat each as a modification of a hidden state. Ours works on the weight side, where adapters and prefixes return as extensions (Def VI.1).
Invariant of \(\rho\)Question it answersWhat is computedWhere
Image germ at \(\theta_0\)What can the method express?Its shape (linear space, cone, group orbit or torus saturation), its first-order image \(S_1\), whether \(\theta_0\) is a smooth point or an apex, cut-rank and dimension.Exposé II
Fibres, with a metricHow does it train?Gauge orbits, and the cometric \(K=D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\) from the optimizer's rates \(\Lambda\). Under gradient flow with per-block rates, the compact half of LoRA's gauge acts by isometries and the non-compact half gives conserved charges.Exposé III; the lens in Exposé IV
Base changeWhat happens when \(\theta_0\) moves?Splitting initialisations, quantisation, restarts and merging.Exposé V
ExtensionsWhere does the network enter?Adapters and prompts extend the network \(f\). Merging is then a lifting problem decided by a local rewriting calculus.Exposé VI
CreditThe second row rests on work on factorised and linear networks, on balancedness (Du, Hu and Lee, 2018; Arora et al., 2018), symmetry and conservation (Kunin et al., 2020; Zhao et al., 2023) and imbalance at initialisation (Tarmoun et al. and Min et al., both ICML 2021).

Each result in the list of checkable results carries the scope of its proof. For example, LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) follows exactly the trajectory of plain LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\), for zero-init \(B\) under Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\), no weight decay or gradient clipping, one learning-rate schedule for both groups and the same data order and dropout masks, in exact arithmetic (Thm III.10, Cor III.11).

CreditThe LoRA and Adam case is due to Schulman et al., 2025, for the method of Hayou et al., 2024. New here are the general torus for multihomogeneous methods and the SGD law.

Where the category theory works

Categorical language is cheap, so we name the seven places where it does real work.

  1. The pointed slice gives the interval [frozen, full FT] and makes “simulation” precise (Def I.2, Obs I.4).
  2. Isomorphism invariants finer than the image, \(S_1\) and the singular locus, classify LoRA's full-rank initialisations as a set (Obs I.6, Thm II.2).
  3. A categorical product produces LoRA-XS. Intersections of images, which are not products, organise others such as HRA (Prop I.8, Thm II.7).
  4. Base change along maps that change the function, such as quantisers, creates pointing defects (Prop V.2).
  5. The lens 2-functor \(\mathbf{Para}(\mathsf R)\) separates tangent-side from cotangent-side methods. Its degree-one collapse shows that GaLore under Adam, without weight decay or global gradient clipping, is period by period one-sided LoRA on a moving base with its optimizer state carried over (Prop IV.2, after Torroba-Hennigen et al., 2025).
  6. Naturality. Gradient flow is natural exactly where cometrics agree, which for LoRA's linear gauge with block-scalar rates means along \(O(r)\) where \(A\) has full row rank and \(r\lt n\). A rule combining LoRA factors is well defined exactly when it is constant on the fibres of \((B,A)\mapsto BA\) (Thm III.3, Prop V.4).
  7. Symmetric monoidal structure bounds rank by a min-cut of a tensor-network presentation (Thm II.10).

The rest is real algebraic geometry, invariant theory, Lie generation and gradient-flow ODEs, and we say so where it happens.

More: constructions we leave out

Some constructions are true here and do no work, so we leave them out. They include coends for the rank filtration, a saturation monad, sheaf language for locality, the Grothendieck construction \(\int\mathsf{Meth}\to\mathbf{PNet}\), and coKleisli 2-cells for mixtures.

Pretrained models and methods

A pretrained network is a function with a parameter slot and a chosen value in it. Its natural home is the bicategory of parametrised maps.

Definition I.1 (Para; pretrained model). For a cartesian category \(\mathcal C\), the bicategory \(\mathbf{Para}(\mathcal C)\) has as 1-cells \(X\to Y\) the pairs \((P,\,f:P\times X\to Y)\), and as 2-cells \((P,f)\Rightarrow(Q,g)\) the maps \(r:Q\to P\) with \(g=f\circ(r\times X)\). A pretrained model is a pointed 1-cell \(F=(\Theta,\theta_0,f)\) of \(\mathbf{Para}(\mathbf{Diff})\). Its realisation is \(\Phi_f:\Theta\to C^\infty(X,Y)\), \(\theta\mapsto f(\theta,-)\). Fine-tuning is a path \(\theta(t)\) with \(\theta(0)=\theta_0\).
Credit\(\mathbf{Para}\) and the lens reading of backpropagation are due to Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras, 2017 and Cruttwell, Gavranović, Ghani, Wilson and Zanasi, 2021. See also Gavranović et al., 2024 for a broader programme.
Definition I.2 (methods and simulations). A weight-space PEFT method at \(\theta_0\) is a pointed smooth map \(M=(Q,q_0,\rho)\), \(\rho:Q\to\Theta\), with \(\rho(q_0)=\theta_0\). Usually \(\dim Q\ll\dim\Theta\), and \(\theta_0\) (or a splitting of it) is frozen inside \(\rho\). These form the pointed slice \[\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0):=\mathbf{Diff}_*/(\Theta,\theta_0).\]
  • A morphism \(h:M\to N\) is a pointed smooth map with \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\). Read it as “\(N\) simulates \(M\)”. Then \(N\) reproduces every weight that \(M\) reaches, together with its parameter path. A weak morphism drops the condition \(h(q_0)=q_0'\).
  • The budget is \(\lvert M\rvert=\dim Q\).
  • \(M\) is additive if \(\rho=\theta_0+\delta\) with \(\delta(q_0)=0\) and \(\delta\) independent of \(\theta_0\).
  • \(M\) is of action type if \(\rho(q)=\gamma(q)\cdot\theta_0\) for a map \(\gamma\) into a group acting on \(\Theta\).
More: four objects and one arrow

On one \(m\times n\) box, LoRA (Hu et al., 2021) is \(\rho(B,A)=W_0+sBA\) on \(Q=\mathbb R^{m\times r}\times\mathbb R^{r\times n}\), pointed at \((0,A_0)\). It is additive, with budget \(r(m+n)\). OFT (Qiu et al., 2023) with the exact Cayley map is \(\rho(Q)=W_0\,C(Q)\) for skew \(Q\) (block-diagonal in OFT), pointed at \(Q=0\). It is of action type, with \(\gamma=C\) landing in \(SO(n)\) on the input side. The frozen model is \((\mathrm{pt},*,\theta_0)\), and full fine-tuning is \((\Theta,\theta_0,\mathrm{id})\).

For an arrow, take LoRA-FA (Zhang et al., 2023), \(B\mapsto W_0+sBA_0\) with \(A_0\) frozen. The pointed map \(h(B)=(B,A_0)\) satisfies \(\rho_{\rm LoRA}\circ h=\rho_{\rm FA}\), so LoRA pointed at \((0,A_0)\) simulates LoRA-FA.

CreditRandom-subspace training, \(\rho(z)=\theta_0+Pz\) with fixed random \(P\), is how Li et al., 2018 measured intrinsic dimension and how Aghajanyan et al., 2020 fine-tuned language models in small subspaces. Here it is a linear object with \(d(M)=\lvert M\rvert\) when \(P\) has full column rank.

What Para adds

Remark I.3 (orientation, and what Para adds). Each \(\rho\) is a 2-cell \(F\Rightarrow F^\rho:=(Q,f\circ(\rho\times X))\) out of \(F\). A simulation \(h:M\to N\) is a 2-cell \(F^{\rho_N}\Rightarrow F^{\rho_M}\), since \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\). Hence \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\) is the opposite of the pointed coslice of reparametrisation 2-cells under \(F\). The slice over \(F\) is a different category, and its objects are extensions (Exposé VI).

\(\mathsf{Meth}\) depends only on \((\Theta,\theta_0)\) and not on \(f\). So for weight-space methods, \(\mathbf{Para}\) is bookkeeping. The network enters in exactly three places, and we use \(\mathbf{Para}\) only there:

  • extensions and mergeability (Def VI.1, Thm VI.3);
  • the realisation \(\Phi_f\), through functional images and descent (Obs II.15);
  • the backpropagation lens (Def IV.1, Prop IV.2).
CreditCalling \(\mathbf{Para}\) bookkeeping concerns this slice only. In the three places listed we use the framework of Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras and Cruttwell et al. directly, including their lens 2-functor in Exposé IV.
More: why the next exposés work in weight space

Reach in \(\Theta\) is a property of the image, and the cometric \(K=D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\) that shapes training is built from \(\rho\) and the optimizer's rates. The network enters training through the gradient it hands to the method, and enters expressivity through \(\Phi_f\) once we ask about functions instead of weights. So Exposés II and III work in \(\Theta\) and flag the places where \(f\) matters.

Frozen and full fine-tuning

Observation I.4 (universal properties). In \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\):

(a) the frozen model \((\mathrm{pt},*,\theta_0)\) is initial, and full fine-tuning \((\Theta,\theta_0,\mathrm{id})\) is terminal. The unique morphism \(M\to\) Full FT is \(\rho_M\) itself.

(b) If the fibre product \(Q_M\times_\Theta Q_N\) exists in \(\mathbf{Diff}\), it is the product \(M\times N\). It exists, for instance, when the set-level fibre product is an embedded submanifold of \(Q_M\times Q_N\), as for a clean intersection or for affine \(\rho\)'s as in Prop I.8(a). Transversality \(\rho_M\pitchfork\rho_N\) cannot be the reason when \(\lvert M\rvert+\lvert N\rvert\lt\dim\Theta\), since at \((q_0,q_0')\) it would force \(\lvert M\rvert+\lvert N\rvert\ge\dim\Theta\). When the product exists in \(\mathsf{Meth}\), its underlying set is the set-level fibre product, so \(\mathrm{Im}(M\times N)=\mathrm{Im}\,M\cap\mathrm{Im}\,N\). The product can fail to exist. On \(\Theta=\mathbb R\) (\(1\times1\) matrices), \(\mathrm{LoRA}_1\times\text{Frozen}\) would have the cross \(\{BA=0\}\subset\mathbb R^2\) as underlying set. No manifold structure on the cross has the universal property, because both axes would have to lift smoothly through the point over the origin, which forces rank \(2\) there.

(c) \(\mathrm{Im}\) is a functor to the poset of subsets of \(\Theta\) containing \(\theta_0\). Conversely, \(\mathrm{Im}\,M\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,N\) yields a morphism of the set-level slice \(\mathbf{Set}_*/(\Theta,\theta_0)\), though not necessarily a smooth one. Take \(\rho_M(t)=t\) and \(\rho_N(u)=u^3\) on \(\mathbb R\), pointed at \(0\). The images agree, yet no smooth \(h:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) satisfies \(\rho_N\circ h=\mathrm{id}\), that is, \(h(t)^3=t\).

(expand)

(a) The only pointed map \(\mathrm{pt}\to Q\) is \(*\mapsto q_0\), and it lies over \(\Theta\) because \(\rho(q_0)=\theta_0\). A morphism into the terminal object must satisfy \(\mathrm{id}\circ h=\rho_M\).

(b) Products in a pointed slice are pullbacks in \(\mathbf{Diff}_*\). The forgetful functor \(\mathbf{Diff}_*\to\mathbf{Diff}\) creates limits, since \(\mathbf{Diff}_*\) is the coslice \(\mathrm{pt}/\mathbf{Diff}\), and the induced map out of a pointed test object is pointed because its components are. Smooth maps landing in an embedded submanifold are smooth into it, which gives the sufficient condition. Transversality at \((q_0,q_0')\) means \(d\rho_M(T_{q_0})+d\rho_N(T_{q_0'})=T_{\theta_0}\Theta\), and the left side has dimension at most \(\lvert M\rvert+\lvert N\rvert\). A point \(\theta\in\mathrm{Im}\,M\cap\mathrm{Im}\,N\) has preimages \(q,q'\), and \((q,q')\) lies in the fibre product. Testing a product in \(\mathsf{Meth}\) against pointed two-point \(0\)-manifolds shows that its points are exactly the pairs \((q,q')\) over a common \(\theta\).

For \(\mathrm{LoRA}_1\times\text{Frozen}\), suppose a product \(P\) exists, with canonical map \(\iota:P\to\mathbb R^2\), and let \(p^*\) be its unique point over the origin \((0,0)\). The forgetful functor \(\mathbf{Diff}_*\to\mathbf{Diff}\) is a right adjoint (its left adjoint adds a disjoint base point), so \(P\) is also the pullback in \(\mathbf{Diff}\), and unpointed smooth curves into the cross lift to \(P\). The axis curves \(t\mapsto(t,0)\) and \(t\mapsto(0,t)\) lift to curves through \(p^*\), so \(d\iota_{p^*}\) hits \((1,0)\) and \((0,1)\) and has rank \(2\). Then \(\iota\) is a submersion near \(p^*\), and its image contains an open subset of \(\mathbb R^2\), which the cross does not. The argument covers every pointing of \(\mathrm{LoRA}_1\), including the standard one \((0,a_0)\) with \(a_0\ne0\), whose base point is a smooth point of the cross.

(c) Functoriality is \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\). For the converse, choose (by the axiom of choice) for each \(\theta\in\mathrm{Im}\,M\) a preimage \(s(\theta)\) under \(\rho_N\) with \(s(\theta_0)=q_0'\), and put \(h=s\circ\rho_M\). In the example the only candidate is the cube root, which is not differentiable at \(0\).∎

Every weight-space method that starts at \(\theta_0\) lies in [frozen, full FT]. Adapters, prefixes and ReFT are extensions outside \(\mathsf{Meth}\) (Exposé VI). Merging into full-precision weights is the canonical morphism to the terminal object, so in exact arithmetic it is definitional; quantised bases and combinations of adapters raise real questions (Prop V.2, Prop V.4). The product, when it exists, is the universal method simulated by both factors, with image \(\mathrm{Im}\,M\cap\mathrm{Im}\,N\). Prop I.8(a) computes one, LoRA-XS, while Prop I.8(b) and Thm II.7 compute intersections of images only.

More: defaults that do not start at the checkpoint

Some defaults move the weights before training starts. Hugging Face PEFT's default FourierFT draws its spectrum from a standard normal, and VB-LoRA's default vector bank and logits are random too. LoRA with init_lora_weights=False, LoRA-XS with its small random \(R\), and every method on a quantised base also start away from \(\theta_0\). Each lives over its own starting weight or carries a pointing defect, which Exposé V measures.

More: lifting obstructions beyond first order

The gap between \(\mathbf{Set}\) and \(\mathbf{Diff}\) in (c) is a smooth lifting obstruction. Obs I.6 below detects its first-order instances. In the cube-root example, \(S_1(M)=\mathbb R\not\subseteq S_1(N)=0\). Higher-order obstructions need finer invariants. The maps \(\rho_M(t)=t^3\) and \(\rho_N(u)=u^5\) have equal images and \(S_1=0\) on both sides, yet the only lift, \(t^{3/5}\), is not \(C^1\).

Invariants of a method

The budget \(\lvert M\rvert\) counts parameters. Two other numbers say more, the dimension of what a method reaches and of what it reaches to first order at the start, which is what the first gradient steps use. For LoRA with \(r=16\) on a \(4096\times4096\) matrix they are \(130{,}816\) and, from zero initialisation, \(65{,}536\), against a budget of \(131{,}072\) (Thm II.2, Prop II.12).

Definition I.5 (invariants of an object). For \(M=(Q,q_0,\rho)\):
  • the image \(\mathrm{Im}\,M=\rho(Q)\);
  • the expressive dimension \(d(M)=\dim\mathrm{Im}\,M=\max_q\operatorname{rank}d\rho_q\);
  • the gauge waste \(g(M)=\lvert M\rvert-d(M)\);
  • the first-order image \(S_1(M)=d\rho_{q_0}(T_{q_0}Q)\), and the first-order deficit \(d(M)-\dim S_1(M)\);
  • the gauge group \(\{\varphi\in\mathrm{Diff}(Q):\rho\circ\varphi=\rho\}\), which is unpointed, since a gauge transformation moves \(q_0\) inside its fibre. In practice we exhibit a Lie group acting on \(Q\) that preserves \(\rho\) and is locally transitive on generic fibres, and call it the generic gauge.

The base point \(\theta_0\) is regular for \(M\) if the deficit is \(0\), and an apex if the deficit is positive. The vertex is the subset-level notion, the cone point of an image germ. A cone germ that is a \(C^1\) manifold at its vertex equals its tangent space there, so it is linear. Hence the vertex of a non-linear cone is a singular point.

More: cones in this site, and the word “gauge”

Apex and vertex differ in general. At a crossing of two smooth branches the image is singular, yet the deficit can be \(0\). In every case below with a positive deficit, the image germ is (diffeomorphic to) a cone with vertex \(\theta_0\). The exception is DoRA, which inherits LoRA's deficit through its torus factor.

“Gauge” here means the symmetry group of a parametrisation, as in the definition above. There is no connection, and away from the full-rank stratum the fibres jump. The word borrows the physicist's picture of redundant coordinates that leave every observation unchanged. Only the group and its action enter the theorems.

If \(N\) simulates \(M\), whatever \(M\) can do at its first step, \(N\) can do too.

Observation I.6 (\(S_1\) is functorial). If \(M\to N\) is a (pointed) morphism, then \(S_1(M)\subseteq S_1(N)\). In particular, isomorphic methods have equal \(S_1\) and equal first-order deficit. Monotonicity holds only along pointed morphisms. Along a weak morphism, such as a gauge map moving \(q_0\) inside its fibre, \(S_1\) can change.
(expand)

Apply the chain rule \(d\rho_N\circ dh=d\rho_M\) at \(q_0\), using \(h(q_0)=q_0'\). If \(h\) is an isomorphism, \(dh_q\) is invertible for every \(q\), so \(\operatorname{rank}d\rho_M(q)=\operatorname{rank}d\rho_N(h(q))\). Hence \(d(M)=d(N)\) (equivalently \(\mathrm{Im}\,M=\mathrm{Im}\,N\)), and since \(dh_{q_0}\) is surjective, \(S_1(M)=S_1(N)\). So the deficits agree.∎

\(S_1\) is what linearised (lazy, NTK) dynamics can use at initialisation, since the lazy kernel \(J_f\,d\rho\,\Lambda\,d\rho^\top J_f^\top\) has range \(J_f(S_1)\). The kernel also depends on \(\Lambda\) and the network Jacobian \(J_f\), and for random-\(A_0\) zero-init LoRA it is close to full fine-tuning's, so a large first-order deficit need not cost much on data. \(S_1\) separates initialisations the image cannot, namely zero-init \(\mathrm T_A\) against \(\mathrm T_B\), and the choice of \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\) (Thm II.2).

CreditThe kernel view of language-model fine-tuning, and the closeness of LoRA's kernel to full fine-tuning's, are due to Malladi et al., 2023. Hayou et al., 2024 compared the zero initialisations \(B_0=0\) and \(A_0=0\) as training procedures; here they are already different objects.

Sums

Weight space is a vector space, so running two methods side by side and adding their updates gives a method again.

Observation I.7 (additive monoidal structure). \(\Theta\) is a vector space, so every object of \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\) can be written \(\rho=\theta_0+\delta\) with \(\delta(q_0)=0\). Put \[M\oplus N:=\big(Q_M\times Q_N,\ (q_0,q_0'),\ \theta_0+\delta_M+\delta_N\big).\] This is a symmetric monoidal structure with the frozen model as unit, and its image is the Minkowski sum \(\theta_0+(\mathrm{Im}\,\delta_M+\mathrm{Im}\,\delta_N)\). It differs from the categorical product, whose image, when the product exists, is \(\cap\) (Obs I.4(b)).

Write \(\mathrm{LoRA}_k\) for LoRA with frozen residual \(\theta_0\); for split pointings the sum below has residual \(\theta_0-P-P'\). Concatenation \(h(B_1,A_1,B_2,A_2)=([B_1\ B_2],[A_1;A_2])\) is an isomorphism \(\mathrm{LoRA}_r\oplus\mathrm{LoRA}_s\cong\mathrm{LoRA}_{r+s}\) of unpointed objects, and a pointed isomorphism onto \(\mathrm{LoRA}_{r+s}\) pointed at \(([B_0\ B_0'],[A_0;A_0'])\). For \(\mathrm T_A\) pointings (\(B_0=0\), \(\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\)), this target pointing has rank \(r+s\) iff \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\cap\mathrm{row}\,A_0'=0\). It lies in the stratum \(\mathrm T_A\) of Thm II.2 when moreover \(r+s\lt\min(m,n)\).

Standard pointings do not add up. If \(F(r)\) and \(F(2r)\) are standard \(\mathrm T_A\) (or \(\mathrm T_B\)) pointings, which needs \(2r\lt\min(m,n)\), then \(F(r)\oplus F(r)\not\cong F(2r)\) as pointed objects. So when \(F(1)\) and \(F(2)\) are standard, \(r\mapsto F(r)\) is not strong monoidal into pointed objects. It is strong monoidal on unpointed objects, and on pointed objects for the origin pointing \((B_0,A_0)=(0,0)\), where \(S_1=0\), and for the stacked pointings \(F(r)=F(1)^{\oplus r}\).

(expand)

\([B_1\ B_2][A_1;A_2]=B_1A_1+B_2A_2\), and \(h\) is a linear bijection. The coherence isomorphisms of \(\oplus\) are those of the cartesian structure on \(\mathbf{Diff}_*\), and they lie over \(\Theta\) because \(+\) is associative and commutative. The pointing claim is \(\dim(U+V)=r+s-\dim(U\cap V)\) for \(U=\mathrm{row}\,A_0\) and \(V=\mathrm{row}\,A_0'\).

For the failure of strong monoidality, recall that any pointed isomorphism preserves \(S_1\) (Obs I.6). If \(F(r)\) is a standard \(\mathrm T_A\) pointing and \(F(r)\oplus F(r)\cong F(2r)\), then \(S_1(F(r)\oplus F(r))=\{XA_0+X'A_0\}\) has dimension \(mr\), while a standard \(F(2r)\) has \(\dim S_1=2mr\). A standard \(F(2r)\) needs \(2r\lt\min(m,n)\) (Thm II.2). The degenerate cases are direct.∎

[Num: \(\dim S_1=28\) for a standard \(\mathrm{LoRA}_4\) at \(m=7\), against \(14\) for \(\mathrm{LoRA}_2\oplus\mathrm{LoRA}_2\) with the same \(A_0\).]

RoSA (LoRA \(\oplus\) a fixed-mask sparse method), GraLoRA (\(\oplus\) of \(k^2\) block-embedded LoRAs) and ReLoRA, read as \(\mathrm{LoRA}^{\oplus K}\) unrolled in time apart from its full-rank warm start, are \(\oplus\)-words with Minkowski-sum images. A sum of \(k\) rank-\(r\) LoRAs with residual \(\theta_0\) is isomorphic in \(\mathsf{Meth}\) to one LoRA of rank \(kr\). Under \(\alpha/r\) scaling the isomorphism rescales the \(A\) blocks, so at equal \(\alpha\) and learning rate the two train differently. Hugging Face PEFT's cat merge is this rescaled concatenation applied to trained adapters, exact for unit weights and plain \(\alpha/r\) sources.

More: what is and is not a sum, and the cat merge

LoRA-soup CAT freezes the trained modules and learns layer-wise weights, so it is a \(\oplus\) of \(k\) frozen line methods \(\alpha_i\mapsto\alpha_iB_iA_i\). Its per-layer image is a span of dimension at most \(k\), much smaller than \(\mathrm{LoRA}_{kr}\). UniPELT and MAM are not \(\oplus\)-words in \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\). Their adapter and prefix parts live on extensions (Exposé VI), and UniPELT's gates depend on the input.

The obstruction in the observation is the reuse of one pointing, since stacking the same \(A_0\) twice gives a rank-deficient frame. For \(r\ne s\), standard pointings satisfy \(F(r)\oplus F(s)\cong F(r+s)\) exactly when the row space of the rank-\((r+s)\) frame is the direct sum of the other two. Concatenation is strictly associative, so discrete \((\mathbb N,+)\) suffices for a plain strong monoidal functor on unpointed objects. Symmetric strong monoidality needs the permutation groupoid, since swapping and then concatenating permutes the blocks.

“Additive” in Def I.2 (\(\delta\) independent of \(\theta_0\)) is a condition on families \(\theta_0\mapsto M(\theta_0)\). It matters for base change (Exposé V) and plays no role in \(\oplus\).

With scales \(s_r=\alpha/r\) (or \(\alpha/\sqrt r\) for rsLoRA) and any target scale \(s_*\), the rescaled concatenation \(([B_1\ B_2],[c_1A_1;c_2A_2])\) with \(c_i=s_{r_i}/s_*\), where \(r_1=r\) and \(r_2=s\), is an isomorphism over \(\Theta\). It preserves row spaces, so the pointing criterion is unchanged. Hugging Face PEFT's add_weighted_adapter(..., combination_type="cat") takes \(s_*=1\). It multiplies each \(A\) block by \(w_is_{r_i}\), leaves the \(B\) blocks alone, and sets the merged lora_alpha to \(r+s\), so the merged scale is \(1\). In PEFT releases up to v0.21.1 the merged adapter also inherits use_rslora from the first source. For rsLoRA sources its scale is then \((r+s)/\sqrt{r+s}=\sqrt{r+s}\), and the merged update is \(\sqrt{r+s}\) times too large. The main branch now sets use_rslora=False on the merged adapter.

CreditRoSA, Nikdan et al., 2024; GraLoRA, Jung et al., 2025; ReLoRA, Lialin et al., 2023; LoRA Soups, Prabhakar et al., 2024; UniPELT, Mao et al., 2021; MAM is from the unified view of He et al., 2022. The rescaled concatenation follows the Hugging Face PEFT source.

Products

Freeze \(A\) at \(A_0\) and train \(B\), or freeze \(B\) at \(B_0\) and train \(A\). The product of these two one-sided methods is LoRA-XS.

Proposition I.8 (a product that produces a named method; an image-level meet). (a) Let \(A_0\in\mathbb R^{r\times n}\) have full row rank and \(B_0\in\mathbb R^{m\times r}\) full column rank. Define the one-sided methods \[\mathrm{FA}(A_0):B\mapsto W_0+BA_0,\qquad\mathrm{FB}(B_0):A\mapsto W_0+B_0A,\] both pointed at \(0\). Then \[\mathrm{FA}(A_0)\times\mathrm{FB}(B_0)\;\cong\;\big(\mathbb R^{r\times r},\ 0,\ R\mapsto W_0+B_0RA_0\big).\] The product supplies this sandwich for any frames. Assume \(\operatorname{rank}W_0\ge r\) (\(\sigma_r\gt0\)) and take the SVD frames \(B_0=U_r\), \(A_0=\Sigma_rV_r^\top\). The result is LoRA-XS pointed at \(R=0\).

(b) Let \(W_0\) have full row rank (so \(m\le n\)), and let \(\mathrm{LoRA}_r\) carry its standard pointing (\(B_0=0\), image \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\)). The image-level meet of \(\mathrm{LoRA}_r\) with the output-scaling method \(\ell\mapsto\mathrm{diag}(\ell)W_0\) is \(\{\mathrm{diag}(\ell)W_0:\#\{i:\ell_i\neq1\}\le r\}\), that is, “rescale at most \(r\) output neurons”.

The LoRA-XS paper writes \(xW+xARB\) in row-vector convention with \(A=U_r\Sigma_r\) and \(B=V_r^\top\), so the isomorphism is \(R\mapsto\alpha R^\top\), and its initialisation \(R\sim\mathcal N(0,\sigma^2)\) with \(\sigma=10^{-5}\) is a negligible pointing defect. The output scaling in (b) is the key/value part of (IA)3. On the FFN, (IA)3 scales the input of \(W_{\rm down}\), \(W\mapsto W\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\), and the mirror statement needs full column rank, or holds generically for \(r\lt d\).

(expand)

(a) A point of the fibre product is a pair \((B,A)\) with \(BA_0=B_0A=:D\). Then \(\mathrm{row}\,D\subseteq\mathrm{row}\,A_0\) and \(\mathrm{col}\,D\subseteq\mathrm{col}\,B_0\), so \(D=B_0RA_0\) for a unique \(R\). Since \(A_0\) has full row rank, \(BA_0=B_0RA_0\) forces \(B=B_0R\), and likewise \(A=RA_0\). So the fibre product is the \(r^2\)-dimensional linear subspace \(\{(B_0R,RA_0)\}\), and \(R\mapsto(B_0R,RA_0)\) is a smooth bijection onto it with smooth inverse \((B,A)\mapsto B_0^+B\). Any pair of maps \((h_1,h_2):T\to Q_{\rm FA}\times Q_{\rm FB}\) that agree over \(\Theta\) factors through it via \(t\mapsto B_0^+h_1(t)\), so this is the product in \(\mathbf{Diff}\). If \(\operatorname{rank}W_0\lt r\), then \(\Sigma_rV_r^\top\) is rank-deficient and the LoRA-XS instance breaks.

(b) \(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)W_0-W_0=\mathrm{diag}(\ell-\mathbf 1)W_0\), and its rank is \(\#\{i:\ell_i\ne1\}\) because the rows of \(W_0\) are independent.∎

[Num: the fibre product of (a) has dimension \(4=r^2\) at \((m,n,r)=(6,8,2)\).]

LoRA-XS is the largest method that factors through both frozen frames. At a split initialisation it is the overlap the split double-counts (Prop II.3), and it is LoRA-XS proper at a PiSSA initialisation, whose frames span the top-\(r\) singular subspaces of \(W_0\). The meet in (b) is only set-level. For \(n\ge2\) no product exists in \(\mathbf{Diff}\): its fibre product is singular at the origin \((b,a,\ell)=(0,0,\mathbf 1)\), where its branches cross, by the rank argument for the cross in Obs I.4(b). (For \(m=n=1\) the fibre product is a graph, and the product exists.) With a fixed support, the meet is (IA)3 masked to \(r\) neurons.

More: the shape of the meet in (b)

The meet is the union of the \(\binom mr\) coordinate \(r\)-planes “rescale at most \(r\) output neurons”. For \(r=1\) the fibre product contains the sheets \(\{b=0\}\) and \(\{a=0\}\), which cross at the origin. The standard base point, with \(b=0\) and \(a_0\ne0\), lies on a smooth sheet, so the singularity sits away from it.

CreditLoRA-XS, Bałazy et al., 2024; (IA)3, Liu et al., 2022; PiSSA, Meng et al., 2024. For sparse rescaling of activations, compare Stoehr et al., 2024.

The dictionary so far

The theorems begin when we look closely at one image near one point, which is where Exposé II starts. The full dictionary is the Rosetta stone.

Exposé II.A

What a method can say, and where it starts

Expressivity is read off the germ of a method’s image at the pretrained point. An initialisation decides whether that point sits at the apex of a cone, on a smooth sheet of one, or just off the image.

A method is an arrow before it is a thing. Ask what it sees of the point where it lands.

after Grothendieck
In one minute
  • Near the pretrained weights, a method reaches the local shape of its image, a linear space, a cone or a piece of a group orbit. Its first-step directions \(S_1\) and its expressive dimension tell whether training starts at an apex.
  • For \(r\lt\min(m,n)\), full-rank LoRA initialisations come in exactly three kinds up to reparametrisation, classified as sets by \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\) (\(B_0=0\)), \(\mathrm{col}\,B_0\) (\(A_0=0\)) or the subtracted product \(B_0A_0\) (PiSSA-type splits).
  • Zero-factor starts sit at the apex of a cone with \(mr\) or \(nr\) first-order directions, split starts at a smooth point with all \(r(m+n-r)\), which for a \(4096\times4096\) matrix at rank 16 is 130,816 against 65,536. Neither kind reaches everything the other reaches.
  • No exact start of LoRA\(_r\) reaches an update of rank above \(2r\). A factor behind a closed gate, such as \(A\) when \(B_0=0\), does not move the weights at first order.

The image germ

Exposé I turned a PEFT method into a pointed map \(\rho:(Q,q_0)\to(\Theta,\theta_0)\). Its image \(\mathrm{Im}\,M=\rho(Q)\) is everything it can reach. Fine-tuning travels only a short way from the pretrained weights, so what matters is the image near \(\theta_0\), its germ at \(\theta_0\).

The germ takes few shapes. It is a linear space when \(\rho\) is affine, as for BitFit, LoRA-FA, LoRA-XS, FourierFT and (IA)³. It is a cone when \(\rho\) is multihomogeneous and non-linear and starts where the update vanishes, as LoRA’s \(BA\) does at zero initialisation. It is a piece of a group orbit for exactly orthogonal methods such as exact-Cayley OFT. DoRA adds an output gain to LoRA’s cone (Prop II.8), and HRA meets that cone with a rotation orbit (Thm II.7), each under its stated hypotheses. Adapters and prefixes change the network itself, and their germs live among functions (Exposé VI).

Four invariants of the germ carry most of the story, and isomorphic methods share them (Obs I.6).

  • The first-order image \(S_1(M)=d\rho_{q_0}(T_{q_0}Q)\), the directions a first step can take.
  • The type of the base point: regular when the first-order deficit \(d(M)-\dim S_1(M)\) vanishes, an apex when it is positive (Def I.5).
  • The maximum rank of an update, bounded by a minimum cut (Thm II.10).
  • The expressive dimension \(d(M)=\dim\mathrm{Im}\,M\), which falls short of the parameter count \(\lvert M\rvert\) by the gauge waste.

II.A asks where a method starts, II.B what a group-type method can never change (Thm II.5, Cor II.6), and II.C how rank differs from dimension (Prop II.12). Expressivity here means reach, and finding a reachable point is left to Exposé III.

CreditWhich functions a LoRA-adapted network can represent is studied by Zeng & Lee, 2023. The intrinsic dimension of Li et al., 2018 and Aghajanyan et al., 2020 depends on the data, while \(d(M)\) is a property of the method alone.

II.A Initialisations: pointings and strata

Every LoRA recipe decides where training starts. LoRA sets \(B_0=0\) and draws \(A_0\) at random. PiSSA takes both factors from the top of the SVD of \(W_0\) and subtracts their product from the frozen weight, LoRA-GA does the same with the first gradient, and CorDA, OLoRA, MiLoRA and EVA choose otherwise. Each recipe is a pointing, a parameter \(q_0\) with the frozen residual that makes \(\rho(q_0)=\theta_0\), and two pointings of one formula can be different objects of \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\). Figure 2 draws the typical cases.

Translates of one shape

An additive method adds updates from a fixed shape \(C\), for LoRA the cone \(\mathcal M_{\le r}\) of matrices of rank at most \(r\). Pointing it at \(q\) slides the shape so that \(\delta(q)\) lands on \(\theta_0\), and reachable updates become differences of two points of \(C\).

Theorem II.1 (pointings of an additive method). Let \(M=(Q,q_0,\theta_0+\delta)\) be additive with shape \(C=\delta(Q)\). Write \(-v+C\) for a translate, and \(C-C:=\{c-c':c,c'\in C\}\) for the Minkowski difference.

(a) For each \(q\in Q\), the re-pointed method \(M_q:=(Q,q,\theta_0-\delta(q)+\delta(\cdot))\), with frozen residual \(\theta_0-\delta(q)\), is pointed at \(\theta_0\), and \(\mathrm{Im}\,M_q=\theta_0+(-\delta(q)+C)\).

(b) \(\bigcup_q\mathrm{Im}\,M_q=\theta_0+(C-C)\).

(c) For LoRA\(_r\) on a single linear box with \(s\ne0\), \(C-C=\mathcal M_{\le\min(2r,m,n)}\); for several boxes, \(C-C\) is the product of the per-box varieties \(\mathcal M_{\le\min(2r,m_i,n_i)}\). Hence no exact pointing of LoRA\(_r\) (frozen residual \(\theta_0-\delta(q)\)), whether zero-init or split (PiSSA, OLoRA, CorDA, LoRA-GA), makes an update \(\theta-\theta_0\) of rank \(\gt 2r\) reachable in any adapted matrix. More robustly, from any starting point \(q_0\) of LoRA\(_r\), pointed or not, \(\rho(q)-\rho(q_0)=\delta(q)-\delta(q_0)\) has rank \(\le\min(2r,m,n)\). Quantised-residual schemes (QLoRA, LoftQ, QPiSSA) are pointed only up to a defect that depends on the order of operations (Prop V.2). Measured from \(\theta_0\), their updates contain it, and its rank is generically at least \(\min(m,n)-r\). The bound \(2r\) is attained by split pointings when \(2r\le\min(m,n)\).

CreditElementary, and known in practice. Meng et al., 2024 convert a trained PiSSA adapter into a rank-\(2r\) LoRA through \(BA-B_0A_0=[B\;\;B_0]\begin{bmatrix}A\\ -A_0\end{bmatrix}\), and HF PEFT ships the conversion. The theorem adds that it loses nothing and that rank \(2r\) is needed in general.
(expand)

(a) At \(q\) the re-pointed map gives \(\theta_0-\delta(q)+\delta(q)=\theta_0\), and its image is \(\theta_0-\delta(q)+C\). (b) Take the union over \(q\) of \(\theta_0+(C-\delta(q))\).

(c) For \(s\ne0\) the shape is \(C=s\,\mathcal M_{\le r}=\mathcal M_{\le r}\). The inclusion \(C-C\subseteq\mathcal M_{\le\min(2r,m,n)}\) is \(\operatorname{rank}(X-Y)\le\operatorname{rank}X+\operatorname{rank}Y\le 2r\). Conversely, \(C\) is a cone and so closed under negation. Split the SVD of a matrix \(Z\) of rank at most \(\min(2r,m,n)\) as \(Z=Z_1+Z_2\) with \(\operatorname{rank}Z_i\le r\), and write \(Z=Z_1-(-Z_2)\). With several boxes \(\delta\) acts box by box, so \(C\) and \(C-C\) are products. The robust form is the same inclusion applied to \(\delta(q)-\delta(q_0)\). For the defects, QLoRA’s \(e\) and QPiSSA’s \(\kappa(W_{\rm res})-W_{\rm res}\) are quantisation errors, generically of full rank, and one-step LoftQ’s \(e-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\) has rank at least \(\operatorname{rank}e-r\). For attainment, a split pointing with \(P=\delta(q_0)\) of rank \(r\) has image \((\theta_0-P)+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), so it reaches \(\theta_0+(Y-P)\) for every \(Y\) of rank \(r\), and \(Y-P\) has rank \(2r\) for generic \(Y\) when \(2r\le\min(m,n)\).∎

An initialisation chooses which translate of the shape passes through \(\theta_0\). Split initialisations gain updates \(Y-P\) of rank up to \(2r\) and lose generic updates of every rank \(\ge1\) (Thm II.2(d)). Moving a weight matrix by more than rank \(2r\) needs rebasing, as in ReLoRA (Thm V.3), or another shape.

Three strata

Isomorphisms of methods are pointed diffeomorphisms over \(\Theta\) (Def I.2), such as the gauge \((B,A)\mapsto(Bg^{-1},gA)\), \(g\in GL_r\). They preserve the image and \(S_1\) (Obs I.6) but may change training (Exposé III). Up to isomorphism, a full-rank LoRA initialisation is determined by one subspace or one matrix.

Theorem II.2 (three strata of LoRA initialisations). Let \(\delta(B,A)=BA\) with \(1\le r\lt\min(m,n)\). Consider pointed LoRA objects in three distinguished strata of full-rank pointings:
  • \((\mathrm T_A)\): \(B_0=0\) and \(\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\);
  • \((\mathrm T_B)\): \(A_0=0\) and \(\operatorname{rank}B_0=r\);
  • \((\mathrm S)\): \(\operatorname{rank}B_0A_0=r\), with frozen residual \(\theta_0-B_0A_0\).

(a) Classification. Within a stratum, \(M_{q_0}\cong M_{q_0'}\) iff, respectively, \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0=\mathrm{row}\,A_0'\); \(\mathrm{col}\,B_0=\mathrm{col}\,B_0'\); or \(B_0A_0=B_0'A_0'\). Objects in different strata are never isomorphic. As a set, the isomorphism classes of the three strata therefore form

\[\mathrm{Gr}(r,\mathbb R^n)\ \sqcup\ \mathrm{Gr}(r,\mathbb R^m)\ \sqcup\ \mathcal M_r(m\times n).\]

(b) First order. \(\dim S_1\) is \(mr\), \(nr\) and \(r(m+n-r)\) respectively, while \(d=r(m+n-r)\) in every case. The deficits are \(r(n-r)\), \(r(m-r)\) and \(0\).

(c) Geometry. In \(\mathrm T_A\) and \(\mathrm T_B\), \(\mathrm{Im}=\theta_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), whose vertex \(\theta_0\) is an apex. In \(\mathrm S\), \(\mathrm{Im}=(\theta_0-P)+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) with \(P=B_0A_0\), and \(\theta_0\) is a smooth point.

(d) Incomparability. The images of \(\mathrm S\)-type and \(\mathrm T\)-type objects are incomparable.

(e) Scope. The three strata do not exhaust the pointings of LoRA\(_r\). Outside them lie: \(B_0=0\) with \(\operatorname{rank}A_0\lt r\); \(A_0=0\) with \(\operatorname{rank}B_0\lt r\); \(B_0=A_0=0\); \(0\lt\operatorname{rank}B_0A_0\lt r\); and pointings with \(B_0,A_0\) both nonzero and \(B_0A_0=0\). HF PEFT ships one of the last kind: for even \(r\) and in exact arithmetic, init_lora_weights="orthogonal" has \((\operatorname{rk}B_0,\operatorname{rk}A_0,\operatorname{rk}B_0A_0)=(r/2,r/2,0)\). It is an apex with \(\dim S_1=\tfrac r2(m+n-\tfrac r2)\), isomorphic to no object of the three strata. In bf16 the two factors are rounded separately, so \(B_0A_0\) is small but nonzero and this init becomes a generic pointing with a tiny defect. For every pointing, the image determines \(P=B_0A_0\) (its vertex is \(\theta_0-P\)), and \(S_1=\{XA_0+B_0Y\}\) determines \((\mathrm{row}\,A_0,\mathrm{col}\,B_0)\) whenever these are proper subspaces.

(expand)
  • “If” in (a). Two full-row-rank matrices with the same row space satisfy \(A_0'=gA_0\) for a unique \(g\in GL_r\), and \((B,A)\mapsto(Bg^{-1},gA)\) is a pointed isomorphism over \(\Theta\). \(\mathrm T_B\) is symmetric. In \(\mathrm S\), the full-rank factorisations of \(P\) form one \(GL_r\)-orbit, and the residuals agree.
  • “Only if” in (a). In \(\mathrm T_A\), \(d\rho_{(0,A_0)}(\dot B,\dot A)=\dot BA_0\), so \(S_1=\{XA_0\}\) is the set of matrices whose row space lies in \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\). It determines \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\), and it is an isomorphism invariant by Obs I.6. \(\mathrm T_B\) is symmetric. In \(\mathrm S\), the image \((\theta_0-P)+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) is an isomorphism invariant, and a translate of \(\mathcal M_{\le r}\) determines its vertex. Suppose \(c+\mathcal M_{\le r}=c'+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) with \(D=c-c'\ne0\), so that \(D+\mathcal M_{\le r}=\mathcal M_{\le r}\), and let \(k=\operatorname{rank}D\). If \(k\gt r\), take \(X=0\). Otherwise take \(X\) of rank \(r-k+1\) whose column and row spaces meet those of \(D\) only in \(0\), which is possible since \(r+1\le\min(m,n)\). Then \(\operatorname{rank}(D+X)=r+1\) in both cases, a contradiction. Hence the image determines \(P\), for every pointing.
  • Separating the strata. \(S_1(\mathrm T_A)=\{Z:\mathrm{row}\,Z\subseteq V\}\) contains \(e_iv^\top\) for every \(i\) and every \(v\in V\), so it can equal \(S_1(\mathrm T_B)=\{Z:\mathrm{col}\,Z\subseteq U\}\) only if \(U=\mathbb R^m\), which is impossible since \(r\lt m\). When \(m\ne n\) the dimensions \(mr\ne nr\) already differ. \(\mathrm S\) differs from both because \(\dim S_1=r(m+n-r)\) differs from \(mr\) (as \(n\gt r\)) and from \(nr\) (as \(m\gt r\)).
  • (b). At a full-rank pair, \(S_1=\{XA_0+B_0Y\}=T_P\mathcal M_r\). In general \(\dim\{XA_0+B_0Y\}=m\operatorname{rk}A_0+n\operatorname{rk}B_0-\operatorname{rk}A_0\operatorname{rk}B_0\) (Prop II.3).
  • (c). Classically, \(\mathcal M_r\) is open in \(\mathcal M_{\le r}\) and is a manifold of dimension \(r(m+n-r)\), and \(\mathrm{Sing}\,\mathcal M_{\le r}=\mathcal M_{\le r-1}\). So \(P\), of rank \(r\), is a smooth point, and \(\theta_0\) is a smooth point of the \(\mathrm S\)-image. In the \(\mathrm T\) strata, \(\theta_0\) is the vertex of the non-linear cone \(\theta_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), a singular point (Def I.5), and by (b) its deficit is positive, so it is an apex.
  • (d). A generic rank-one \(X\) satisfies \(\operatorname{rank}(P+X)=\operatorname{rank}(X-P)=r+1\). So \(\theta_0+X\) lies in the \(\mathrm T\)-image but not in the \(\mathrm S\)-image, and \(\theta_0-P+X\) lies in the \(\mathrm S\)-image but not in the \(\mathrm T\)-image.
  • (e). The dimension is Prop II.3. For any pointing, \(S_1=\{XA_0+B_0Y\}\) is the space of matrices that send \((\mathrm{row}\,A_0)^\perp\) into \(\mathrm{col}\,B_0\). Its Frobenius orthogonal complement is \((\mathrm{col}\,B_0)^\perp\otimes(\mathrm{row}\,A_0)^\perp\), whose column and row spans recover \((\mathrm{col}\,B_0)^\perp\) and \((\mathrm{row}\,A_0)^\perp\) when both subspaces are proper. HF’s orthogonal init draws one orthogonal \(Q\in O(r)\) and sets \(A_0=(G_AQ')^\top/10\) and \(B_0=G_BQ''/10\) with Gaussian \(G_A,G_B\), where \(Q'\) and \(Q''\) are the odd- and even-numbered rows of \(Q\) (HF’s q_odd and q_even). Distinct rows of \(Q\) are orthogonal, so \(B_0A_0=0\), with both factors of rank \(r/2\). Its image is \(\theta_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), with vertex \(\theta_0\), so it is not in \(\mathrm S\). Its pair \((\mathrm{col}\,B_0,\mathrm{row}\,A_0)\) has dimensions \((r/2,r/2)\), against \((0,r)\) for \(\mathrm T_A\) and \((r,0)\) for \(\mathrm T_B\), so its \(S_1\) differs from theirs. Its deficit is \(\tfrac r2(m+n-\tfrac{3r}2)\gt0\). In low precision the separately rounded factors acquire small extra singular values, so the placement holds only up to a small perturbation.

Numerical check: \(\dim S_1=(14,10,20)\) for \((\mathrm T_A,\mathrm T_B,\mathrm S)\) at \((m,n,r)=(7,5,2)\), and \(11\) for HF’s orthogonal init.∎

Analogy (a moduli space of initialisations). Calling the three spaces a moduli space borrows a word. The bijection is of sets only. \(\mathrm T_A\) lies in the closure of \(\mathrm S\), since \((\varepsilon B',A_0)\to(0,A_0)\), so the quotient is not a topological disjoint union, and no universal family is built.

Most published schemes are points of the three strata. LoRA-FA and MiCA freeze one factor and are sub-objects, and the last three rows lie outside the strata.

schemestratumpoint
vanilla LoRA (linear layers)\(\mathrm T_A\)a random point of \(\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\), Haar under Gaussian \(A_0\), roughly Haar under Kaiming-uniform
EVA\(\mathrm T_A\)the top-\(r\) principal subspace of the input activations, at its per-layer rank
LoRA-FAsub-object of \(\mathrm T_A\)the same point, with \(A\) frozen
“Init[B]” (Hayou et al., 2024); vanilla LoRA on embeddings\(\mathrm T_B\)a random point of \(\mathrm{Gr}(r,m)\) (on embeddings, loralib and HF set \(A_0=0\), \(B_0\) Gaussian)
MiCAsub-object of \(\mathrm T_B\)the minor left-singular frame of \(W_0\), with \(B\) frozen
PiSSA / MiLoRA\(\mathrm S\)\(P\) = the top / bottom rank-\(r\) SVD part of \(W_0\) (HF absorbs the scale: \(P=sB_0A_0\))
OLoRA\(\mathrm S\)\(P=sQ_rR_r\), from a truncated QR factorisation of \(W_0\)
CorDA\(\mathrm S\)\(P\) from the SVD of \(W_0C\), with \(C\) the activation covariance
LoRA-GA\(\mathrm S\)\(P\) from the SVD of the first gradient
Astra\(\mathrm S\)\(P=QQ^\top W_0\) (times \(s\) in HF), with \(Q\) output-activation eigenvectors (the centred tail in the paper, the uncentred top \(r\) in HF and the official code), when \(\operatorname{rank}Q^\top W_0=r\)
