Cut \(W_0\) out of a linear layer and a hole \(V\to U\) remains. A weight-space method fills it with a small network of frozen and trainable boxes, a plug. Every invariant above is read off the plug: the budget (Def I.2), the min-cut, the Jacobian rank (Def I.5), the gauges on its wires, and the merge type, set by whether an activation sits on the path (Thm VI.3).

CreditThe plug is a weight-space cousin of the unified view of He et al., 2022, which puts adapters, prefix tuning and LoRA in one design space.

Here is one \(4096\times4096\) box and one budget, spent three ways (Proposition II.12).

method at \(\lvert M\rvert=131{,}072\)\(d(M)\)fibre \(\lvert M\rvert-d(M)\)max rankblock structure
LoRA\(_{16}\)130,81625616none
GraLoRA, \(k=2\)130,81625632each of 4 blocks has rank \(\le8\)
LoHa\(_{8,8}\)≤ 122,753≥ 8,31964none

The methods differ by at least 8,063 dimensions and by a factor of four in rank. The LoHa row uses the upper bound \(F\), whose equality is checked numerically at small sizes and open in general.

Pred Benchmarks should report \(d(M)\) beside \(\lvert M\rvert\), with the maximum rank and the cut it refers to. At budgets of a few thousand parameters, rankings at equal effective dimension can change.

At rest, Figure 6 shows LoHa\(_2\) on an \(8\times8\) box, with 64 entries, rank at most 4, Jacobian rank 41 and a gauge orbit of dimension \(23=4+4+15\) that fills the fibre. LoRA\(_4\) costs the same and reaches 7 more dimensions, with LoHa’s image strictly inside it.

Covariance and descent

A method is usually one recipe applied to every pretrained weight. Rename a layer’s input basis by \(h\) and its output basis by \(g\), so that \(W_0\) becomes \(gW_0h^{-1}\); the method is covariant under \((g,h)\) if its image moves the same way.

Definition II.14 (covariance group). For a method defined for every \(W_0\) (a natural family), the covariance group \(\mathcal G_M\subseteq GL_m\times GL_n\) is the largest subgroup with \(\mathrm{Im}\,M(gW_0h^{-1})=g\,\mathrm{Im}\,M(W_0)\,h^{-1}\). When the method depends on random frames or on data, the identity is required in distribution, with the data transformed. \(\mathcal G_M\) is the geometry the method lives in, in Klein’s sense.
More: in what sense this is Klein’s

Klein defined a geometry by its group of motions and studied what the group leaves unchanged. For group-type methods this reading is a theorem (Exposé II.B). For cones such as LoRA’s image it is a naming device, and the content is the shape of the image and its covariance group.

LoRA’s image \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) is covariant under all of \(GL_m\times GL_n\), since rank ignores bases, while a coordinate mask has a much smaller group. This matters because networks have symmetries of their own, such as permutations of hidden neurons, rotations of the residual stream and the query–key gauge of attention, which change the weights and keep the function.

Observation II.15 (descent through function-preserving reparametrisations).

Let \(M=\prod_bM_b\) be a box-wise method: each \(M_b\) is a member of a natural family with covariance group \(\mathcal G_{M_b}\), and no trainable parameters or random frames are shared across boxes. Let \(G_{\rm arch}\) act on \(\Theta=\prod_b\Theta_b\times\Theta_{\rm frozen}\), preserving this split and acting on each adapted box by \(W_b\mapsto g_bW_bh_b^{-1}\) with \((g_b,h_b)\in\mathcal G_{M_b}\). Assume \(f(g\theta,-)=f(\theta,-)\) on a \(G_{\rm arch}\)-stable set containing \(\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)\). Then \(\mathrm{Im}\,M(g\theta_0)=g\cdot\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)\), and the set of functions reachable by \(M\) from \(g\theta_0\) equals the set reachable from \(\theta_0\). With random frames drawn independently per box, this holds in distribution. For data-dependent methods it holds when each box’s covariance holds pathwise in the data and box \(b\) sees, at \(g\theta_0\), the transform of its data at \(\theta_0\), as for the gauges below. Training trajectories and outcomes can still differ.

Examples of \(G_{\rm arch}\): hidden-neuron permutations; the per-head query–key gauge \((W_Q,W_K)\mapsto(gW_Q,g^{-\top}W_K)\), with \(g\in GL_{d_k}\) without RoPE and \(g\) restricted further under RoPE and QK-norm (exact groups in the proof); and one global orthogonal rotation of the residual stream of a pre-norm transformer whose norm gains all fuse into the linear layers they feed, with LayerNorm converted to RMSNorm (SliceGPT’s computational invariance). Per-block rotations, sandwich norms and post-norms (Gemma-2/3, OLMo-2) are excluded.

(expand)

Apply per-box covariance factor by factor; the frozen coordinates are single points, so \(\mathrm{Im}\,M(g\theta_0)=g\,\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)\). Then

\[\{\Phi_f(\theta):\theta\in\mathrm{Im}\,M(g\theta_0)\}=\{\Phi_f(g\theta):\theta\in\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)\}=\{\Phi_f(\theta):\theta\in\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_0)\}.\]

With independent per-box frames, the joint law of the image is the product of the per-box laws, and each factor moves covariantly in law. Shared frames are excluded because covariance in law box by box does not control the joint law. SliceGPT’s per-block rotations need extra residual matrices, which change the architecture.

Attention gauges. With no positional encoding or an absolute one, any \(g\in GL_{d_k}\) preserves the scores. Under RoPE, \(g\) must commute with every RoPE rotation, which leaves the per-frequency rotation–scalings \((\mathbb C^\times)^{d_k/2}\), shared across a GQA group, or \((\mathbb C^\times)^{r/2}\times GL_{d_k-r}\) when RoPE covers \(r\) of the dimensions. Under QK-norm, \(g\) must in addition be orthogonal up to a positive scale (exactly orthogonal when the norm has \(\varepsilon\gt0\)) and compatible with the norm gains.

Numerical check: a tied rank-one update shared by two boxes violates the conclusion (relative distance up to \(0.30\)), while independent per-box LoRA satisfies it. Under RoPE a generic \(g\in GL_8\) changes attention scores by up to \(313\), a RoPE-commuting \(g\) by \(5\times10^{-14}\).

∎
CreditResidual-stream rotations are the device of SliceGPT (Ashkboos et al., 2024). QuaRot’s offline per-head Hadamard on \(W_v\) and \(W_{\rm out}\) is a value–output head gauge in \(G_{\rm arch}\); its online Hadamards insert new operations and lie outside it.

For one-sided methods ((IA)\(^3\), DoRA, OFT, BOFT), descent depends on the side: a residual rotation acts on the input side of \(q\), \(k\), \(v\), gate and up and on the output side of \(o\) and down. LoHa with \(r\ge2\), HiRA and magnitude-chosen masks pass hidden-unit permutations and fail rotations. The table compares gain-fused models with and without the rotation, since fusing gains is a diagonal rescaling. Failing a sufficient condition does not prove that reachable sets differ.

gaugesatisfy the hypothesis (reachable functions unchanged)fail the sufficient condition
SliceGPT-style residual rotationLoRA, full FT, BitFit, PiSSA; (IA)\(^3\) with HF defaults (output side of \(k\) or \(q\) and \(v\), input side of down); DoRA on \(q\), \(k\), \(v\), gate and up; OFT on \(o\) and downblock OFT and BOFT on residual-input projections; BOFT on \(o\) and down (its output-side scale); DoRA on \(o\) and down; LoHa (\(r\ge2\)); HiRA; coordinate masks; FourierFT
query–key and value–output head gaugesLoRA(IA)\(^3\) on \(k\) (or \(q\)) and \(v\); OFT on \(o\)

Conj A Against a gain-fused, unrotated control, a SliceGPT-style rotation measurably changes the fine-tuning results of block OFT/BOFT on residual-input projections, LoHa, HiRA and coordinate masks, because LLM residual streams have a privileged basis. Adam is only \(B_r\)-equivariant (Thm III.12) and HF’s Kaiming-uniform \(A\) is not rotation-invariant in law, so LoRA as trained need not descend even though its image does.

SVD-based initialisations raise the same question. PiSSA, MiLoRA and CorDA split \(W_0\) into a trainable part and a frozen residual by a truncated SVD of \(W_0\), or, for CorDA, of \(W_0\) weighted by the input second moment. Truncated SVD commutes only with rotations and uniform scalings, so these splits depend on the input coordinates, and whitening removes that dependence.

Proposition II.16 (covariant data-aware splits).

Let \(C=\mathbb E[xx^\top]\succ0\) be the input second moment, so that under \(x\mapsto hx\) we have \(W\mapsto Wh^{-1}\) and \(C\mapsto hCh^\top\). Let \(LL^\top=C\) and \(p=\min(m,n)\). Write \([M]_r\) for the rank-\(r\) truncated SVD, \(\langle M\rangle_r:=M-[M]_{p-r}\) for the bottom \(r\) singular components, and \(CO(n)=\mathbb R_{\gt0}\cdot O(n)\). Assume \(1\le r\lt p\) and the relevant singular gaps (\(\sigma_r\gt\sigma_{r+1}\) for a top-\(r\) split, \(\sigma_{p-r}\gt\sigma_{p-r+1}\) for a bottom-\(r\) split, of \(W_0L\), \(W_0C\) or \(W_0\) as appropriate); covariance groups are taken over the generic set where these gaps hold.

(a) The whitened split \(X^*=\arg\min_{\operatorname{rank}X\le r}\|(W_0-X)L\|_F=[W_0L]_rL^{-1}=\Pi_rW_0\), where \(\Pi_r\) is the top-\(r\) eigenprojector of the output second moment \(W_0CW_0^\top\), is \(GL_n\)-covariant on the input side and \(CO(m)\)-covariant on the output side.

(b) The paper-form CorDA splits (IPM: \(X=[W_0C]_rC^{-1}\), the minimiser of \(\|(W_0-X)C\|_F\); KPM: \(X=\langle W_0C\rangle_rC^{-1}\)) and the PiSSA and MiLoRA splits (\([W_0]_r\) and \(\langle W_0\rangle_r\)) have input-side covariance group exactly \(CO(n)\).

(expand)

Definition II.14 concerns images. For \(r\lt p\) the translation stabiliser of \(\mathcal M_{\le r}\) is \(\{0\}\), so covariance of the image \(W_0-X+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) is equivalent to covariance of the split \(X\).

(a) With \(L'=hL\), \([W_0h^{-1}hL]_r(hL)^{-1}=[W_0L]_rL^{-1}h^{-1}\), and the result does not depend on the square root chosen, since \([W_0LO]_rO^\top=[W_0L]_r\) for \(O\in O(n)\). Also \([W_0L]_r=\Pi_rW_0L\), because the top left singular vectors of \(W_0L\) are the top eigenvectors of \(W_0LL^\top W_0^\top\). On the output side, for \(g\in CO(m)\) the eigenprojector of \(gW_0CW_0^\top g^\top\) is \(g\Pi_rg^{-1}\).

(b) Lemma. For \(1\le r\lt p\), \([Mh]_r=[M]_rh\) for all \(M\) with a gap at \(r\) iff \(h\in CO(n)\). “If”: \(M\,cQ\) has the singular vectors of \(M\) rotated by \(Q\) and the singular values scaled by \(c\). “Only if”: take \(M=\sum_{i\le r+1}\sigma_iu_iv_i^\top\) with distinct \(\sigma\)’s. The top-\(r\) left singular space of \(Mh\) is \(\mathrm{span}\{u_1,\dots,u_r\}\) only if the coupling \(\sigma_r\sigma_{r+1}v_r^\top hh^\top v_{r+1}\) vanishes; requiring this for every orthonormal pair forces \(hh^\top\in\mathbb R_{\gt0}I\).

CorDA IPM’s covariance requires \([W_0Ch^\top]_rh^{-\top}C^{-1}h^{-1}=[W_0C]_rC^{-1}h^{-1}\), which is the lemma for \(h^\top\) applied to \(M=W_0C\), an arbitrary matrix. PiSSA is the lemma for \(h^{-1}\). For the bottom-\(r\) splits (KPM, MiLoRA), \(\langle M\rangle_r=M-[M]_{p-r}\), so the lemma applies at index \(p-r\).

Numerical check: under \(h=3Q\) with \(Q\) orthogonal, CorDA and PiSSA move covariantly to \(2\times10^{-15}\). Under a generic \(h\) only the whitened split does (\(2\times10^{-15}\), against \(0.63\) and \(1.1\)), and an absolute damping \(0.1\) breaks it (\(0.14\)).

∎
CreditThe splits are CorDA (Yang et al., 2024), PiSSA (Meng et al., 2024) and MiLoRA (Wang et al., 2024). The whitened truncation is the one used by SVD-LLM for compression.

CorDA damps \(C\) by a multiple of \((\operatorname{tr}C)\,I\), which keeps the \(CO(n)\) covariance; an absolute damping \(C+\lambda I\) would cut it to \(O(n)\). The released CorDA code also divides each calibration sample by the absolute value of its largest entry, which keeps only positive scalings and coordinate permutations and so breaks even rotation covariance; (b) covers the published formulas only.

A data-aware split ignores the choice of input coordinates if it has the form \(X=F(W_0CW_0^\top)\,W_0\), as the whitened (SVD-LLM-style) split does; Astra’s paper form, a tail eigenprojector of the centred output covariance, has the same form with \(C\) replaced by the centred input covariance, which transforms in the same way. Diagonal rescalings such as norm-gain folding or SmoothQuant change the CorDA and PiSSA splits. Covariance on both sides would need an output-side statistic independent of \(W_0\) and \(C\), such as a gradient second moment, and training dynamics stay non-covariant.

More: EVA

EVA (Paischer et al., 2024) does not split \(W_0\). It starts at \(B_0=0\) and points \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\) at the top-\(r\) centred principal subspace of the inputs, which is \(CO(n)\)-covariant. At a fixed rank per layer its image \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) is \(GL_m\times GL_n\)-covariant under Definition II.14. With HF’s default rho=2, ranks are redistributed by explained-variance ratios, which are invariant only under \(CO(n)\), so the image is \(GL_m\times CO(n)\)-covariant.

Conj C Whitened CorDA or PiSSA is more robust to function-preserving input reparametrisations, and no worse otherwise.

Rank is a cut, dimension a volume and covariance a group. All three can be computed before training. The next exposé turns from what a method can reach to how it moves there.

Exposé III

Fibres and metrics

The image of a method fixes what it can reach. Its fibres and the optimizer’s metric fix how it gets there, so isomorphic methods can train differently and different-looking methods can train identically.

Before asking how a method learns, ask what it cannot help keeping.

after Grothendieck
In one minute
  • Training with rates \(\Lambda\) moves the weights by \(\dot\theta=-K\nabla_\theta L\), with the cometric \(K=D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\), which depends on where the parameters sit in their fibre.
  • A 2-torus of rescalings acts on LoRA’s knobs \((\alpha,\eta_A,\eta_B,\sigma_A)\) without changing training. Under Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\), no decay or clipping and one shared schedule, LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) from \(B_0=0\) is plain LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\) (after Schulman et al., 2025).
  • Gradient flow conserves LoRA’s charge \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\). It sorts initialisations, and when it is isotropic one scale \(\sigma^*\) separates a LoRA-FA-like regime from balanced dynamics.
  • On full-rank factors, SGD respects \(O(r)\) of the gauge, Adam only the signed permutations. So from a zero-\(B\) start the image depends only on \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\), SGD’s trajectory on \(A_0^\top A_0\), and Adam’s on \(A_0\) up to signed row permutation.

One experiment first

Training happens upstairs, in the parameter space \(Q\), where each fibre of \(\rho\) collects the parameters that give one weight. The optimizer’s metric on \(Q\), pushed down to weight space, is what the model feels. Every statement below names its optimizer.

Figure 7 shows the cleanest consequence. Train with Adam at \(\varepsilon=0\) from \(B_0=0\), with no weight decay or clipping, one schedule for both groups, and the same data order and dropout masks. Then LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) (\(\eta_B=\lambda\eta_A\)) and plain LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\), at the same \(\eta_A\) and \(A_0\), produce the same weights at every step (Thm III.10, Cor III.11).

CreditThis LoRA/Adam statement is due to Schulman et al., 2025, for the LoRA+ of Hayou et al., 2024; Thm III.10 says what is added here.

The metric a method carries

A learning rate is a metric in disguise. Gradient descent with rates \(\Lambda\) moves \(q\) by \(-\Lambda\nabla_q\), and pushed down by \(D\rho\) this is the weight gradient multiplied by a positive semidefinite operator.

Definition III.1 (trained method; cometric; three equivalences). A trained method is a method together with optimizer data: a learning-rate operator \(\Lambda\succ0\) (per-block rates), an initial scale, and an update rule. Gradient flow \(\dot q=-\Lambda\nabla_q(L\circ\rho)\) moves the weights by \[\dot\theta=-K_q\nabla_\theta L,\qquad K_q=D\rho_q\,\Lambda\,D\rho_q^\top.\] We call \(K_q\) the induced cometric. For a backward method with compression \(c_t\) and anchor \(a_t\) (Def IV.1), \(K_t=a_tc_t\).

Two methods are expressively equivalent if their images agree, first-order equivalent if their \(K_{q_0}\) agree, and dynamically equivalent if they produce the same \(\theta\)-trajectory for every loss.

Cometrics compose the way derivatives do.

Observation III.2 (chain rule for cometrics). Let \(\sigma:Q'\to Q\) and \(\rho:Q\to X\) be smooth, with \(X=\Theta\) or a function or output space. Train \(Q'\) by gradient flow with a constant rate operator \(\Lambda'\succ0\), and put \(K^\sigma_{q'}:=D\sigma_{q'}\Lambda'D\sigma_{q'}^\top\). Then \[K^{\rho\sigma}_{q'}=D\rho_{\sigma(q')}\,K^\sigma_{q'}\,D\rho_{\sigma(q')}^\top,\] and no metric on \(Q\) is used. \(K\) is a degenerate cometric (PSD, of rank \(\le\dim Q'\)); when \(\sigma\) is not injective, \(K^\sigma\) is a field along \(\sigma\) and fails to be a cometric on \(Q\).

For LoRA, \(\rho(B,A)=W_0+sBA\) with \(s=\alpha/r\) (\(\alpha/\sqrt r\) for rsLoRA), block rates \(\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}(\eta_BI,\eta_AI)\) and no LoRA dropout, under gradient flow (equivalently, to first order in the step size for GD), \[K_{(B,A)}(G)=s^2\big(\eta_B\,GA^\top A+\eta_A\,BB^\top G\big).\] Under Adam there is no state-independent linear cometric.

(expand)

\(D(\rho\sigma)=D\rho\,D\sigma\), so \(K^{\rho\sigma}=D\rho\,D\sigma\,\Lambda'D\sigma^\top D\rho^\top\). For LoRA, use \(\nabla_B=sGA^\top\), \(\nabla_A=sB^\top G\) and \(\dot W=s(\dot BA+B\dot A)\). Discrete GD adds the term \(s\,\delta B\,\delta A\), of order \(\eta^2\).

Num Finite-difference Jacobians agree with the formula to \(6\times10^{-9}\).

∎

For LoRA, \(\eta_B\,GA^\top A\) lets the update reach all outputs while filtering its inputs through \(\mathrm{row}\,A\), and \(\eta_A\,BB^\top G\) does the reverse. At \(B_0=0\) only the first survives, so the first-step cometric \(s^2\eta_B\,GA_0^\top A_0\) is fixed by \(A_0\) (Cor III.13).

More: what prefix tuning’s MLP changes

Take \(X\) the prefix space (Exposé VI). HF PrefixTuning with prefix_projection=True (Li and Liang’s MLP \(\sigma\)) and False (P-tuning v2, minus its head) have the same prefix image when encoder_hidden_size \(\ge\) num_virtual_tokens \(-1\). Their cometrics are \(D\sigma\Lambda'D\sigma^\top\) and \(\Lambda\), so the MLP makes a flat metric point-dependent; that alone does not explain the instability Li and Liang saw under AdamW.

CreditPrefix tuning and its MLP are Li and Liang, 2021; the setting without it is P-tuning v2. Backpropagation as a lens, the source of the chain rule, is due to Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras and Cruttwell et al., 2021.

When a change of chart does not matter

A gauge transformation changes the parameters and keeps the weights. It leaves training unchanged exactly when the cometrics agree, which for LoRA’s gauge \(GL_r\) singles out \(O(r)\).

Theorem III.3 (gradient descent is natural exactly where cometrics agree; for LoRA’s linear gauge, exactly along \(O(r)\)). (a) Let \(\varphi:Q\to Q'\) be a diffeomorphism with \(\rho'\circ\varphi=\rho\), and let \(\Lambda,\Lambda'\) be the rate operators on \(Q,Q'\). Under gradient flow, the two methods induce the same \(\theta\)-velocity at \(q\) and at \(\varphi(q)\) for every loss iff \(K^\rho_q=K^{\rho'}_{\varphi(q)}\), that is, \[D\rho'_{\varphi(q)}\big(D\varphi_q\Lambda D\varphi_q^\top-\Lambda'\big)D\rho'^\top_{\varphi(q)}=0.\] This holds whenever \(D\varphi\Lambda D\varphi^\top=\Lambda'\), which is an isometry when \(\Lambda=\Lambda'=I\). That condition is necessary when \(D\rho'_{\varphi(q)}\) is injective, but not in general; every linear gauge of a linear parametrisation is natural, and so is every pointwise-orthogonal non-linear LoRA gauge \(\varphi(q)=\varphi_{g(q)}(q)\), \(g(q)\in O(r)\).

(b) For LoRA’s linear gauge \(\varphi_g(B,A)=(Bg^{-1},gA)\) with block-scalar rates, at points where \(A\) has full row rank (\(B\) arbitrary, including \(B=0\)) and \(r\lt n\), the criterion holds iff \(g\in O(r)\). The same holds symmetrically where \(B\) has full column rank and \(r\lt m\).

(expand)

(a) Put \(\ell=L\circ\rho'\), so that \(L\circ\rho=\ell\circ\varphi\). The \(\rho\)-flow is \(\dot q=-\Lambda D\varphi^\top\nabla\ell\), so \(\tfrac{d}{dt}\varphi(q)=-D\varphi\Lambda D\varphi^\top\nabla\ell\). Apply \(D\rho'\) and use \(\nabla\ell=D\rho'^\top\nabla_\theta L\). The \(\rho'\)-flow gives \(-D\rho'\Lambda'D\rho'^\top\nabla_\theta L\). Since \(\nabla_\theta L\) is arbitrary, the criterion follows. For the examples, a linear \(\rho\) has constant \(D\rho\), and \(D\rho\,P=D\rho\) for a linear gauge \(P\); for instance, for \(\rho(x,y)=\theta_0+x+y\) the shear \((x,y)\mapsto(2x,y-x)\) preserves \(\rho\) and \(K\) without being orthogonal. For a pointwise gauge, \(D\varphi_q=D\varphi_{g(q)}+V\) with \(V\) valued in fibre directions, which \(D\rho'\) kills, so the criterion reduces to that of \(\varphi_{g(q)}\), which (b) covers.

(b) The two cometrics agree for all \(G\) iff \[\eta_B\,GA^\top(g^\top g-I)A=\eta_A\,B(I-g^{-1}g^{-\top})B^\top G\quad\text{for every }G.\] An identity \(GX=YG\) for all \(G\) forces \(X=cI_n\) and \(Y=cI_m\) (test it on the matrix units \(E_{ij}\)). Here \(\operatorname{rank}X\le r\lt n\), so \(c=0\), and full row rank of \(A\) gives \(g^\top g=I\). Only the full row rank of \(A\) is used.

Num At a random point a non-orthogonal \(g\) gives a cometric mismatch of about \(18\), an orthogonal one about \(10^{-15}\).

∎

So gradient descent on LoRA is a family of dynamics indexed by \(O(r)\backslash GL_r\cong\mathrm{Sym}^+_r\), via \(g\mapsto g^\top g\). Under GD, LoRA+’s ratio \(\lambda\) is the gauge \(g=\lambda^{1/4}I\) plus an overall change of rate, while rsLoRA is a move on the hyperparameter torus (Thm III.10).

CreditThe \(GL_r\) symmetry of \(W=BA\) and the quotient geometry of fixed-rank matrices are classical (Mishra et al., 2012). Putterman et al., 2024 build learners on LoRA weights that respect it.

Some published optimizers use instead a cometric that depends only on the weight, at least in an idealised form.

Proposition III.4 (cometrics that descend to the rank-\(r\) manifold). Known in part All statements concern gradient flow, or (S)GD to first order, on the stated gradients, undamped and without Adam; cometrics include the learning rate. In (a), (b) and the first sentence of (d) we work on the full-rank locus and write \(U=\mathrm{col}\,B\), \(V=\mathrm{row}\,A\).

(a) Scaled GD, \(\delta B=-\eta\nabla_B(AA^\top)^{-1}\) and \(\delta A=-\eta(B^\top B)^{-1}\nabla_A\), is \(GL_r\)-equivariant with first-order cometric \(K(G)=\eta s^2(P_UG+GP_V)\).

(b) LoRA-Pro’s equivalent gradient \(\tilde g=P_UG+(I-P_U)GP_V\) is the Frobenius-orthogonal projection \(\Pi_{T_{W-W_0}\mathcal M_r}(G)\). The flow of its SGD variant (LoRA-Pro Alg. 1) is the Riemannian gradient flow on \(W_0+\mathcal M_r\).

(c) LoRA-FA’s corrected gradient \(s^{-2}\nabla_B(A_0A_0^\top)^{-1}\), fed to SGD with \(A_0\) frozen, gives \(K(G)=\eta\,GP_V\), \(V=\mathrm{row}A_0\), for any \(B\) (including \(B_0=0\)) once \(\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\). It depends on \(A_0\) only through \(V\in\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\). The original LoRA-FA, plain SGD on \(B\), has \(K(G)=s^2\eta_BGA_0^\top A_0\).

(d) The cometrics of (a) and (b) are functions of \(W\) and define fields tangent to \(W_0+\mathcal M_r\). The flow of (c) is the Frobenius gradient flow on the \(mr\)-dimensional slice \(W_0+\{XA_0\}\subset W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\). On its open dense rank-\(r\) part \(\{\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)=V\}\) the field is \(\eta\,GP_{\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)}\), a function of \(W\) tangent to \(\mathcal M_r\), so (c) descends as a degenerate cometric of rank \(mr\) (less than \(r(m+n-r)\) when \(r\lt n\)) whose flow keeps \(\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)\) fixed. The cometric of plain GD depends on the representative.

CreditScaled GD is Tong, Ma and Chi, 2021 and Zhang and Pilanci, 2024; LoRA-Pro is Wang et al., 2024; LoRA-FA is Zhang et al., 2023, with the correction in (c) from its 2026 revision and HF PEFT. The \(GL_r\)-invariant metric and its descent to \(\mathcal M_r\) are classical (Mishra et al., 2012; Mishra and Sepulchre, 2016).

Practice. As published and implemented, these methods use damped inverses, AdamW or a Sylvester-chosen free matrix, which keep only the symmetries of Thm III.12. At \(B_0=0\), off the full-rank locus, (a) and (b) need damping; Zhang and Pilanci report damped scaled GD and scaled AdamW, and HF’s create_riemannian_optimizer and LoRA-Pro’s code damp too. LoRA-Pro’s experiments use \(\dot W=-\Pi_T(\mathrm{Adam}(\Pi_TG))\), which is no gradient flow, and revised LoRA-FA and HF’s create_lorafa_optimizer apply AdamW after the correction, so the basis and scale of \(A_0\) matter too.

2026Riemannion (Bogachev et al., 2025) defines its optimizer on \(\mathcal M_r\) directly. Its variable is the point \(X\in\mathcal M_r\), stored as thin factors, and apart from weight decay each step uses only \(X\), the projected gradient and the momentum: the tangent projection of Prop III.4(b), momentum carried along by the same projection, a Muon step that sets the nonzero singular values of the momentum to 1 and projects again, and a truncated SVD as retraction. No Gram matrix is inverted, so nothing needs damping, and with weight decay off the \(W\)-trajectory, momentum included, does not depend on how \(X\) is factored. The published Algorithm 4 writes its weight decay through the orthonormal factors, as a term \(\gamma A_LB_R^\top\), so this holds for \(\gamma=0\). Its non-linearity acts on a weight-space matrix, while Adam’s acts coordinatewise on the factors, which is what cuts the gauge down to \(B_r\) (Thm III.12).
(expand)

(a) Substitute \(\nabla_B=sGA^\top\) and \(\nabla_A=sB^\top G\) into \(\delta W=s(\delta BA+B\delta A)+O(\eta^2)\), using \(A^\top(AA^\top)^{-1}A=P_V\) and \(B(B^\top B)^{-1}B^\top=P_U\). For equivariance: under \((Bg^{-1},gA)\) we have \(\nabla_{B'}=\nabla_Bg^\top\) and \((A'A'^\top)^{-1}=g^{-\top}(AA^\top)^{-1}g^{-1}\), so \(\delta B'=\delta B\,g^{-1}\); the same holds for \(A\).

(b) The tangent space is \[T_{W-W_0}\mathcal M_r=\{XA+BY\}=\{D:P_{U^\perp}DP_{V^\perp}=0\},\] and the orthogonal projection onto it is \[G-P_{U^\perp}GP_{V^\perp}=P_UG+GP_V-P_UGP_V=\tilde g.\] For any free matrix \(X\) in Alg. 1, \(s(h_BA+Bh_A)=P_UG+P_{U^\perp}GP_V\), so \(\delta W=-\eta\,\Pi_TG+O(\eta^2)\).

(c) \[\Delta W=s\,\Delta B\,A_0=-\eta\,GA_0^\top(A_0A_0^\top)^{-1}A_0=-\eta\,GP_V,\] exactly, since \(A_0\) is frozen.

(d) For (a) and (b), read off the formulas. For (c), \(XA_0\) has rank \(r\) iff \(X\) does, an open dense condition, and then \(\mathrm{row}(XA_0)=V\), so \(GP_V=GP_{\mathrm{row}(W-W_0)}\) lies in \(\{XA+BY\}\).

Num Gauge defects are about \(10^{-15}\) for (a), against \(52\) for plain GD. LoRA-FA with AdamW from \(A_0\) and from \(gA_0\) differs by \(O(1)\) after a few steps, against \(10^{-15}\) with SGD.

∎

Under (S)GD each method is summarised by its weight-space cometric. LoRA, scaled GD and LoRA-Pro share the image \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\) and differ only in cometric; LoRA-FA is the sub-object \(W_0+\{XA_0\}\); GaLore (with a gradient-SVD or a random projector) and MeZO are full-parameter methods, listed for comparison. Flora proper compresses only its momentum, takes full-space Adafactor steps and has no cometric of this form (Exposé IV).

Method (gradient flow or SGD)Cometric \(K(G)\)
LoRA\(s^2(\eta_BGA^\top A+\eta_ABB^\top G)\)
LoRA-FA (original / corrected)\(s^2\eta_BGA_0^\top A_0\) / \(\eta GP_V\)
scaled GD (undamped Riemannian preconditioning)\(\eta s^2(P_UG+GP_V)\)
LoRA-Pro (SGD variant)\(\eta\,\Pi_{T\mathcal M_r}G\)
GaLore (one period, scale \(\alpha\), Prop IV.2)\(\eta\alpha PP^\top G\), or \(\eta\alpha\,GQQ^\top\) when \(m\gt n\)
GaLore with a random projector \(A_t\), one period (the compressed SGD of Hao et al., 2024)\(\eta GA_t^\top A_t\)
MeZO\(\eta zz^\top G\), equal to \(\eta G\) in expectation

Isometries and charges

Split LoRA’s gauge in two. With per-block scalar rates, the antisymmetric generators rotate the rank channels and preserve the rate metric, so the optimizer cannot feel them. Along the symmetric ones, gradient flow conserves a quadratic charge; for LoRA the charges form one matrix \(\Phi\), whose vanishing is the balancedness of deep linear networks.

Theorem III.5 (Noether charges and isometries for pulled-back signals). Known Let a Lie group act linearly on \(Q=\mathbb R^N\) with \(\rho\circ k=\rho\), let \(\Lambda\succ0\) be constant, and let \(\gamma(t)\in T^*_{\rho(q(t))}\Theta\) be any cotangent signal, gradient or not. Consider the continuous-time updates \[\dot q=-\Lambda\,D\rho_q^\top\gamma(t).\]

(i) For every generator \(\xi\) with \(\Lambda^{-1}\xi\) symmetric, the charge \(J_\xi(q)=\tfrac12\langle q,\Lambda^{-1}\xi q\rangle\) is conserved.

(ii) For every generator \(\xi\) with \(\Lambda^{-1}\xi\) antisymmetric, \(k=e^{\tau\xi}\) preserves \(\langle\cdot,\Lambda^{-1}\cdot\rangle\), so \(K_{kq}=K_q\): for the same cotangent \(\gamma\), the \(\theta\)-velocities at \(q\) and at \(kq\) agree. Suppose moreover that \(\gamma\) depends on \(q\) only through \((t,\rho(q))\), as \(\nabla_\theta L\) or an open-loop signal does, is locally Lipschitz in \(\theta\), and that \(\rho\in C^{1,1}_{\rm loc}\). Then the \(\theta\)-trajectories from \(q_0\) and from \(kq_0\) coincide. A signal that uses Euclidean norms on \(Q\), as global-norm clipping does, keeps this when \(k\) is also a Euclidean isometry and can break it otherwise.

(iii) When \(\mathfrak g\) is closed under \(\xi\mapsto\Lambda\xi^\top\Lambda^{-1}\), every generator splits into parts of types (i) and (ii), a Cartan decomposition \(\mathfrak g=\mathfrak k\oplus\mathfrak p\); this holds for LoRA’s \(\mathfrak{gl}_r\) with per-block scalar rates. Without closure some generators are of neither type. With per-rank-channel rates, closure holds iff the products \(\eta_{B,j}\eta_{A,j}\) are all equal; when they are pairwise distinct, only diagonal \(X\) give charges and no nonzero \(X\) gives an isometry.

CreditPart (i) is Zhao et al., 2023, Prop. 5.1, for symmetric rate matrices. Laws valid for every loss and dataset are characterised by Marcotte, Gribonval and Peyré, 2023; the principle goes back to Du, Hu and Lee, 2018, Arora, Cohen and Hazan, 2018 and Kunin et al., 2021. A non-gradient \(\gamma\) adds little, as \(\gamma(t)\) is the gradient of \(\langle\gamma(t),\theta\rangle\). New here are the PEFT instances.
(expand)

Invariance of \(\rho\) gives \(D\rho_q(\xi q)=0\). Hence \[\tfrac{d}{dt}J_\xi=\langle\Lambda^{-1}\xi q,\dot q\rangle=-\langle\xi q,D\rho_q^\top\gamma\rangle=-\langle D\rho_q\xi q,\gamma\rangle=0.\]

For (ii), \(\Lambda^{-1}\xi\) antisymmetric gives \(\tfrac{d}{d\tau}\big(e^{\tau\xi^\top}\Lambda^{-1}e^{\tau\xi}\big)=0\), equivalently \(k\Lambda k^\top=\Lambda\). From \(\rho\circ k=\rho\) we get \(D\rho_q=D\rho_{kq}k\), so \[K_q=D\rho_{kq}k\Lambda k^\top D\rho_{kq}^\top=K_{kq},\qquad \tfrac{d}{dt}(kq)=-k\Lambda k^\top D\rho_{kq}^\top\gamma=-\Lambda D\rho_{kq}^\top\gamma.\] When \(\gamma\) factors through \((t,\rho)\), \(kq(t)\) solves the same ODE as the trajectory from \(kq_0\); under the stated regularity the solution is unique.

(iii) Write \[\xi=\tfrac12(\xi+\Lambda\xi^\top\Lambda^{-1})+\tfrac12(\xi-\Lambda\xi^\top\Lambda^{-1}).\] For per-channel rates \(\Lambda(B,A)=(BD_B,D_AA)\), \(\Lambda^{-1}\xi_X(B,A)=(BXD_B^{-1},-D_A^{-1}XA)\) is symmetric (antisymmetric) iff \(XD_B^{-1}\) and \(D_A^{-1}X\) both are. Entrywise, \(X_{ji}=\pm X_{ij}\eta_{B,i}/\eta_{B,j}\) and \(X_{ji}=\pm X_{ij}\eta_{A,j}/\eta_{A,i}\), which admit \(X_{ij}\ne0\) iff \(\eta_{B,i}\eta_{A,i}=\eta_{B,j}\eta_{A,j}\). So an off-diagonal pair gives a charge and an isometry exactly when its products agree, and closure holds iff all products agree.

Num With a non-gradient, \(q\)-dependent \(\gamma\) on LoRA, \(\Phi\) drifts by \(1.6\times10^{-12}\). With Euclidean clipping and a non-Euclidean isometry \(k\), the \(\theta\)-trajectories from \(q_0\) and \(kq_0\) differ by \(0.06\).

∎

Instances, under gradient flow or SGD without momentum, with per-block rates. AdamW and heavy-ball momentum fall outside the theorem, since their buffers hold \(D\rho\) at past points.

  • LoRA. \(\xi_X(B,A)=(BX,-XA)\) gives \(\Lambda^{-1}\xi_X\) symmetric iff \(X=X^\top\), so the \(r(r+1)/2\) charges are the entries of \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\). The \(\mathfrak{so}_r\) half of \(\mathfrak{gl}_r\) acts by Euclidean isometries, so (ii) survives global-norm clipping.
  • DyLoRA: only \(\mathrm{diag}\,\Phi\), as the gauge reduces to the torus. VeRA: \(\|b\|^2/\eta_b-\|d\|^2/\eta_d\), from \((b,d)\mapsto(cb,d/c)\); NOLA is analogous.
  • AdaLoRA (\(P\Lambda Q\)), between masking events, at equal rates and without its orthogonality regulariser (HF default orth_reg_weight=0.5, which breaks them): charges \(\|p_k\|^2-\lambda_k^2\) and \(\|q_k\|^2-\lambda_k^2\).
  • HRA: a conserved \(\|u_i\|^2\). ReLU bottleneck adapters: one charge per hidden unit. TT adapters: one conserved matrix per bond.
  • DoRA’s detached-norm backward and global-norm clipping keep the form \(\Lambda D\rho_{\rm LoRA}^\top\gamma\), so \(\Phi\) is conserved.

Conservation needs only the shape \(\dot q=-\Lambda D\rho^\top\gamma\), so it holds for any loss and data. With \(\eta\) the common scale of the rates, a GD step changes \(\eta\Phi\) by \(O(\eta^2)\) and an Adam step by \(O(\eta)\), as Panel A of Figure 8 shows.

Analogy (Noether; gauge). Noether names the passage from a symmetry to a conserved quantity, here proved for gradient flow without a Lagrangian. Gauge means the symmetry group of a parametrisation; no connection is involved.

A conserved charge is fixed at time zero, so it labels initialisations. Weight decay moves it on purpose, and its two conventions move it toward different targets.

Corollary III.6 (charge at initialisation; weight decay). (a) \(\Phi_0=B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B-A_0A_0^\top/\eta_A\) is the value of the conserved charge, fixed by the initialisation and the rates. \(O(r)\) acts on \(\Phi\) by conjugation and preserves \(\theta\)-trajectories, so only the spectrum of \(\Phi_0\) labels a trajectory.
Initialisation\(\Phi_0\)
HF default, Linear/Conv (\(B_0=0\); Kaiming-uniform \(A_0\), entries \(U(-n^{-1/2},n^{-1/2})\), \(n\) the fan-in)\(-A_0A_0^\top/\eta_A\), with \(\mathbb E[A_0A_0^\top]=\tfrac13I_r\) and fluctuations \(O(n^{-1/2})\)
HF default, embeddings (\(A_0=0\), normal \(B_0\))\(+B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B\) (Init[B]-type)
EVA (orthonormal rows; default whiten=False)\(-I/\eta_A\) exactly
PiSSA in HF PEFT (\(B_0^\top B_0=A_0A_0^\top=S_r/s\): top-\(r\) singular values over the LoRA scale); MiLoRA, not in HF PEFT, likewise with the bottom \(r\) when split the same way\((S_r/s)(1/\eta_B-1/\eta_A)\), zero iff \(\eta_A=\eta_B\) (when \(S_r\ne0\))
OLoRA (\(B_0=Q_r\), \(A_0=R_r\))\(I/\eta_B-R_rR_r^\top/\eta_A\)
“Init[B]” (\(A_0=0\))\(+B_0^\top B_0/\eta_B\)

(b) Let \(G\) be any cotangent signal, with \(s\) absorbed into it. Under gradient flow with decay per unit time on both factors (\(\dot B=-\eta_BGA^\top-\lambda B\), \(\dot A=-\eta_AB^\top G-\lambda A\)), \(\dot\Phi=-2\lambda\Phi\). With learning-rate-coupled decay (PyTorch SGD weight_decay; the decay term of PyTorch AdamW), \(\dot B=-\eta_B(GA^\top+\lambda B)\) and likewise for \(A\), so \(\dot\Phi=-2\lambda(B^\top B-AA^\top)\). This gives \(\Phi(t)=e^{-2\eta\lambda t}\Phi(0)\) when \(\eta_A=\eta_B=\eta\), and in general no law in \(\Phi\) alone when \(\eta_A\ne\eta_B\). Adam(W) lies outside Thm III.5; under it \(\eta\Phi\) drifts at first order in the step size even without decay, so neither law describes AdamW training.

(expand)

(a) Substitute the initialisations. Each Kaiming entry has variance \(1/(3n)\) and there are \(n\) entries per row, so the diagonal has mean \(1/3\) with fluctuations \(O(n^{-1/2})\), and the off-diagonal entries have standard deviation \(1/(3\sqrt n)\). HF’s pissa_init divides \(S_r\) by the scale before splitting. Under \(q\mapsto(BO,O^\top A)\) with \(O\in O(r)\), \(\Phi\mapsto O^\top\Phi O\).

(b) Differentiate: \[\tfrac{d}{dt}\tfrac{B^\top B}{\eta_B}=-(AG^\top B+B^\top GA^\top)-2c_B\tfrac{B^\top B}{\eta_B},\] with \(c_B=\lambda\) in the per-time convention and \(c_B=\eta_B\lambda\) in the coupled one. Do the same for \(A\) and subtract.

Num With coupled decay at \(\eta_B/\eta_A=4\), the closed form (taken with \(\eta=\eta_A\)) has relative error of order \(10\); in the valid cases the error is \(10^{-8}\) to \(10^{-4}\), the integrator’s. An Adam step moves \(\eta\Phi\) at first order in the learning rate, an SGD step at second order.

∎

Zero-product starts are maximally unbalanced (Prop III.8). HF’s PiSSA starts with \(\mu_0=B_0^\top B_0-A_0A_0^\top=0\) at every rate, but with \(\Phi_0=0\) only at equal rates.

Both decay conventions gauge-fix at equilibrium, each being a \(\Lambda\)-gradient flow of \(L\) plus a Kempf–Ness potential on the fibres. Per-time decay adds \(\tfrac\lambda2(\|B\|^2/\eta_B+\|A\|^2/\eta_A)\) and drives \(\Phi\to0\); coupled decay adds \(\tfrac\lambda2(\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2)\), with equilibria on the slice \(\mu=0\) of Prop III.7(a). The slices coincide iff \(\eta_A=\eta_B\) [Num: at \(\eta_B/\eta_A=4\) each run reaches its own slice to \(10^{-12}\) and stays \(O(1)\) from the other].

CreditThe names Init[A] and Init[B], and the finding that these zero-product starts train differently, are due to Hayou et al., 2024. Exponential decay of such charges under weight decay is the rescale-symmetry law of Kunin et al., 2021.

The balanced slice

In Kempf–Ness theory, the least-norm vectors on a closed orbit of a reductive group form one orbit of its compact part, and they are where the moment map vanishes. For LoRA the moment map is \(\mu=B^\top B-AA^\top\), and the least-norm representatives are the balanced ones.

Proposition III.7 (Kempf–Ness slice; Loewner floor). Known (a) On a full-rank \(GL_r\)-orbit, the critical points of \(\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2\) are exactly the balanced representatives, those with \(\mu=B^\top B-AA^\top=0\). They form one \(O(r)\)-orbit, they are the minima, and the minimum value is \(2\|BA\|_*\).

(b) Assume gradient flow with block-scalar rates \(\eta_A,\eta_B\), no weight decay, a loss that depends on \((B,A)\) only through \(BA\), and any cotangent signal of the form \(\Lambda D\rho^\top\gamma\) (dropout, minibatches and clipping included). From \(B_0=0\), \[B^\top B=\tfrac{\eta_B}{\eta_A}\big(AA^\top-A_0A_0^\top\big)\succeq0,\] so \(AA^\top\succeq A_0A_0^\top\) for all time; the \(A\)-factor never shrinks below its initialisation in the Loewner order. \(AA^\top(t)\) need not be monotone in \(t\); only this floor is guaranteed. Discrete steps break the bound by \(O(\eta^2)\) per step. Under Adam it is not guaranteed and fails empirically. With per-time decay \(\lambda\), only \(AA^\top\succeq e^{-2\lambda t}A_0A_0^\top\) holds.

CreditKempf and Ness, 1979, in the real form of Richardson and Slodowy, 1990; the nuclear-norm identity is Srebro, Rennie and Jaakkola, NIPS 2004. BaLoRA (Castin et al., 2026) turns the slice into an algorithm, moving after every step to a product-preserving representative with \(B^\top B=AA^\top\) diagonal.
(expand)

(a) For \(\xi\in\mathfrak{gl}_r\) with symmetric part \(\xi_s\), \[\tfrac{d}{dt}\big|_0\big(\|Be^{-t\xi}\|^2+\|e^{t\xi}A\|^2\big)=-2\operatorname{tr}(\xi_s\mu).\] This vanishes for all \(\xi\) iff \(\mu=0\). The function is \(O(r)\)-invariant, so by the polar decomposition \(GL_r=O(r)\exp(\mathrm{Sym}_r)\) it suffices to study \(h(t)=f(e^{t\xi}\cdot x)\) for symmetric \(\xi=P\,\mathrm{diag}(d)P^\top\). Then \[h''(t)=4\sum_id_i^2\big(\|(BP)_{:,i}\|^2e^{-2td_i}+\|(P^\top A)_{i,:}\|^2e^{2td_i}\big)\ge0,\] so \(h\) is convex and critical points are minima. If \(x\) and \(e^{\xi}\cdot x\) are both balanced, then \(h'(0)=h'(1)=0\), so \(h''\equiv0\) on \([0,1]\). Since \(B\) has full column rank, every \((BP)_{:,i}\ne0\), which forces all \(d_i=0\). Hence the balanced points form one \(O(r)\)-orbit. For existence, write \(BA=U\Sigma V^\top\); the representative \(B=U\Sigma^{1/2}O\), \(A=O^\top\Sigma^{1/2}V^\top\) is balanced, with value \(2\operatorname{tr}\Sigma\).

(b) Conservation of \(\Phi\) (Thm III.5) with \(B_0=0\) gives \[B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A=-A_0A_0^\top/\eta_A.\] For per-time decay, use Cor III.6(b).

Num On 5 random orbits at \((m,n,r)=(7,5,3)\) the minimum equals \(2\|BA\|_*\) to \(10^{-14}\).

∎

An explicit penalty \(\lambda(\|B\|^2+\|A\|^2)\) equals, at its minimisers, a \((2\lambda/s)\)-nuclear-norm penalty on \(\Delta W=sBA\) of rank \(\le r\), and SGD-coupled decay with coefficient \(\lambda\) a \((\lambda/s)\)-penalty; stationary points of either lie on \(\mu=0\). Per-time decay at unequal rates targets \(\Phi=0\), and decoupled AdamW decay is not covered.

The default start is as unbalanced as possible, and no start meets all three natural wishes.

Proposition III.8 (the balance–escape trilemma). No LoRA initialisation is simultaneously
  • (a) identity-at-init without a residual split (\(B_0A_0=0\)),
  • (b) balanced (\(B_0^\top B_0=cA_0A_0^\top\) for some \(c\gt0\): unweighted, \(c=1\), or rate-weighted, \(\Phi_0=0\) with \(c=\eta_B/\eta_A\)), and
  • (c) non-stationary: \(D\rho\) at \((B_0,A_0)\) is not the zero map; equivalently, \(\dot W(0)\ne0\) for some parameter velocity, under any first-order optimizer.

Moreover, if \(B_0A_0=0\) then \(\langle B_0^\top B_0,A_0A_0^\top\rangle_F=0\), so \[\|B_0^\top B_0-cA_0A_0^\top\|_F^2=\|B_0^\top B_0\|_F^2+c^2\|A_0A_0^\top\|_F^2,\] and zero-product initialisations are maximally unbalanced.

(expand)

Assume (a) and (b). Then \(0=B_0^\top B_0A_0=cA_0A_0^\top A_0\), so \((A_0^\top A_0)^2=0\). Since \(A_0^\top A_0\) is PSD, it vanishes, so \(A_0=0\) and then \(B_0=0\). At \((0,0)\), \(D\rho(\dot B,\dot A)=s(\dot BA+B\dot A)=0\), and also \(\nabla_B=\nabla_A=0\); so every gradient-based update rule, including Adam, momentum and weight decay, is stationary there. For the identity, \[\operatorname{tr}(B_0^\top B_0A_0A_0^\top)=\operatorname{tr}(B_0^\top(B_0A_0)A_0^\top)=0.\]

∎

Type I starts keep \(\rho\) and pick \(q_0\) in the apex fibre \(\{BA=0\}\): LoRA, EVA, Init[B], all maximally unbalanced. Type II starts change \(\rho\) by an exact split \(\theta_0=\theta_{\rm res}+\delta(q_0)\): PiSSA, MiLoRA, OLoRA, LoRA-GA, CorDA. LoftQ and QPiSSA split only approximately, over a quantised residual, so they are not identity-at-init (Prop V.2). By the trilemma, a balanced, non-stationary, identity-at-init start must be Type II, though Type II starts such as OLoRA and CorDA can be unbalanced; by Thm II.2 they are different objects, incomparable with LoRA.

Two regimes and a crossover

When the charge is a multiple of the identity, the LoRA flow has a closed form. Below a scale \(\sigma^*\), one factor carries the motion, as in LoRA-FA; above it, the factors share the work, as in balanced deep linear networks.

Theorem III.9 (closed-form isotropic-charge LoRA dynamics). Known Let \(\rho(B,A)=W_{\rm res}+sBA\) be trained by gradient flow with rates \(\eta_A,\eta_B\), and suppose the charge is isotropic, \(\Phi=-\kappa I_r\). Put \(\mu=s\sqrt{\eta_A\eta_B}\) (a rate, unrelated to the moment map of Prop III.7), and on the locus where \(Z=W-W_{\rm res}\) has rank \(r\) write its compact SVD \(Z=U\Sigma V^\top\). Then \[\dot W=-\mu^2\Big[G\,V g_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)V^\top+U f_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)U^\top G\Big],\qquad f_\kappa(x)=\sqrt{x+\tfrac{\kappa^2}4}-\tfrac\kappa2,\quad g_\kappa=f_\kappa+\kappa.\] The identity holds pointwise on \(\{\Phi=-\kappa I,\ \operatorname{rank}Z=r\}\); gradient flow is used only to keep \(\Phi\) fixed. The two terms under the root are equal at the crossover scale \(\sigma^*=\mu\lvert\kappa\rvert/2\).
  • For \(\sigma\ll\sigma^*\) with \(\kappa\gt0\), \(f\to0\) and \(g\to\kappa\), so \(\dot W\approx-\mu^2\kappa\,GP_V\). These are LoRA-FA dynamics; \(A\)’s contribution to \(\dot W\) is \(O(\sigma^2/(\mu^2\kappa))\), although \(A\) itself still drifts slowly. For \(\kappa\lt0\) the roles swap, \(\dot W\approx-\mu^2\lvert\kappa\rvert P_UG\), and only \(A\) effectively moves (Init[B]).
  • For \(\sigma\gg\sigma^*\), \(f\approx g\approx\sigma/\mu\), so \[\dot W\approx-\mu\big[G(Z^\top Z)^{1/2}+(ZZ^\top)^{1/2}G\big].\] These are the balanced dynamics of Arora, Cohen and Hazan.
  • Off the rank-\(r\) locus a correction term appears (given in the proof). At the default-LoRA start \(Z=0\) (\(B_0=0\)) it gives \(\dot W=-\mu^2\kappa\,GP_{\mathrm{row}A_0}\), so the \(\sigma\ll\sigma^*\) regime extends continuously to \(t=0\).
CreditTarmoun, França, Haeffele and Vidal (ICML 2021), who call this charge “\(\lambda\)-balanced”, and Min, Tarmoun, Vidal and Mallada (ICML 2021); see also Dominé et al., 2024 (From lazy to rich) and Kunin et al., 2024 (Get rich quick). The balanced regime is Arora, Cohen and Hazan, 2018. The PEFT reading is ours.
(expand)

Put \(\beta=B/\sqrt{\eta_B}\) and \(\alpha=A/\sqrt{\eta_A}\). Then \(\beta^\top\beta-\alpha\alpha^\top=-\kappa I\) and \(\beta\alpha=Z/\mu\). Let \(T=\beta\beta^\top\) and \(S=\alpha^\top\alpha\).

  • \[ZZ^\top/\mu^2=\beta(\beta^\top\beta+\kappa I)\beta^\top=T^2+\kappa T,\] and likewise \(Z^\top Z/\mu^2=S^2-\kappa S\).
  • On the rank-\(r\) locus, \(\alpha\alpha^\top\succ0\) and \(\beta^\top\beta\succ0\). The nonzero eigenvalues of \(T\) equal those of \(\beta^\top\beta=\alpha\alpha^\top-\kappa I\), so they exceed \(\max(0,-\kappa)\); those of \(S\) exceed \(\max(0,\kappa)\).
  • On these ranges, \(t\mapsto t^2+\kappa t\) and \(s\mapsto s^2-\kappa s\) are strictly increasing and send \(0\) to \(0\), with positive roots \(f_\kappa\) and \(g_\kappa\). The compact SVD matters here, because \(f_\kappa(0)=\lvert\kappa\rvert\) for \(\kappa\lt0\) and \(g_\kappa(0)=\kappa\) for \(\kappa\gt0\), so the full matrix function would be wrong on \(\ker Z\). Hence \(T=Uf_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)U^\top\) and \(S=Vg_\kappa(\Sigma^2/\mu^2)V^\top\).
  • Finally, \[\dot W=-s^2(\eta_BGA^\top A+\eta_ABB^\top G)=-\mu^2(GS+TG).\]

Off the locus: for \(\kappa\ge0\), \(T=f_\kappa(ZZ^\top/\mu^2)\) still holds as a full matrix function, but \(S\) gains the term \(\kappa(P_{\mathrm{row}A}-P_V)\), since \(S=\alpha^\top\alpha\) also has the eigenvalue \(\kappa\) on \(\mathrm{row}A\ominus\mathrm{row}Z\); symmetrically, for \(\kappa\lt0\), \(T\) gains \(\lvert\kappa\rvert(P_{\mathrm{col}B}-P_U)\). At \(Z=0\) this leaves \(S=\kappa P_{\mathrm{row}A_0}\) and \(T=0\).

Num The formula matches direct integration to \(10^{-12}\), and the pointwise identity holds to \(10^{-15}\) for \(\kappa\in\{-2,-0.3,0,0.3,2,10\}\) and all shape cases.

∎

Default LoRA has \(B_0=0\) and \(A_0A_0^\top=cI\), so \(\kappa=c/\eta_A\) and \(\sigma^*=\tfrac{sc}2\sqrt{\eta_B/\eta_A}\), with \(c\approx1/3\) for HF’s default. LoRA+ multiplies \(\sigma^*\) by \(\sqrt\lambda\) at fixed \(\eta_A\). PiSSA at equal rates has \(\kappa=0\) and stays balanced under gradient flow, though not under Adam.

CreditThe one-sided regime fits the finding of Zhu et al., 2024 that a random, untrained \(A\) does nearly as well as a tuned one; the theorem says it lasts while \(\Delta W\) stays well below \(\sigma^*\).

The theorem is exact for orthonormal frames (EVA, LoRA-GA). For Kaiming starts the eigenvalues of \(A_0A_0^\top\) lie near \(c(1\pm\sqrt{r/n})^2\) (Marchenko–Pastur), so the crossover is a band of relative width \(O(\sqrt{r/n})\); at \(r=256\), \(n=4096\), those of \(3A_0A_0^\top\) span \(0.57\) to \(1.57\). Prediction Under SGD, singular values of \(\Delta W\) switch from one-sided to two-sided growth as they cross \(\sigma^*\).

The hyperparameter torus

Rescale block \(i\) by \(c_i\) and divide \(s\) by the matching power of the \(c_i\). The method is the same object of \(\mathsf{Meth}\), and training is the same if every step rescales alike. Adam ignores the size of a block’s gradient, so its rate must scale like \(c_i\); an SGD step scales with the gradient, which shrinks by \(c_i\), so its rate must scale like \(c_i^2\).

Theorem III.10 (the hyperparameter torus). Known in part Let \(\rho(q)=\theta_b+s\,\delta(q_1,\dots,q_k)\) with \(\delta\) multihomogeneous of multidegree \((d_1,\dots,d_k)\). Train block \(i\) from \(q_i(0)=\sigma_iu_i\) with Adam (\(\beta_1\in[0,1)\), \(\beta_2\in(0,1)\), bias correction allowed) at learning rate \(\eta_i(t)\), on the loss \(L\circ\rho\) with no \(q\)-dependent regulariser. Assume no gradient clipping (or per-block clipping with thresholds rescaled as below), common random numbers (the same data order and dropout masks in both runs), and exact arithmetic (the result is bit-exact in floating point when every \(c_i\) is a power of \(2\)). Then the trajectory \(t\mapsto\rho(q(t))\) is invariant under the torus action of \(c\in\mathbb R_{\gt0}^k\): \[\big(s,\ \eta_i(t),\ \sigma_i,\ \varepsilon_i,\ \lambda_i,\ C_i\big)\mapsto\Big(s\prod_ic_i^{-d_i},\ c_i\eta_i(t),\ c_i\sigma_i,\ \varepsilon_i/c_i,\ \lambda_i/c_i,\ C_i/c_i\Big).\]

Here \(\varepsilon_i\) is a per-block Adam \(\varepsilon\) (with the convention \(0/0=0\) at \(\varepsilon=0\)), \(\lambda_i\) a per-block decoupled (AdamW) weight decay, and \(C_i\) a per-block clipping threshold. Under SGD, with or without heavy-ball or Nesterov momentum, the same holds with \(\eta_i(t)\mapsto c_i^2\eta_i(t)\) and coupled \(L^2\) decay \(\lambda_i\mapsto\lambda_i/c_i^2\). In particular, any learning-rate schedule shared by all blocks, warm-up included, is preserved.

(expand)

The rescaling \(q_i'=c_iq_i\), together with the compensating \(s\), is an isomorphism in \(\mathsf{Meth}\). At corresponding points, \(\nabla_{q_i'}=c_i^{-1}\nabla_{q_i}\). With \(\varepsilon_i'=\varepsilon_i/c_i\), Adam’s normalised step is invariant to the positive rescaling \(c_i^{-1}\) of a block’s whole gradient history, so \(\Delta q_i'=c_i\Delta q_i\) requires \(\eta_i'(t)=c_i\eta_i(t)\), and the decoupled decay term \(\eta_i'\lambda_i'q_i'=c_i\eta_i\lambda_iq_i\) requires \(\lambda_i'=\lambda_i/c_i\).

For SGD and momentum the buffers are linear in the gradients, so the gradient factor \(c_i^{-1}\) combines with the required \(c_i\) to give \(c_i^2\), and coupled decay enters the gradient as \(\lambda_i'q_i'\), giving \(\lambda_i/c_i^2\). Clipping decisions agree when the thresholds scale like the gradients. The time-dependence of \(\eta_i\) plays no role. Conclude by induction over steps.

Num Trajectory differences are exactly \(0.0\) for power-of-two \(c\) and about \(10^{-15}\) otherwise, including a warm-up schedule and rescaled \(\varepsilon\) and decay. With \(\varepsilon\) or decay held fixed, or with global-norm clipping, they are \(10^{-3}\) to \(O(1)\).

∎

Only the torus orbit of the hyperparameters reaches the weights. LoRA is the case \(k=2\), \(d=(1,1)\); \(\delta\) may be any multilinear update, such as VeRA’s in its two scaling vectors or LoHa’s in its four factors.

CreditThe LoRA/Adam case, with the \(\varepsilon\gt0\) extension and the two effective hyperparameters, is Schulman et al., 2025, LoRA Without Regret. New here are arbitrary multihomogeneous \(\delta\) and the SGD and heavy-ball \(c^2\) law.
Corollary III.11 (LoRA+ is \(\alpha\) in disguise; rsLoRA is LoRA). Known in part For linear LoRA modules (\(k=2\), \(d=(1,1)\), \(\sigma_B=0\)), under the hypotheses of Thm III.10:

(a) Effective hyperparameters. Under Adam the torus invariants of \((s,\eta_A,\eta_B,\sigma_A)\) are \((s\eta_A\eta_B,\ \sigma_A/\eta_A)\); Schulman et al.’s \(\alpha\cdot\mathrm{init}_A\cdot\mathrm{LR}_B\) and \(\mathrm{init}_A/\mathrm{LR}_A\) are equivalent generators. Under SGD the invariants are \((s^2\eta_A\eta_B,\ \sigma_A^2/\eta_A)\).

(b) LoRA+. Under Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\), no weight decay, no gradient clipping and a learning-rate schedule shared by both groups, LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) at scale \(s\) produces exactly the trajectory of plain LoRA at scale \(\lambda s\) (same \(\eta_A\), same \(A_0\)). For \(\varepsilon\gt0\) the equivalence needs a per-block \(\varepsilon_B'=\lambda\varepsilon\), and with AdamW a per-group decay \(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{wd}\) on \(B\). Under SGD it holds at scale \(\sqrt\lambda\,s\), or equivalently with plain LoRA at the single rate \(\sqrt{\eta_A\eta_B}\) and \(A_0\mapsto\lambda^{1/4}A_0\).

(c) rsLoRA. \(\rho_{\rm rs}(B,A)=\rho_{\rm LoRA}(\sqrt r\,B,A)\) is an isomorphism of objects. Up to the torus, rsLoRA is LoRA at the same \(\alpha\) with \(\eta_B\) multiplied by \(\sqrt r\) under Adam (by \(r\) under SGD), or LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\sqrt r\) at the same rates; “same object, different metric” is a choice of orbit representative. (c) fixes the rank and does not address rsLoRA’s prescription for how \(\alpha\) should scale with \(r\).

(expand)

(a) These are the invariants of the 2-torus action. (b) Take \(c_B=1/\lambda\), \(c_A=1\) under Adam, or \(c_B=\lambda^{-1/2}\) under SGD; the single-rate form is \(c_A=\lambda^{1/4}\), \(c_B=\lambda^{-1/4}\). Since \(\sigma_B=0\), the start \(B_0=0\) is unchanged. Thm III.10 gives \(\varepsilon_B'=\varepsilon/c_B\) and \(\mathrm{wd}_B'=\mathrm{wd}/c_B\). (c) Take \(c_B=\sqrt r\).

Num The maximum trajectory difference is exactly \(0.0\) at \(\varepsilon=0\) for power-of-two \(\lambda\) and about \(10^{-14}\) otherwise, against \(0.93\) for the unrescaled control; \(5\times10^{-9}\) at a shared \(\varepsilon=10^{-8}\); \(0.6\) with global-norm clipping at \(0.5\).

∎

So LoRA+, rsLoRA and \(\alpha\)-tuning form one family. The slogan depends on the chart. Holding \(\eta_A\) fixed, LoRA+ is \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\); holding \(\eta_B\) fixed, it is LoRA with \(\eta_A\) divided by \(\lambda\).

Prediction Any gain of LoRA+ over LoRA with \(\alpha\) re-tuned by \(\lambda\) must come from what breaks the torus: global-norm clipping (HF Trainer default max_grad_norm=1.0), \(\varepsilon\) or weight decay shared across groups (as in HF PEFT’s LoRA+ optimizer), or group-specific schedules. That optimizer treats embeddings and 1-D parameters separately, so this covers linear modules only.

CreditLoRA+ is Hayou et al., 2024, rsLoRA is Kalajdzievski, 2023, and (a) with the LoRA+ reading is Schulman et al., 2025. On the empirical side, Lee et al., 2026 find that nine LoRA variants reach similar peak performance once the learning rate is tuned.

What the optimizer can see

An optimizer is equivariant under a gauge element \(g\) if starting from \((Bg^{-1},gA)\) gives the transported trajectory. SGD’s Euclidean metric respects rotations of the rank channels; Adam, acting coordinatewise, respects only the signed permutations.

2026Privacy noise fits the same pattern. DP-SGD on the factors adds isotropic Gaussian noise \(\xi\) to the clipped factor gradients, and \(\xi g^{-1}\) has the law of \(\xi\) only for orthogonal \(g\), so it respects \(O(r)\) and no larger subgroup. PRISM (Wang and Zhang, 2026) clips and noises the projection of each gradient onto the tangent space of \(\mathcal M_r\) at \(BA\), which depends only on \(\mathrm{col}\,B\) and \(\mathrm{row}\,A\). For full-rank factors the privatised gradient then has the same law for every factorisation of \(BA\). Its Algorithm 1 then applies damped \(r\times r\) preconditioners (its Eqs. 24–26), which the paper also calls gauge invariant. They respect only \(O(r)\): under \((B,A)\mapsto(cB,A/c)\) the preconditioned factor steps do not change, so \(\delta B\,A\) scales by \(1/c\) and \(B\,\delta A\) by \(c\). The released code returns to balanced factors, \(B^\top B=AA^\top\), after every step, where the remaining gauge is \(O(r)\).
Theorem III.12 (the optimizer gauge hierarchy). On LoRA’s full-rank locus, with hyperparameters shared by all rank channels, the largest subgroup of the gauge \(GL_r\) under which an update rule is equivariant, at the level of parameters, is as follows.

(a) SGD and GD with any block rates, with or without heavy-ball momentum: \(O(r)\).

(b) Adam and AdamW (\(\beta_1,\beta_2\in[0,1)\), any \(\varepsilon\ge0\), any weight decay): the signed permutations \(B_r\), of order \(2^rr!\). A positive diagonal \(g=\mathrm{diag}(d)\) is a symmetry only jointly with per-rank-channel hyperparameters, without global-norm clipping: rates \(\eta_{B,j}/d_j\) and \(d_j\eta_{A,j}\), and, when nonzero, \(\varepsilon_{B,j}=d_j\varepsilon\), \(\varepsilon_{A,j}=\varepsilon/d_j\) and (for decay multiplied by the learning rate, as in PyTorch’s AdamW) \(\lambda_{B,j}=d_j\lambda\), \(\lambda_{A,j}=\lambda/d_j\).

(c) Undamped scaled GD (\(\delta=0\); the Riemannian preconditioner of Tong, Ma and Chi and of Zhang and Pilanci, with or without heavy-ball momentum): all of \(GL_r\). From gauge-related initial states, with zero or correspondingly transformed momentum, its \(W\)-trajectory does not depend on the representative.

CreditScaled GD is Tong, Ma and Chi, 2021 and Zhang and Pilanci, 2024. LoRA-RITE (Yen et al., 2024) makes gauge invariance a design requirement for LoRA optimizers and reports that it pays.

Published variants. Damped scaled GD (\(\delta\gt0\)) is only \(O(r)\)-equivariant, and wrapped in AdamW (Zhang and Pilanci’s scaled AdamW, HF’s create_riemannian_optimizer) it is \(B_r\)-equivariant. LoRA-Pro’s parameter update with the Sylvester-optimal \(X\) is only \(O(r)\)-equivariant, and its discrete \(W\)-step depends on the representative at \(O(\eta^2)\). LoRA-RITE’s Definition 1 asks only for \(W\)-level invariance, yet on the full-rank locus its update is \(GL_r\)-equivariant in the parameters. Followed line by line through its Algorithm 1, a gauge change alters its internal quantities (unmagnified gradients, transported moments) only by orthogonal matrices, and the final multiplication by \((R_B^\top)^+\) supplies the gauge element; the resulting step is its Eq. 15. That puts this Adam-type optimizer in class (c).

(expand)

Under \((Bg^{-1},gA)\) we have \(\nabla_{B'}=\nabla_Bg^\top\) and \(\nabla_{A'}=g^{-\top}\nabla_A\). Equivariance means \(\delta B'=\delta B\,g^{-1}\) and \(\delta A'=g\,\delta A\), for arbitrary gradients.

(a) SGD needs \(g^\top=g^{-1}\); momentum buffers are linear in the gradients.

(b) “\(\supseteq\)”: coordinatewise rules commute with signed permutations, since the moment \(m\) flips sign and \(v\) does not change. “\(\subseteq\)”: the first Adam step from zero state is \(\delta B=-\eta\,\phi(\nabla_B)\) with \(\phi(x)=x/(\lvert x\rvert+\varepsilon)\) coordinatewise. Write \(h=g^{-1}\).

  • Case \(\varepsilon=0\). Equivariance reads \(\mathrm{sign}(xg^\top)=\mathrm{sign}(x)h\) for every row vector \(x\). Perturbing \(x\) realises every sign pattern \(s\in\{\pm1\}^r\) with \(xg^\top\) zero-free, so \(sh\in\{\pm1\}^r\) for all \(s\). Flipping \(s_i\) changes \((sh)_j\) by \(2s_ih_{ij}\in\{0,\pm2\}\), so \(h_{ij}\in\{0,\pm1\}\). A column with \(t\ne1\) nonzero entries yields a sum of absolute value \(0\) or \(\ge2\) for suitable \(s\), so each column of \(h\) has exactly one entry \(\pm1\). Hence \(h\), and therefore \(g\), lies in \(B_r\).
  • Case \(\varepsilon\gt0\). For small \(x\), \(\phi(x)\approx x/\varepsilon\), which forces \(g\in O(r)\). For large \(x\), \(\phi\to\mathrm{sign}\), which forces \(g\in B_r\).

For the joint torus, take \(g=\mathrm{diag}(d)\). Gradient column \(j\) of \(B\) is scaled by \(d_j\), while equivariance requires the step to be scaled by \(1/d_j\). At \(\varepsilon=0\), Adam’s step is invariant to positive per-coordinate scaling of the gradient, so the rate of column \(j\) must be multiplied by \(1/d_j\), and the rate of row \(j\) of \(A\) by \(d_j\). With \(\varepsilon\gt0\), \[d_jm/(d_j\sqrt v+\varepsilon')=m/(\sqrt v+\varepsilon'/d_j),\] which requires \(\varepsilon'=d_j\varepsilon\); the decoupled decay term \(\eta'\lambda'B'_{:,j}=(\eta/d_j)\lambda'B_{:,j}/d_j\) requires \(\lambda'=d_j\lambda\). Rows of \(A\) are symmetric. Global-norm clipping compares the total gradient norm with a threshold, and a non-orthogonal \(g\) changes that norm.

(c) This is Prop III.4(a); heavy-ball buffers transform like the gradients they accumulate. Damping fails for non-orthogonal \(g\) because \[g^{-1}(gAA^\top g^\top+\delta I)g^{-\top}=AA^\top+\delta g^{-1}g^{-\top}.\]

Num Defects are \(10^{-15}\) for signed permutations, \(11.3\) for a positive diagonal, \(6.6\) for a rotation, and \(5\times10^{-16}\) for the diagonal with jointly rescaled per-channel rates at \(\varepsilon=0\). With \(\varepsilon=10^{-8}\) left unrescaled the joint torus fails by about \(2\%\). LoRA-Pro with the Sylvester \(X\) has a parameter-level defect of \(1.2\) under a non-orthogonal \(g\), against \(2\times10^{-15}\) with \(X=0\) or with an orthogonal \(g\). LoRA-RITE’s Algorithm 1 from \((B,A)\) and from \((Bg^{-1},gA)\) with a generic \(g\) gives parameters that agree to \(10^{-13}\) after 30 steps.

∎

The hierarchy \(B_r\subset O(r)\subset GL_r\) is strict for \(r\ge2\). A \(GL_r\)-equivariant rule, or any rule mapping gauge orbits to gauge orbits, defines an optimizer on the model itself; \(O(r)\)- and \(B_r\)-equivariant rules in general do not. The default, Adam, sees a finite shadow of the gauge.

What a zero-B start chooses

Default LoRA starts at \(B_0=0\) with a random full-rank \(A_0\). The image remembers only \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0\) (Thm II.2). Training remembers what the optimizer’s gauge group cannot move.

Corollary III.13 (what a zero-\(B\) initialisation really chooses). At \(q_0=(0,A_0)\) with \(\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\), with hyperparameters shared by the rank channels and the same data order and dropout masks, two choices of \(A_0\) give the same \(\theta\)-trajectory whenever the optimizer’s gauge group relates them. The resulting invariant of \(A_0\) is:
  • \(\mathrm{row}A_0\in\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\) for an update rule that is \(GL_r\)-equivariant at every point of its trajectory, including \(B=0\) and rank-deficient \(B\), run without global-norm clipping. An example is \(\delta B=-\eta\nabla_B(AA^\top)^{-1}\), \(\delta A=-\eta(AA^\top)\nabla_A\), which needs only \(\operatorname{rank}A=r\);
  • the PSD matrix \(A_0^\top A_0\) for SGD, which is exactly the initial preconditioner \(\dot W(0)=-s^2\eta_BGA_0^\top A_0\);
  • \(A_0\) up to signed permutation of its rows for Adam.

Conversely, different invariants give different dynamics, for SGD and for Adam at \(\varepsilon=0\).

Random-\(A\) LoRA and EVA are different points of the \(B=0\) stratum \(\{(0,A):\operatorname{rank}A=r\}\) of the apex fibre \(\rho^{-1}(\theta_0)=\{(B,A):BA=0\}\); Init[B] lies in another stratum of the same fibre. LoRA-FA is a different object, the sub-object \(\mathrm{FA}(A_0)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{LoRA}_{(0,A_0)}\) (Prop I.8). More precisely, EVA is a point of the apex fibre of LoRA\(_{r_l}\) at its per-layer redistributed rank and adjusted \(\alpha\), with orthonormal rows only when whiten=False.

(expand)

Only the pointwise “\(\supseteq\)” half of Thm III.12 is used. For SGD and Adam it holds off the full-rank locus, and the initial Adam state \((m,v)=0\) is fixed by \(B_r\). With \(B_0=0\) the term \(B_0g^{-1}\) stays \(0\), so equivariant updates give identical \(W\)-trajectories. Orthogonal \(g\), hence \(O(r)\) and \(B_r\), preserve gradient norms and so commute with global-norm clipping; a general \(g\in GL_r\) does not. Left \(O(r)\)-orbits of full-rank \(A_0\) are classified by \(A_0^\top A_0\) (Thm II.5(2)), and \(GL_r\)-orbits by the row space (proof of Thm II.2). For the example rule, \(\nabla_{A'}=g^{-\top}\nabla_A\) and \(A'A'^\top=gAA^\top g^\top\) give \(\delta A'=g\,\delta A\), and \(\delta B'=\delta B\,g^{-1}\) as in Prop III.4(a).

The converse compares first steps. Under SGD the first step from \((0,A_0)\) is exactly \(\delta W_1=-s^2\eta_BGA_0^\top A_0\), so equal trajectories for every linear loss force \(A_0^\top A_0=A_0'^\top A_0'\). Under Adam at \(\varepsilon=0\) it is \(\delta W_1=-s\eta_B\,\mathrm{sign}(GA_0^\top)A_0\), and row by row \(F(x)=\mathrm{sign}(xA_0^\top)A_0\) is constant off the hyperplanes \(a_j^\perp\) and jumps by \(\pm2a_j\) across each. So \(F\) determines the hyperplanes, hence each row up to a factor, and the size of its jump fixes that factor up to sign: \(A_0'\) is a signed row permutation of \(A_0\).

Num SGD from \(A_0\) and \(QA_0\) agree to \(10^{-15}\), and from \(A_0\) and \(DA_0\) differ by \(0.81\). Adam from \(A_0\) and \(PA_0\) agree to \(10^{-16}\), and from \(A_0\) and \(QA_0\) differ by \(0.98\). The example rule from \(A_0\) and \(gA_0\) agrees to \(3\times10^{-15}\). LoRA-RITE’s Algorithm 1, run as written with its pseudo-inverses from \(B_0=0\), gives the same weights from \(A_0\) and from \(gA_0\) for a generic \(g\) to \(10^{-13}\), and weights that differ by \(O(1)\) for a different row space.

∎

The strata of Thm II.2 are the expressive quotient; each optimizer sees a finer one. Undamped scaled GD is undefined at \(B=0\), so scaled-GD optimizers enter in damped (\(O(r)\)) or AdamW-wrapped (\(B_r\)) form. LoRA-RITE is defined there through pseudo-inverses, but its \(GL_r\)-equivariance is proved only on the full-rank locus.

Update ruleEquivariance groupWhat it reads off \(A_0\) at \(B_0=0\)
\(GL_r\)-equivariant along the whole trajectory (the example rule; LoRA-RITE’s Algorithm 1, checked numerically)\(GL_r\)\(\mathrm{row}A_0\in\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\)
SGD, GD\(O(r)\)\(A_0^\top A_0\)
damped scaled GD\(O(r)\)\(A_0^\top A_0\)

CreditHayou et al., 2024 showed that the two zero-product starts, random \(A\) with \(B=0\) and random \(B\) with \(A=0\), train differently; here they lie in different strata of one apex fibre, with charges of opposite sign. EVA (Paischer et al., 2024) and random-\(A\) LoRA share a stratum.

Two problems stay open. One is the apex fibre modulo \(B_r\) for Adam, stratified by \(\Phi_0\), degenerate pointings included. The other is an Adam analogue of Thm III.9, a conserved or monotone quantity for sign-like updates.

Exposé IV reads the same cometric from the backward side of the lens, where methods that compress gradients have cometrics of their own.

Exposé IV

The lens: tangent and cotangent methods

Backpropagation pairs every map with its reverse derivative, so a method can shape what the model expresses on the forward side or compress the gradient on the backward side. For linear methods and optimizers that read only their gradients, the two sides give the same weights, and GaLore becomes one-sided LoRA with a moving frame and carried optimizer state.

Every arrow can be read twice. Forward, it says where the point may go. Backward, it says what the point is allowed to hear.

a working maxim
In one minute
  • Backpropagation pairs every map with its reverse derivative, a lens. A method can change the forward map, and so what the model can express (LoRA), or only compress the gradient on the way back (GaLore, LISA, MeZO).
  • If an adapter is linear in its trainable tensor and the optimizer reads only its gradients (Adam with no weight decay and no global-norm clipping, say), the two readings give identical weights. GaLore is then one-sided LoRA with its projector as frozen factor, re-based every \(T\) steps with the optimizer state carried over (Torroba-Hennigen et al., 2025).
  • Frobenius’ theorem decides when a backward method’s directions could come from forward methods. It is vacuous for GaLore, LISA and MeZO, which fix their directions for a period, and full-batch GaLore with \(T=1\) can fail it at every rank.
  • Only the backprop cone costs activation memory: inputs of trainable linear boxes and residuals of nonlinear boxes downstream of them. Frozen linear layers need to keep nothing, so LoRA-FA and ladder side-tuning save activations, while LISA and prompt tuning truncate nothing.

Two sides of one port

A layer does two jobs in training. Forward, it turns parameters and inputs into outputs; backward, it turns a cotangent at the output into cotangents at the input and the parameters. Backpropagation keeps the pair together, and a category theorist calls it a lens.

CreditThe equivalence between GaLore and one-sided LoRA is due to Torroba-Hennigen, Lang, Guo and Kim, 2025, who prove a duality between linear gradient transformations and adapters (their Thm 1 and Cor. 3, with weight decay in App. B). The frozen-\(A\) case under SGD, with random projections, appears earlier in Hao, Cao and Mou, 2024. Our additions are the lens bookkeeping, the explicit hypotheses on the optimizer, and the table that sorts the cotangent family.

Every method in Exposés I to III changes the forward job, replacing the parameter port \(\Theta\) by a smaller space \(Q\) and a map \(\rho:Q\to\Theta\). A second family edits only the backward job. GaLore compresses the weight gradient \(G\) to \(P^\top G\), with \(P\) from an SVD of \(G\), runs Adam on that small matrix and maps the result back with \(P\). Every weight trains; only the optimizer state is small. LISA and MeZO follow the same pattern.

When \(\rho\) is linear the two families meet. The pullback of the forward method is then the compression of a backward method, and an optimizer that reads only its gradients cannot tell the two apart. Figure 10 sorts the backward family and draws what backpropagation must store.

Backpropagation as a lens

In the language of Def I.1, a layer is a parametrised map \(f:P\times X\to Y\). Its reverse derivative \(\mathsf R[f](x,y)=J_f(x)^\top y\) takes an output cotangent to an input cotangent, parameters included, and backpropagation is the 2-functor

\[\mathbf{Para}(\mathsf R):\mathbf{Para}(\mathcal C)\longrightarrow\mathbf{Para}(\mathbf{Lens}(\mathcal C)),\qquad f\longmapsto(f,\mathsf R[f]).\]

A reparametrisation \(\rho\) is a 2-cell, so the functor turns it into a lens \((\rho,\mathsf R[\rho])\). Its tangent side is the image, what the method can express (Exposé II). Its cotangent side \(\mathsf R[\rho](q,\gamma)=D\rho_q^\top\gamma\) pulls the weight gradient back to \(Q\) and, with the rates \(\Lambda\), induces the cometric \(K=D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\) (Def III.1). A lens need not come from a map, though, and a backward half written down directly gives the second family.

CreditThe parametrised-map construction and the reading of backpropagation as a functor into lenses are due to Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras, 2017 and Cruttwell, Gavranović, Ghani, Wilson and Zanasi, 2021.
Definition IV.1 (forward and backward methods). A forward method acts on the parameter port by a lens in the image of \(\mathsf R\), namely \((\rho,\mathsf R[\rho])\). A backward, or cotangent-side, method acts by a lens whose forward part is \(\mathrm{id}_\Theta\) and whose backward part is a compression \(c_t:T^*_\theta\Theta\to S\) into a state space \(S\), paired with an anchor \(a_t:S\to T_\theta\Theta\). The update is \[\theta\leftarrow\theta-\eta\,a_t\big(\mathrm{opt}(c_t(\nabla L))\big).\]
MethodCompression \(c_t\)Anchor \(a_t\)
GaLore\(P_t^\top\), with \(P_t\) from a gradient SVD\(P_t\)
GaLore with a random projectoras GaLore, with \(P_t\) random\(P_t\)
LISAlayer-block coordinate projectionthe same projection
MeZO\(g\mapsto\langle z_t,g\rangle\), estimated by finite differences without a backward pass\(\lambda\mapsto\lambda z_t\)

A backward method has its own cometric \(K_t=a_tc_t\) (Def III.1), for instance \(K(G)=PP^\top G\) for GaLore under SGD. The update must lie in the image of the anchor, so Flora as published, APOLLO and Fira, whose updates leave it, are not backward methods here.

More: why Flora falls outside

Flora compresses its momentum or gradient accumulator with a random projection and hands the decompressed tensor to Adafactor, which acts in the full space, so its update need not lie in the image of the anchor. On resampling it transports its momentum to the new frame. It fits Def IV.1 only in an idealised form with an SGD or heavy-ball base. An earlier version of the table above listed Flora where GaLore with a random projector now stands.

The degree-one collapse

If \(\rho(C)=W_k+\mathcal LC\), training \(C\) and the backward method with \(c=\mathcal L^*\), \(a=\mathcal L\) feed the optimizer the same gradients \(\mathcal L^*G\) and so make the same weight updates. A curved \(\rho\), such as LoRA’s \(BA\), separates them after one step, and so does an optimizer that reads the parameter tensor, \(C\) in one procedure and \(W\) in the other.

Proposition IV.2 (the degree-one collapse). Known Let \(\rho_k(C)=W_k+\mathcal LC\) with \(\mathcal L\) linear and \(C_0=0\). Let opt be a first-order optimizer whose state and output depend only on the sequence of gradients it is fed, and which does not read the parameter tensor: SGD, heavy-ball or Nesterov momentum, Adam or AMSGrad, or Adafactor with scale_parameter=False (fixed or relative step, factorising the compressed gradient in the shape of \(C\)), each with no weight decay, coupled or decoupled. Run both procedures with the same implementation of opt, on the same minibatches and from the same optimizer state, fresh or carried over. Then they produce the same \(W\)-trajectory over a period:
  • training \(C\) with opt and merging at the end of the period;
  • the backward method with \(c=\mathcal L^*\) and \(a=\mathcal L\), started at \(W_k\).

AdamW with \(\lambda\gt0\), LAMB and LARS trust ratios, parameter-scaled Adafactor (scale_parameter=True, the default in HF and in galore_torch’s GaLoreAdafactor) and global-norm clipping of the full gradient, such as HF Trainer’s default max_grad_norm=1.0 whenever it triggers, all break the identity. Injectivity of \(\mathcal L\) is not needed.

In particular, on each matrix GaLore projects (its other parameters train in full in both procedures), GaLore with a projector \(P_k\) fixed on each period equals one-sided LoRA \(W_k+P_kC\) (frozen \(B=P_k\), or frozen \(A\) when projecting on the right), merged and re-based every period. This needs the hypotheses above, \(C\)’s optimizer state (moments and step counter) carried over unchanged rather than reset, and \(P_k\) equal to GaLore’s matrix itself, signs and column order included; its span alone does not suffice. ReLoRA-style resets give a different trajectory. GaLore’s scale \(\alpha\) multiplies the optimizer output, so it is a learning-rate factor.

(expand)

By induction, \(W_t=W_k+\mathcal LC_t\) along the first procedure. Both procedures therefore feed opt the same sequence \(\mathcal L^*\nabla L(W_t)\) from the same state, and they receive the same outputs \(U_t\). One sets \(C\leftarrow C-\eta U_t\), which moves the weight by \(-\eta\mathcal LU_t\); the other sets \(W\leftarrow W-\eta\mathcal LU_t\) directly. Injectivity of \(\mathcal L\) is never used.

The excluded ingredients see different tensors in the two procedures. Decoupled decay acts on \(C\) in one, pulling \(W\) toward \(W_k\), and on \(W\) in the other, pulling it toward \(0\); coupled decay adds \(\lambda C\) to the gradient in one and \(\lambda W\) in the other. Parameter-scaled steps read \(\mathrm{RMS}(C)\) in one and \(\mathrm{RMS}(W)\) in the other. Full-gradient clipping uses \(\|G\|\) in one and \(\|\mathcal L^*G\|\) in the other.

For GaLore, apply this on each period with \(\mathcal LC=P_kC\) (or \(\mathcal LC=CP_k^\top\) when projecting on the right). GaLore’s update is \(W\leftarrow W-\eta\,\alpha\,P_k\,\mathrm{opt}(P_k^\top G)\), so \(\alpha\) multiplies the learning rate. Since the trajectories agree up to the start of a period, so do the gradients there, and both procedures read off the same \(P_k\). The state handed from one period to the next is the same object in both procedures, so the induction runs across periods. That state holds coordinates relative to \(P_{k-1}\)’s columns, so replacing \(P_k\) by another basis of its span changes what the reused moments mean.

Num Against the official galore_torch optimizers (least squares, \(W\in\mathbb R^{6\times10}\), \(r=2\), \(T=7\), four periods, total movement \(0.046\)), the two procedures agree to \(8\times10^{-17}\) with the state carried over. Resetting the LoRA side’s state separates them by \(0.029\), weight decay \(0.1\) by \(0.020\), HF-default Adafactor by \(0.030\), and flipping the sign of one column of \(P_k\) by \(0.12\). Figure 10 reruns a check of this kind live.

∎

Beyond one-sided LoRA, GaLore adds a frame read off the gradient’s SVD and the carry-over of optimizer state. The carry-over reads moments written in the coordinates of \(P_{k-1}\) as coordinates for \(P_k\), with no change of basis. LDAdam and Flora transport the state instead.

More: scale, implementation, review

Moving GaLore’s \(\alpha\) inside \(\rho\) as a LoRA scale multiplies the compressed gradient by \(\alpha\). Under Adam this gives GaLore’s trajectory only at \(\varepsilon=0\), or with \(\varepsilon\) multiplied by \(\alpha\) as well. This is the exchange of scale for learning rate and \(\varepsilon\) on the hyperparameter torus of Thm III.10.

Both sides must run the same implementation. galore_torch’s AdamW adds \(\varepsilon\) to \(\sqrt v\) before the bias correction, as in transformers, and swapping in torch.optim.Adam on the LoRA side moves the trajectory by about \(4\times10^{-5}\) in the galore_torch check quoted in the proof.

The statement was repaired after review. The optimizer class is restricted to optimizers that do not read the parameter tensor, which excludes HF’s default parameter-scaled Adafactor. With fresh state the GaLore statement failed beyond one period, because GaLore carries its Adam state across projector changes, so the state is now carried on both sides. Decoupled decay and full-gradient clipping are listed as exclusions, injectivity was dropped, and the carry-over is no longer called a transport.

The cotangent family sorts by where the projector comes from and how often it changes.

Projector / schedulefrozenre-based periodicallyresampled every step
randomLoRA-FA, with \(K\propto GA_0^\top A_0\) under SGDGaLore with a random projector; idealised FloraMeZO (rank 1, global), up to its finite-difference estimator
