Prop IV.2 covers the first three rows under its optimizer hypotheses, except EVA and LoRA-GA, which initialise two-sided LoRA and sit in the frozen column only for their first step. LoRA’s map \((B,A)\mapsto W_0+sBA\) has degree two, so its cometric moves with the state (Obs III.2) and the collapse does not apply. The other frozen entries gain nothing from merge-and-restart (Thm V.3, corollary 2); their re-based neighbours move the frame and can reach all of weight space.

CreditLoRA-FA is due to Zhang et al., 2023, MeZO to Malladi et al., 2023, LISA to Pan et al., 2024, ReLoRA to Lialin et al., 2023, EVA to Paischer et al., 2024 and LoRA-GA to Wang et al., 2024. The LoRA-FA cometric holds for the original method under SGD; HF PEFT’s LoRA-FA optimizer preconditions by \((AA^\top+\delta I)^{-1}\), which in the undamped limit gives the row-space projector of Prop III.4(c).

When a backward method is a forward one

A backward method chooses at each weight a space of directions \(D_\theta=\mathrm{im}\,a(\theta)\), and it reads as a forward method only if these fit into \(k\)-dimensional surfaces, the images of immersive parametrisations. Frobenius’ theorem tests this. The bracket \([X,Y]\), the gap by which a small loop along \(X\), \(Y\), \(-X\), \(-Y\) fails to close, must stay in \(D\). Discrete steps and the optimizer’s metric ask for more.

Proposition IV.3 (integrability). Frobenius Let \(D\) be a smooth distribution of constant rank \(k\) on an open set \(U\subseteq\Theta\), for instance the anchor distribution \(D_\theta=\mathrm{im}\,a(\theta)\) of a time-independent backward method. Then \(D\) is involutive on \(U\) iff through every \(\theta\in U\) passes an injective immersion \(\rho_\theta:(\mathbb R^k,0)\to(U,\theta)\) with \(\mathrm{im}\,D\rho_\theta=D\) along its image. A single such \(\rho\) through \(\theta_0\) gives involutivity only along \(\mathrm{im}\,\rho\). For example, \(D=\mathrm{span}\{\partial_x,\ \partial_y+xz\,\partial_z\}\) on \(\mathbb R^3\) has \([\partial_x,\partial_y+xz\,\partial_z]=z\,\partial_z\notin D\) off \(z=0\), yet the plane \(z=0\) is an integral manifold.

The dynamic content needs separate hypotheses.

  • In continuous time, if \(D\) is involutive, a backward flow with \(\dot\theta\in D_\theta\) stays on the leaf through \(\theta_0\).
  • In the discrete update of Def IV.1, the iterates stay on a leaf for all step sizes and optimizer outputs iff the leaf contains \((\theta+D_\theta)\cap U\) at every iterate, that is, iff the leaves are affine, as with an anchor constant over the period (Prop IV.2).
  • Under gradient flow, equivalence with a forward lens \((\rho,\mathsf R[\rho])\) also needs matching frames, \(a_tc_t=D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\) along the trajectory, so \(K=ac\) is symmetric PSD and, for injective \(a\), \(c=Ma^\top\) with \(M\) symmetric PSD on \(S\). If \(\rho\) is an immersion with \(\dim Q=k\), the leaf metric induced by \(K\) must also be flat; methods with gauge fibres escape this. Exact agreement of discrete steps for every step size further forces \(\rho\) to be affine along each line \(q+t\,\Lambda D\rho_q^\top g\).

Instances. GaLore with a projector fixed on each period, its random-projector variant, LISA and MeZO have anchors that do not depend on \(\theta\) within a period, so their distributions are constant and trivially involutive with affine leaves. Under the hypotheses of Prop IV.2 (which LISA with AdamW at positive decay violates) they are rebasing schemes of linear forward methods, whose reach Thm V.3 describes. At \(T=1\) this reading is automatic; each MeZO step is, up to its finite-difference estimator, a step of \(\theta_t+\lambda z_t\). What separates these methods from LoRA is the schedule and the carried state, and integrability plays no part in it.

Non-involutivity needs a \(\theta\)-dependent anchor. Full-batch GaLore with \(T=1\), read as \(D_W=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb R^n,U_r(\nabla L(W)))\) where a singular-value gap exists (for the right projection, \(\mathrm{Hom}(V_r(\nabla L(W)),\mathbb R^m)\)), need not be involutive. For every \(r\) with \(1\le r\lt\min(m,n)\) it fails for \(L=\tfrac12\|W\|_F^2\), and numerically for least squares and cross-entropy with several samples; with one least-squares sample it is involutive. Minibatch GaLore with \(T=1\) and MeZO are not time-independent, since their anchors depend on step-wise randomness.

(expand)

The first equivalence is Frobenius’ theorem. The tangent spaces of the image of an injective immersion are involutive along that image, which gives the remark about a single \(\rho\), and the bracket in the example is a direct computation. For the discrete statement, \(\theta-\eta\,a(\theta)s\) stays on the leaf for all \(\eta\) and \(s\) iff the leaf contains \((\theta+D_\theta)\cap U\).

For the frame condition, gradient flow of the forward method moves the weight by \(-D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\nabla_\theta L\) (Def III.1), and agreement for every loss forces \(a_tc_t=D\rho\,\Lambda\,D\rho^\top\). This operator is symmetric PSD with range in \(\mathrm{im}\,a\), which gives \(c=Ma^\top\) when \(a\) is injective. When \(\rho\) is an immersion with \(\dim Q=k\), it is a local diffeomorphism onto the leaf, and \(D\rho\) carries the flat metric \(\Lambda^{-1}\) on \(Q\) to the metric \(K^{-1}\) on the leaf, which is therefore flat. A forward method whose \(\rho\) has gauge fibres induces the leaf metric by a submersion instead, and that metric can be curved; balanced full-rank LoRA and \(B\mapsto BB^\top\) are examples. For discrete steps, the forward step lands at \(\rho(q-\eta v)\) with \(v=\Lambda D\rho_q^\top g\) and the backward step at \(\rho(q)-\eta\,D\rho_q v\); equality for all \(\eta\) is affineness along that line.

For the GaLore field, take \(L=\tfrac12\|W\|_F^2\), so \(\nabla L=W\), at \(W=\mathrm{diag}(\sigma_1,\dots,\sigma_{\min(m,n)})\) with \(\sigma_1\gt\dots\gt\sigma_{\min(m,n)}\gt0\), where \(U_r\) is spanned by the first \(r\) basis vectors. The fields \(X_E(W)=\Pi(W)E\), with \(\Pi\) the projector onto \(U_r\), span \(D_W\). First-order perturbation of \(\Pi\) gives, for \(E=e_1e_{r+1}^\top\) and \(F=e_1e_1^\top\), a bracket \([X_E,X_F]\) whose row \(r+1\) is \(\sigma_{r+1}/(\sigma_1^2-\sigma_{r+1}^2)\,e_1^\top\ne0\), so it leaves \(D\). At \(m=n=2\), \(r=1\) and \(W=\mathrm{diag}(2,1)\) this reads \([X_1,X_2]=-\tfrac13e_2e_1^\top\) for \(X_i=u(W)e_i^\top\). With a single sample, \(\nabla L=(Wx-y)x^\top\), and moving \(W\) along \(D\) keeps \(Wx-y\) in \(U_1\), so \(U_1\) is constant along \(D\) and the leaves are affine.

Num For least squares and cross-entropy with several samples, the relative normal component of the bracket is \(0.08\) to \(0.95\) at \((m,n,r)=(4,3,1)\), \((5,4,1)\) and \((4,3,2)\); Figure 10 recomputes it live at \(W\in\mathbb R^{3\times4}\). The diagonal formula above was checked by central differences for several \((m,n,r)\).

∎
More: what review changed

Repaired after two reviews. The literal “only if” was false, since one integral manifold implies involutivity only along itself; the example in the statement shows it. The dynamic hypotheses are now separate. Flatness of the leaf metric is required only of immersive \(\rho\) with \(\dim Q=k\), since gauge-fibred methods escape it: balanced full-rank LoRA induces a curved leaf metric. An earlier claim that full-batch rank-one GaLore is integrable is withdrawn; the diagonal example in the proof refutes it at every rank.

More: in what sense “dual”
Analogy (the lens duality). “Tangent side” and “cotangent side” are exact words here. A forward method acts on tangent vectors through \(D\rho\), and a backward method acts on cotangent vectors through \(c\). Calling GaLore and one-sided LoRA dual is exact only at degree one, and only for optimizers that do not read the parameter tensor. Beyond that range the word organises the zoo and proves nothing.

What the backward pass must remember

Fine-tuning memory is weights, optimizer state, and activations stored for the backward pass. Shrinking \(Q\) shrinks the second. As for the third, a linear box’s reverse derivative \(W^\top y\) ignores its input, and cotangents are needed only downstream of something trainable, so only a cone through the network costs activation memory.

Observation IV.4 (backward reach and the backprop cone). Upper bound Assume reverse mode in which each box keeps its own residual, with no checkpointing across boxes, and take boxes at the granularity where adapters are separate boxes (for LoRA-FA, \(A_0\) and \(B\) separately). A VJP residual is an input, an output, a sufficient statistic such as a ReLU mask, or enough to recompute one of these within the box. Then it suffices to keep a VJP residual for
  • each trainable box whose parameter-VJP depends on its input: linear and multiplicative boxes such as \(W\), \(A\), \(B\) and (IA)³ scales; additive boxes such as biases and prompts need nothing;
  • each nonlinear box on a directed path from a trainable box to the loss.

Deterministic frozen linear boxes, and all boxes off this cone, need nothing; dropout on the cone needs its mask or random state. Alternatively, \(k\) forward-mode JVPs give the \(k\) coordinates of \(P^\top\nabla L\) exactly, with no reverse pass, \(O(1)\)-layer activation memory, and time a small constant multiple of \(k\) forward passes. That route is time-competitive with one VJP only for very small \(k\), and it needs \(P\) in advance, so it cannot produce GaLore’s SVD refresh, where \(P\) depends on \(\nabla L\), more cheaply than a backward pass.

(sketch; expand)

Cotangents are needed only on the downstream cone of the trainable boxes, so everything else can be pruned. A frozen linear box has \(\mathsf R[f](x,y)=W^\top y\), which does not depend on \(x\), whereas a nonlinear box’s reverse derivative depends on its input through some residual. A trainable linear box \(y=Wx\) has parameter-VJP \(\bar y\,x^\top\), and a multiplicative box \(y=l\odot x\) has \(\bar y\odot x\) summed over tokens, so both read \(x\). A bias’s parameter cotangent is its incoming cotangent. A dropout box’s VJP applies the same rescaled mask to \(\bar y\).

The \(i\)-th coordinate of \(P^\top\nabla L\) is the directional derivative \(DL(\theta)[p_i]\), which forward mode computes alongside a forward pass, keeping only the current layer’s values and tangents. The memory and time costs of forward mode are the standard ones of automatic differentiation and are not re-derived here.

∎

The bound reads off the activation memory of the zoo.

  • LST reads the backbone through trainable down-projection ladders, so the backbone’s backward pass never runs.
  • LoRA-FA stores \(A_0x\) (\(r\) numbers per token) instead of \(x\) (\(n\) numbers), plus the dropout mask unless lora_dropout=0, HF’s default.
  • Top-layer adaptation truncates the cone, as long as no checkpointing sends cotangents further down (see the caveat).
  • LISA trains the embedding and LM head at every step, so its cone is the whole network. Its unsampled layers’ frozen linear boxes drop their input residuals, so it keeps fewer activations than full fine-tuning or LoRA, though most of its saving is in gradients and optimizer state.
  • Prompt tuning has a \(Q\) as small as VeRA’s, FourierFT’s or (IA)³’s but the maximal cone, the whole network, and its prompt tokens enlarge every stored activation.
  • MeZO is the case \(k=1\), with a central difference in place of the JVP and no reverse pass.
CaveatWith reentrant gradient checkpointing, HF PEFT makes the embedding outputs require gradients (enable_input_require_grads), so cotangents run down to the embeddings and top-layer adaptation no longer truncates the cone.
CreditLadder side-tuning is due to Sung, Cho and Bansal, 2022, prompt tuning to Lester et al., 2021, and the zeroth-order estimator in this setting to Malladi et al., 2023. That reverse derivatives of linear maps ignore their inputs is the observation behind many activation-saving designs; the lens of Cruttwell et al., 2021 only makes it visible box by box.
More: fine print on the readings

LoRA-FA’s saving is real only where no neighbouring nonlinear box already keeps \(x\). MeZO’s estimate is unbiased for the gradient of the Gaussian-smoothed loss, and for a loss with Lipschitz Hessian its bias against \(\nabla L\) is \(O(\varepsilon^2)\). Prompt tuning shares the maximal cone with every method that trains something in the first layer. A LISA variant that froze the embedding would truncate the cone at its lowest sampled layer. GaLore, its random-projector variant and LISA all take the reverse route. Exact unbiasedness, \(\mathbb E[(\nabla L\cdot z)z]=\nabla L\), holds for the forward gradient computed with an exact JVP rather than MeZO’s finite difference.

Repaired after review. The observation is now an upper bound with an explicit notion of residual. Additive trainable boxes need nothing, dropout needs its mask, the JVP route is competitive in time only for very small \(k\), and MeZO’s central difference is biased at order \(\varepsilon^2\). LISA, which trains its embedding, keeps the whole cone while still dropping the residuals of its frozen linear boxes.

So every cotangent method meeting the hypotheses of Prop IV.2 is, period by period, a linear forward method on a moving base. What it reaches, and what a quantiser does to the base, are the subject of Exposé V.

Exposé V

Base change: moving the point

Quantising and restarting move the point a method is attached to, and combining adapters builds one update from several. Each move comes down to one computation, of the defect a quantiser leaves, the monoid one cycle generates, or the gauge a combination rule must ignore.

Study each object over a base, so that the base can be changed. What survives the change is what the object really is.

after Grothendieck
In one minute
  • QLoRA is LoRA anchored at the quantised weight \(\kappa\theta_0\), so it misses \(\theta_0\) by the quantisation error \(e\), generically of full rank. LoftQ and QPiSSA are built to shrink this defect, by quantising and splitting in opposite orders. HF PEFT’s LoftQ initialiser ignores the LoRA scale \(s=\alpha/r\), so with its default single iteration it beats QLoRA in Frobenius norm only for \(0\lt s\lt2\).
  • With a fixed shape, merge-and-restart reaches exactly the monoid one cycle generates. ReLoRA reaches full rank after \(\lceil\min(m,n)/r\rceil\) cycles, while fixed linear shapes such as BitFit, (IA)\(^3\) or a fixed mask gain nothing.
  • Exactly orthogonal block OFT on a fixed partition never leaves \(W_0\cdot\prod SO(b)\), and re-merged BOFT reaches all rotations only at full butterfly depth. HF’s default Cayley–Neumann OFT is not orthogonal, and with small generators each merge can only shrink neuron norms and singular values.
  • A rule combining LoRA factors is well defined only if it ignores each adapter’s \(GL_r\) gauge. Task arithmetic on the products passes. LoraHub and HF’s factor-wise linear merge fail through cross terms, and become exact only when the adapters share a factor.

The point moves

Every method so far has been an arrow into one fixed point \((\Theta,\theta_0)\). In practice the point moves. Quantisation moves it once, ReLoRA at every merge, and task arithmetic, LoraHub, TIES and DARE build a new update from several adapters. A PEFT method is a formula that makes sense over any base point, so it is a family \(\theta_0\mapsto M(\theta_0)\), and this exposé relates its members. Four kinds of move occur.

moveexampleswhat to computewhere
a reparametrisation that keeps the functionpermuting hidden units; SliceGPT-style rotations of the residual streamnothing new; methods are carried along by compositionDef V.1(a), Obs II.15
a quantiserQLoRA, LoftQ, QPiSSA, QA-LoRAthe pointing defect, and the invariants that move with the baseProp V.2
merge and restartReLoRA, re-merged OFT and BOFT, re-HRA; GaLore period by periodthe monoid generated by one cycleThm V.3
combining adapterstask arithmetic, LoraHub, TIES, DARE, KnOTSwhether the rule is a function of the adapters, and which symmetries it commutes withProp V.4
Definition V.1 (base change and rebasing). (a) A function-preserving pointed reparametrisation \(\phi:(\Theta',f',\theta_0')\to(\Theta,f,\theta_0)\), with \(f\circ(\phi\times X)=f'\), induces \[\phi_!:\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta',\theta_0')\to\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0),\qquad(Q,q_0,\rho)\mapsto(Q,q_0,\phi\circ\rho),\] by postcomposition.

(b) A map \(\kappa:\Theta\to\Theta\) that does not preserve the function, such as a quantiser, moves the base point. Its pointing defect is \(e=\kappa\theta_0-\theta_0\).

(c) A rebasing scheme (merge-and-restart) picks \(\theta_{k+1}\in\mathrm{Im}\,M(\theta_k)\). Its reachable sets are \(R_K\), the points \(\theta_K\) it can reach in \(K\) cycles, and \(R_\infty=\bigcup_KR_K\).

Only part (a) is base change in the algebraic geometer’s sense, and it costs nothing; whether \(\phi_!\) turns LoRA into LoRA is the covariance question of Observation II.15. Parts (b) and (c) borrow the word, and the work lies there and in the combination rules. Figure 11 lets you run the restart computations of Theorem V.3 by hand.

More: what “base change” means here

In algebraic geometry a family over a base \(S\) pulls back along any map \(S'\to S\), and the statements worth having are the ones that survive the change. Part (a) of Definition V.1 is the standard companion of pullback on slice categories, composition with a map of bases. A quantiser changes the network’s function, so there is no commuting square to transport along, and we compute the defect it leaves instead. A rebasing scheme is an iteration, and we compute its reachable set. “Gauge” keeps the sense of Exposé III, the symmetry group of a parametrisation; no connection is involved.

Quantisation and splitting

For an additive method (Def I.2) whose starting point does not depend on the base, such as zero-init LoRA, quantising the base only translates the image, so QLoRA is LoRA with its vertex at \(\kappa\theta_0\). A quantisation error is spread over all singular directions, so \(e\) is generically of full rank and the image misses \(\theta_0\). Methods whose starting point is computed from the base, such as PiSSA, CorDA, OLoRA, LoftQ and DoRA, meet two operations that do not commute, and the order fixes the defect (tabulated in Exposé II.A).

2026SRR (Cho et al., 2026) joins the first two orders of Proposition V.2(b) by splitting the rank budget. It puts \(\mathrm{SVD}_k(W)\) into the adapter, quantises \(W_{\rm res}=W-\mathrm{SVD}_k(W)\) and fits the quantisation error with the other \(r-k\) ranks, so its defect is \(e_k\) minus its best rank-\((r-k)\) approximation, where \(e_k=\kappa(W_{\rm res})-W_{\rm res}\), and \(\mathrm{SVD}_k\) and best approximations are taken in its activation-scaled norm. With that scaling set to the identity, \(k=r\) gives the split-first defect of QPiSSA and \(k=0\) that of one-step LoftQ at \(s=1\). SRR picks \(k\) per matrix from a closed-form proxy.
Proposition V.2 (quantisation and splitting). Write \(W\) for the full-precision weight, \(\kappa\) for the quantiser, \(e=\kappa W-W\), \(\delta_0=\delta(q_0)\), and \(s\) for the LoRA scale \(\alpha/r\) (\(\alpha/\sqrt r\) under rsLoRA).

(a) Additive families commute with translation, \(M(\theta+v)=\tau_v\circ M(\theta)\). Hence QLoRA is LoRA pointed at \(\kappa\theta_0\), with defect \(e\), and \(\theta_0\notin\mathrm{Im}\,\mathrm{LoRA}_r(\kappa\theta_0)\) unless \(\operatorname{rank}e\le r\).

(b) For splitting families derived from an SVD or from data, three orders occur in practice.

  • Split, then quantise the residual (QPiSSA; HF’s PiSSA and CorDA workflow; LoftQ’s quantisation half-steps for \(t\ge2\)). The defect is \(\kappa(W_{\rm res})-W_{\rm res}\).
  • Quantise, then fit the error (one-step LoftQ, HF’s default loftq_iter=1; HF replace_lora_weights_loftq). HF PEFT does not divide by \(s\), so \(\delta_0=-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\), and the defect \(e-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\) has squared norm \(\|e-\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\|^2+(1-s)^2\|\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\|^2\). At \(s=1\), HF’s bare default \(r=\alpha=8\), this is \(e\) minus its best rank-\(r\) approximation; at \(s=2\) its norm is \(\|e\|\).
  • Quantise, split, re-quantise (HF olora_init on bitsandbytes weights). The defect is \(e+\kappa(r')-r'\), with \(r'=\kappa W-\delta_0\).

Quantise-then-split has defect exactly \(e\) iff the frozen residual equals \(\kappa W-\delta_0\), for instance \(\kappa W\) minus a frozen copy of \(\delta_0\). Action families with a base-independent pointing (OFT at \(R=I\), BOFT at \(R=I\) with unit scale, HiRA at \(B=0\)) carry the defect \(e\). DoRA’s magnitude gives defect \(e\) when initialised from \(\kappa\theta_0\), and \(\mathrm{diag}\big(\|w_{0,i}\|/\|(\kappa\theta_0)_i\|\big)\,\kappa\theta_0-\theta_0\) when initialised from \(\theta_0\). For action families the absolute invariants move too. NF4’s exact zero code enlarges the zero set HiRA must preserve, and exactly orthogonal OFT preserves the Gram matrix of \(\kappa\theta_0\) rather than that of \(\theta_0\).

(c) LoftQ alternates an exact rank-\(r\) SVD step with round-to-nearest quantisation whose blockwise scales are recomputed from its input. The second step is not an exact projection, so the objective \(\|\theta_0-Q-\delta(q)\|\) need not decrease monotonically. At \(s=1\) the output is an approximate section, a choice \((Q,q_0)\) with reduced \(\|\rho(q_0)-\theta_0\|\). In general, with \(Q_T\) and the low-rank fit \(L_TR_T\) after \(T\) iterations, the deployed defect is \((Q_T+L_TR_T-W)+(s-1)L_TR_T\).

(d) For a generic high-precision adapter \(\Delta\), fine-tune-then-quantise and quantise-then-fine-tune do not commute, since \(\kappa\theta_0+\Delta\) leaves the quantisation grid. For a uniform grid with fixed scales the obstruction is the off-grid update; NF4, and absmax grids with recomputed scales, are not closed under addition at all. Type-preserving merges, such as HF PEFT’s dequantise–add–requantise for bitsandbytes layers, stay in the quantised type but are lossy. Under affine group quantisation QA-LoRA is the structured exception. Its group-constant adapter merges into the zero-points, so the merged model stays INT-\(k\).

(expand)

(a) is the definition of additivity. With \(\rho=\theta_0+\delta\) and \(\delta\) independent of \(\theta_0\), moving the base by \(v\) translates every value of \(\rho\) by \(v\). For the last clause, \(\kappa\theta_0+\delta(q)=\theta_0\) needs \(\delta(q)=-e\), and LoRA’s \(\delta(q)\) has rank at most \(r\).

(b) Compute \(\rho(q_0)-W\) in each pipeline. Splitting first gives \(\kappa(W_{\rm res})+\delta_0-W\) with \(W_{\rm res}=W-\delta_0\) (for LoftQ, \(W_{\rm res}=W-\delta(q_{t-1})\)). Fitting the error gives \(\kappa W-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)-W=(e-\mathrm{SVD}_r(e))+(1-s)\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\), and the two terms are orthogonal in the Frobenius inner product. Re-quantising gives \(\kappa(r')+\delta_0-W=(\kappa(r')-r')+(\kappa W-W)\). For DoRA at \(B=0\), \(\rho(q_0)=\mathrm{diag}\big(\mu_0/\|(\kappa\theta_0)_i\|\big)\,\kappa\theta_0\), where \(\mu_0\) is the vector of row norms of whichever base initialised the magnitude.

(c) holds by construction, with deployed weight \(Q_T+s\,L_TR_T\); the failure of monotonicity is observed numerically. (d) In general \(\kappa(\theta_0+\Delta)\ne\kappa\theta_0+\Delta\). On a quantisation group with codes \(c\), scale \(a\) and zero-point \(\beta\), a group-constant \(\Delta\) gives \(ac+\beta+\Delta=ac+(\beta+\Delta)\), so it is absorbed into the zero-point.

Num On one \(256\times256\) NF4 example with \(r=8\), \(s=1\) and \(\|e\|=5.65\), QPiSSA’s defect is \(1.45\), one-step LoftQ’s is \(5.22\), and the LoftQ objective rises in \(27\) of \(60\) half-steps. QPiSSA’s large advantage there relies on a weight with a dominant low-rank part; on a Gaussian weight all three defects are close to \(\|e\|\).

∎
More: LoftQ as deployed

The official LoftQ checkpoints were made with \(r=64\), \(\alpha=16\) and five iterations, so \(s=1/4\). In a referee’s simulation of that configuration on a Gaussian \(512\times512\) NF4 weight, the attained residual was \(0.67\|e\|\) and the deployed defect \(0.96\|e\|\). For \(T\ge2\), HF’s internal residual also omits \(s\), so the objective in (c) is not the defect HF starts from. With a callback, replace_lora_weights_loftq can roll a module back to the zero initialisation, and that module’s defect is then \(e\).

CreditTraining LoRA over a 4-bit NF4 base is due to Dettmers et al., 2023 (QLoRA), building on the blockwise absmax quantisation of Dettmers et al., 2021. Fitting the adapter to the quantisation error is LoftQ (Li et al., 2023), and the split-first variant is QPiSSA in Meng et al., 2024. The data-aware and QR splits are CorDA and OLoRA. The group-pooled adapter that merges into the zero-points is QA-LoRA (Xu et al., 2023). The pipelines are as implemented in HF PEFT.

Over a quantised base, exactly orthogonal OFT keeps the neuron geometry of \(\kappa\theta_0\) (Cor II.6) and HiRA keeps the zeros NF4 created (Prop II.9(e)). QA-LoRA makes merging commute with quantisation by living inside the quantiser’s own degrees of freedom.

Restarting: what merge-and-restart can reach

Merge the adapter, restart at the merged point, repeat. Each cycle adds one more copy of an additive shape or applies one more group element, so the long-run reach is whatever one cycle generates. A rank-\(r\) cone generates every matrix, and a subspace, subgroup or submonoid generates only itself. Block-orthogonal factors generate the rotations of each connected piece of their block hypergraph, because the bracket of infinitesimal rotations in the planes \((a,c)\) and \((c,b)\) is the infinitesimal rotation in the plane \((a,b)\).

Theorem V.3 (rebasing closure). (a) Additive methods with a fixed shape \(C\ni0\), with frames, masks and compression operators frozen at \(\theta_0\) across cycles. Then \(R_K=\theta_0+C^{+K}\), the \(K\)-fold Minkowski sum, and \(R_\infty=\theta_0+\mathrm{Mon}(C)\), the additive monoid generated by \(C\). If \(C\) is closed under real scalings, \(R_\infty=\theta_0+\mathrm{span}\,C\).

(b) Action methods, where one cycle applies an element of a set \(S\) with \(1\in S\subseteq G\); for scaling methods, \(S\subseteq\mathrm{End}(\Theta)\) is a subset containing the identity. Then \(R_K=S^K\theta_0\), and \(R_\infty\) is the orbit of the monoid generated by \(S\). Suppose \(S\) lies in the subgroup generated by connected Lie subgroups \(H_1,\dots,H_p\) and contains a neighbourhood of \(1\) in each. Then the monoid generated by \(S\) is the connected Lie subgroup \(H\) with \(\mathrm{Lie}(H)=\mathrm{Lie}\langle\mathfrak h_1,\dots,\mathfrak h_p\rangle\).

(c) Block-orthogonal factors. Let the factors have coordinate blocks \(I_j\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}\) (block OFT, BOFT factors, Givens planes). Then \[\mathrm{Lie}\langle\mathfrak{so}(I_j)\rangle_j=\bigoplus_C\mathfrak{so}(C),\] summed over the connected components \(C\) of the hypergraph \((\{1,\dots,n\},\{I_j\})\).

Corollaries (of Theorem V.3). The following hold for the named methods under rebasing.
  1. ReLoRA. \(R_K=W_0+\mathcal M_{\le\min(Kr,m,n)}\). It reaches full fine-tuning iff \(K\ge\lceil\min(m,n)/r\rceil\).
  2. Idempotent methods gain nothing from rebasing. Their shape is exactly a subspace, subgroup or submonoid independent of the base, with frames, masks and compression operators frozen across cycles. Examples are LoRA-FA with a fixed \(A\), LoRA-XS with frozen frames, MoRA with fixed operators, FourierFT with fixed indices, WaveFT, C3A, MiSS in its default variant, SHiRA, BitFit, FISH with a fixed mask, LN-tuning, (IA)\(^3\), SSF and RoAd. Block OFT with a fixed partition and an exact Cayley map gains only the measure-zero set of block rotations with an eigenvalue \(-1\), so \(R_\infty=W_0\cdot\prod SO(b)=\overline{R_1}\); COFT (an \(\varepsilon\)-ball) with an exact Cayley map gains up to that set. Methods that recompute frames or masks from the merged weight (an SVD-recomputing LoRA-XS, ReMoRA’s alternating operators, resampled indices) do gain.
  3. VeRA. \(\mathrm{VeRA}^\infty=\text{LoRA-FA}(A)\) whenever the frozen \(B\) has no zero entries.
  4. OFT family. Block OFT with an exact Cayley map is stuck in \(W_0\cdot\prod SO(b)\). HF’s default Cayley–Neumann factor is \(R=C(Q)(I-Q^4)\), with \(C\) the exact Cayley map, so \(R^\top R=(I-Q^4)^2\) and it is not orthogonal. Its singular values are \(\lvert1-\mu_k^4\rvert\), where \(\pm i\mu_k\) are the eigenvalues of \(Q\). When every \(\mu_k\le2^{1/4}\), the regime the truncated series is meant for, each factor is a contraction, so each merge can only shrink \(K_{\rm out}\) in the Loewner order, and no singular value grows. Iterated BOFT (exact Cayley in HF) with \(m'\) butterfly factors of block size \(b\) reaches \[\mathrm{diag}(\mathbb R^m)\cdot W_0\cdot P\Big(\prod SO\big(b\,2^{m'-1}\big)\Big)P^\top,\] because the factor hypergraph has components of size \(b\,2^{m'-1}\). For injective \(W_0\) this is \(\mathrm{diag}(\mathbb R^m)\cdot W_0\cdot SO(n)\) iff \(b\,2^{m'-1}=n\), the full-depth butterfly \(m'=1+\log_2(n/b)\). HF’s default boft_n_butterfly_factor=1 is block OFT with an output scale. A single full-depth BOFT has dimension at most \(m'\tfrac nb\binom b2\), below \(\dim SO(n)\) when \(m'(b-1)\lt n-1\).
  5. Re-HRA (HF apply_GS=False), with \(W_0\) injective and \(r\) even. \(R_K=(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le Kr})\cap W_0\cdot SO(n)\), so rebasing commutes with the meet of Theorem II.7. This multiplicative twin of ReLoRA reaches \(W_0\cdot SO(n)\) in at most \(\lceil n/r\rceil\) cycles.
  6. GaLore (with a gradient-SVD or a random projector), LISA and MeZO, under the optimizer hypotheses of Prop IV.2, are rebasing schemes of linear methods whose shape moves. As a set, over the sequences of shapes they can choose, they reach all of \(\Theta\), while a fixed linear shape \(L\) reaches only \(\theta_0+L\). Low rank is the per-period obstruction only for GaLore; LISA and MeZO already make full-rank per-matrix updates. Flora as published takes full-space steps with a compressed momentum, so it has no per-period low-rank shape.
  7. Merging never rescues an exactly orthogonal method from its absolute invariants. Every exactly orthogonal input-side rebasing preserves \(K_{\rm out}\) and the spectrum (Cor II.6); output-side ones preserve \(K_{\rm in}\) and the spectrum.
(expand)

(a) follows by induction on \(K\). If \(C=\mathbb RC\), the monoid generated by \(C\) contains \(-C\) and is closed under scaling, so it is the span.

(b) Each cycle applies one element of \(S\), so \(R_K=S^K\theta_0\), and since \(1\in S\) the sets increase with \(K\). Any neighbourhood of \(1\) contains a symmetric one, and a symmetric neighbourhood of \(1\) in a connected group generates that group as a monoid. The subgroup generated by connected Lie subgroups is the connected Lie subgroup with the generated Lie algebra (Yamabe).

(c) Write \(L_{ab}=E_{ab}-E_{ba}\). Then \([L_{ac},L_{cb}]=L_{ab}\) for distinct \(a,b,c\). Overlapping hyperedges therefore generate \(\mathfrak{so}\) of their union, and \(\bigoplus_C\mathfrak{so}(C)\) is a subalgebra that contains every generator.

The corollaries.

  • (1) Rank is subadditive, and an SVD splits into blocks of rank at most \(r\).
  • (2) The exact Cayley image is dense and open in \(SO(b)\), and every element of \(SO(b)\) is a product of two elements of it.
  • (3) The entries of \(\Lambda_bB\Lambda_d\) are \(b_iB_{ij}d_j\). Their span over \((b,d)\) is all of \(\mathbb R^{m\times r}\) when every \(B_{ij}\ne0\) (take \(b=e_i\), \(d=e_j\)).
  • (4) Apply (c) to the butterfly blocks. For the Cayley–Neumann factors, \((I-Q)(I+Q+Q^2+Q^3)=I-Q^4\) gives \(R=C(Q)(I-Q^4)\), where \(C(Q)\) is orthogonal and commutes with the symmetric matrix \(Q^4\). Hence the singular values of \(R\) are \(\lvert1-\mu_k^4\rvert\), at most \(1\) when every \(\mu_k\le2^{1/4}\), and \(K_{\rm out}(WR^\top)=W(I-Q^4)^2W^\top\preceq WW^\top\). Also \(\det R=\prod_k(1-\mu_k^4)^2\ge0\), and \(R\) is singular when some \(\mu_k=1\). The invertible factors generate \(\prod GL^+(b)\) as a group (\(\prod CO^+(2)\) when \(b=2\)): \(R(Q)R(-Q)=(I-Q^4)^2\) gives every positive scaling that is constant on the rotation planes of some skew \(Q\), \(R(Q)\lvert I-Q^4\rvert^{-1}=C(Q)\) for small \(Q\) gives \(SO(b)\), and \(GL^+(b)=SO(b)\cdot D^+\cdot SO(b)\); for \(b=2\) the scalings are scalar. This identifies the group only. Re-merging reaches the monoid, which can be smaller, as the contraction bound shows.
  • (5) “\(\subseteq\)” holds because \(R_1R_2-I=R_1(R_2-I)+(R_1-I)\), so ranks add. For “\(\supseteq\)”, an \(R\in SO(n)\) with \(k=\operatorname{rank}(R-I)\le Kr\) is a product of exactly \(k\) reflections, with \(k\) even (Cartan–Dieudonné in Scherk’s form). Split them into \(K\) consecutive groups of even size at most \(r\), pad each group with pairs \(H_uH_u=I\), and observe that each group is an \(\mathrm{HRA}_r\) element. Injectivity of \(W_0\) gives \(\operatorname{rank}W_0(R-I)=\operatorname{rank}(R-I)\).
  • (6) A fixed linear shape \(L\) is its own additive monoid, by corollary 2.
  • The remaining corollaries are immediate.

Num For the butterfly hypergraph at \(n=64\), \(b=4\), the components have sizes \(4,8,16,32,64\) for \(m'=1,\dots,5\), so it is connected only at \(m'=5\); the referees found the same with HF’s own permutation code. With skew entries of size about \(0.05\), \(K_{\rm out}\) moves by \(0.2\%\), \(1.3\%\), \(6\%\) and \(22\%\) after \(1\), \(10\), \(50\) and \(200\) default-OFT merges, and every one of these moves is a Loewner-order shrinkage.

∎

Cayley parametrisations of orthogonal layers are studied in Lezcano-Casado and Martínez-Rubio, 2019. ReLoRA is Observation I.7 unrolled in time, since a sum of \(K\) rank-\(r\) LoRAs is one LoRA of rank \(Kr\). A fixed additive shape gains from restarts only when it is not closed under addition, as LoRA’s cone and VeRA’s shape are not.

2026POET-X (Qiu et al., 2026), a pretraining method whose \(W_0\) is a random initialisation, changes the partition between cycles, on both sides of the weight. Each round trains a block-diagonal factor conjugated by a random permutation on each side, merges both into the weight and redraws the permutations. With exact Cayley blocks every merged weight lies in \(SO(m)\,W_0\,SO(n)\), so it keeps the singular values of \(W_0\) (Thm II.5), and by Thm V.3(b) and (c) blocks that together connect all coordinates of a side generate all of \(SO\) on that side. Its blocks are in fact \((I+Q)(I+Q+Q^2+Q^3)\), the Cayley–Neumann factor of corollary 4, so while every \(\mu_k\le2^{1/4}\) no merge raises a singular value, and the spectrum can only drift down.
Example (one projection of a 7B model). Take a \(4096\times4096\) projection. ReLoRA with \(r=16\) reaches full fine-tuning after \(256\) cycles and not before. For injective \(W_0\), re-HRA with \(r=8\) reaches \(W_0\cdot SO(4096)\) within \(512\) cycles. BOFT with \(b=4\) needs \(m'=11\) butterfly factors before re-merging reaches that orbit up to output scaling; at HF’s default of one factor it stays in \(W_0\cdot\prod SO(4)\) up to the scale. A single 11-factor BOFT has dimension at most \(11\cdot1024\cdot6=67{,}584\), against \(\dim SO(4096)=8{,}386{,}560\).

Prediction Re-merged exact-Cayley block OFT and COFT on a fixed partition never leave \(W_0\cdot\prod SO(b)\); going further needs partitions that change between cycles or full-depth butterflies. These are statements about reach, and whether extra reach improves a trained model is the experiment.

Combining: rules that must ignore the gauge

A LoRA module ships as factors \((B,A)\), the model sees only \(BA\), and \((Bg^{-1},gA)\) gives the same product for every \(g\in GL_r\). A combination rule should return the same model for every choice of factors, or two people with the same adapters can merge them into different models. Task arithmetic adds the products and passes. LoraHub adds the \(B\)’s, adds the \(A\)’s and multiplies, and fails, because its cross terms \(B_iA_l\) see a rescaling \((cB,A/c)\) of one module. Tab B of Figure 9 shows the LoraHub merge moving under that rescaling while task arithmetic stays fixed.

Proposition V.4 (combination rules: gauge and naturality). (a) A rule \(\Phi\big((B_i,A_i)_i\big)\) for combining LoRA factors descends to a function of the adapters \((B_iA_i)_i\) iff it is constant on the fibres of \((B_i,A_i)\mapsto(B_iA_i)\). On the full-rank locus this is equivalent to \(\prod_iGL_r\)-invariance. It is also equivalent for \(\Phi\) continuous on the whole factor space; this clause rests on the unique closed orbit in each fibre, which lies in the closure of every orbit of the fibre.
  • Task arithmetic \(\sum_iw_is_iB_iA_i\) (HF’s cat), with \(s_i\) the LoRA scale \(\alpha_i/r_i\), or \(\alpha_i/\sqrt{r_i}\) under rsLoRA, is invariant and lands in \(\mathrm{LoRA}_{\sum_ir_i}\).
  • LoraHub’s factor-wise rule \(\big(\sum_iw_iB_i\big)\big(\sum_jw_jA_j\big)\) is not invariant. Replacing \((B_2,A_2)\) by \((cB_2,A_2/c)\) changes it by \[\sum_{l\ne2}w_lw_2\big[(c-1)B_2A_l+(c^{-1}-1)B_lA_2\big].\] HF’s add_weighted_adapter(..., combination_type="linear") is a factor-wise rule of the same type. HF’s non-SVD ties, dare_* and magnitude_prune variants also act on factors; the *_svd variants act on full updates.
  • Poly and MHR train their skill inventories jointly through the product-of-sums map. Its evident symmetry, one common \(g\in GL_r\) for every skill, leaves every combined adapter unchanged, so their cross terms are a design choice of a jointly trained parametrisation.

(b) Well-definedness versus exactness. A joint gauge fixing makes a factor-wise rule well defined. Exactness, agreement with task arithmetic for all weights, needs degree one in \(w\), which for product-of-sums rules with injective factors means a shared factor. A frozen shared factor (FFA-LoRA; LoRA-FA) gives \(\big(\sum_iw_iB_i\big)A=\sum_iw_iB_iA\). LoraHub with a shared \(A\) returns \(\big(\sum_jw_j\big)\sum_iw_iB_iA\), exact up to that unnormalised factor. A per-module SVD still leaves a \((\mathbb Z/2)^r\) of sign flips per module, plus rotations within repeated singular values, and these change the cross terms. A joint re-factorisation with a shared factor, such as KnOTS’s shared \(U\Sigma\), does not. FedAvg-LoRA clients start each round from the same broadcast \((B,A)\), so their failure, \[\tfrac12(B_1+B_2)\cdot\tfrac12(A_1+A_2)\ne\tfrac12(B_1A_1+B_2A_2),\] is bilinearity, and it persists in a fully fixed gauge.

(c) Naturality. On updates with \(N\) coordinates, write \(T_N\), \(B_N\) and \(\mathrm{Mon}_N\) for the invertible diagonal, signed permutation and monomial matrices. Task arithmetic is \(GL(\Theta)\)-equivariant. DARE satisfies \(D(m\odot\tau)=m\odot(D\tau)\) for every invertible diagonal \(D\) and the same mask \(m\), so it is exactly \(T_N\)-equivariant per mask, sign flips included, and \(\mathrm{Mon}_N\)-equivariant in distribution. TIES with global trimming and sign election by total mass (the paper’s rule and HF’s default) is equivariant under \(\mathbb R^\times\cdot B_N\), homotheties times signed permutations, away from ties in magnitude. None of these sparsifying rules is \(O(N)\)-equivariant. Applied to factors and summed across adapters, as in HF’s non-SVD variants, they are gauge-dependent through the cross terms of (a). Factor-wise DARE is invariant under one common \(g\in\mathrm{Mon}_r\) applied to every adapter, exactly for the torus with the same masks and in law for permutations, while a gauge on a single adapter changes its cross terms.

(expand)

(a) On the full-rank locus, the fibres of \((B_i,A_i)\mapsto(B_iA_i)\) are \(\prod GL_r\)-orbits (Thm II.2). Off it, invariance alone is not enough. The projector onto \(\mathrm{row}\,A\) is \(GL_r\)-invariant on \(\{\operatorname{rank}A=r\}\), yet it differs at \((0,A)\) and \((0,A')\), which have the same product. For a continuous \(\Phi\), invariance makes \(\Phi\) constant on orbit closures, and the unique closed orbit of a fibre lies in the closure of every orbit of that fibre. The LoraHub formula follows by expanding the product: the diagonal term \(w_2^2B_2A_2\) is unchanged, and each cross term \(w_lw_2B_2A_l\) gains a factor \(c\) while \(w_lw_2B_lA_2\) gains \(c^{-1}\). For Poly and MHR, a common \(g\) cancels inside every product of sums. Whether it is the only continuous symmetry depends on the routing: with at least as many skills as tasks, or with the routing counted as a parameter, there are further ones, such as relabelling the skills.

(b) Flipping column \(j\) of \(B_i\) together with row \(j\) of \(A_i\) preserves the SVD form and changes \(B_iA_l\) for \(l\ne i\). A product of two weighted sums is quadratic in \(w\), unless one factor is shared and left unweighted.

(c) Masks and the rescaling by \(1/(1-p)\) commute with diagonal maps, and an i.i.d. mask is permutation-invariant in law. Trimming by magnitude, electing signs by total mass and taking disjoint means commute with signed coordinate permutations and with \(c\cdot I\) for \(c\ne0\). A rotation changes the magnitudes of individual coordinates. For the factor clause, the all-ones mask has positive probability, and on that event factor-wise DARE is the product of sums \(\big(\sum_iw_iB_i\big)\big(\sum_jw_jA_j\big)/(1-p)^2\), which a gauge \((cB_2,A_2/c)\) on one adapter changes by the formula of (a). A common diagonal gauge commutes with the masks and cancels in every product.

Num DARE with a random non-unit diagonal \(D\) is equivariant to \(2\times10^{-15}\) per mask. TIES is homogeneous to \(9\times10^{-16}\) and fails by \(5.6\) under a generic positive diagonal. The LoraHub change formula matches with three modules to \(2\times10^{-15}\). On the factors of two adapters, a diagonal gauge on one of them moves factor-wise DARE by an amount of order one, while the same gauge shared by both leaves it fixed to \(10^{-15}\).

∎
More: TIES variants and HF options

With per-tensor trimming, TIES is equivariant instead under \(\prod_b\big(\mathbb R^\times\cdot B_{N_b}\big)\), a signed permutation and a scalar per block, together with permutations of equal-size blocks. DARE followed by full TIES is \(\mathbb R^\times\cdot B_N\)-equivariant in distribution. HF’s dare_ties has no magnitude trim, so with total-mass election it is exactly \(T_N\)-equivariant per mask, like DARE. With HF’s majority_sign_method="frequency", sign counts are integers, ties are common and are broken towards \(+1\), so sign flips and negative homotheties fail for ties and dare_ties alike. HF’s linear rule weights \(A_i\) by \(\mathrm{sign}(w_i)\sqrt{\lvert w_is_i\rvert}\) and \(B_i\) by \(\sqrt{\lvert w_is_i\rvert}\), so even on adapters that share a frozen \(A\) it is not exact up to a global factor; the relative weights become \(\sqrt{\lvert w_is_i\rvert}\).

CreditThe rules studied here are task arithmetic (Ilharco et al., 2022), LoraHub (Huang et al., 2023), TIES (Yadav et al., 2023), DARE (Yu et al., 2023) and KnOTS (Stoica et al., 2024), whose joint SVD is the shared frame of (b). Jointly trained skill inventories are Poly (Ponti et al., 2023) and MHR (Caccia et al., 2022). Federated averaging of LoRA factors is used in FedIT (Zhang et al., 2023), and the frozen shared factor that removes its bilinearity is FFA-LoRA (Sun et al., 2024).
CreditThat functions of LoRA weights should respect their \(GL_r\) symmetry is the starting point of the \(GL\)-equivariant networks of Putterman et al., 2024; for full models, hidden-unit permutations play that role in Git Re-Basin (Ainsworth et al., 2022). The unique closed orbit in each fibre is classical invariant theory. Kempf and Ness (1979) characterise closed orbits by norm-minimising points, here the balanced factorisations of Prop III.7, and Richardson and Slodowy (1990) treat real groups. For optimizers, the same question leads to Zhang and Pilanci, 2024 and Yen et al., 2024; see Theorem III.12.

Post-hoc factor merging of independently trained modules, as in LoraHub and HF’s linear, depends on how each module happened to be factorised, which nobody controls after training. Federated LoRA fails through bilinearity instead. Combine LoRAs on products, or only after a joint re-factorisation that produces a shared factor.

Prediction LoraHub behaves like update arithmetic, up to the factor \(\sum_jw_j\), when its modules share the \(A\)-seed and \(A\) stays close to its initialisation. That regime is empirical and favoured by a large ratio \(\eta_B/\eta_A\), as in LoRA+. LoraHub degrades when the modules come from independent seeds.

What base change leaves for the network

Every move here stayed inside weight space, where merging into the base is the canonical arrow to the terminal object of Observation I.4 and always exists. Adapters, prefixes and ReFT change the network itself, and for them merging is a lifting problem whose obstructions the activation function decides. That is Exposé VI.

Exposé VI

Extensions and merging

Adapters, prompts and ReFT enlarge the network before they train, so merging them back is a lifting problem. Seven local rewrite rules give sufficient conditions for a merge and name the side condition that fails when they stop.

When a lift fails, look for the place where it fails, and give the obstruction a name.

after Grothendieck
In one minute
  • Adapters, prefixes, prompts and ReFT add boxes to the network. Merging one back means finding a weight of the original network that computes the trained function.
  • Seven rewrite rules slide an edit into a weight that absorbs it. They certify a merge when the edit acts alike at every position, the absorbing weights are unshared, shifts have bias slots and, under RoPE, scales are constant on rotary pairs. A stuck rewrite shows only a box-local obstruction.
  • (IA)³’s feed-forward scale always folds into \(W_{\rm down}\). It folds into \(W_{\rm up}\) under SwiGLU for every scale and under ReLU for nonnegative ones, and under GELU only entries \(0\) and \(1\) fold.
  • A prefix is a gated parallel adapter whose gate depends on the input, so for generic frozen weights it cannot be folded into the head’s own weights. Per hidden vector LoReFT is LoRA on an inserted identity box, and since it acts at selected positions no merge is known.

The weight that absorbs the edit

(IA)³ rescales the hidden units of a feed-forward block by a learned vector \(\ell\), which one would like to fold into a frozen weight after training. The weight that reads the hidden units, \(W_{\rm down}\), absorbs \(\ell\) under every activation. The weight that produces them, \(W_{\rm up}\), absorbs it under SwiGLU for every \(\ell\), under ReLU when no entry is negative, and under GELU only for entries \(0\) or \(1\).

The activation belongs to the network, which a weight-space method never sees (Remark I.3). Adapters, prefixes, prompts and ReFT add boxes the pretrained network lacked, and whether they can be removed depends on their surroundings. Figure 12 runs the rules of this exposé on one block.

Extensions, and merging as a lift

An adapter \(y\mapsto y+U\sigma(Dy)\) adds weights \((U,D)\), and at \(U=0\) the network computes the old function. A prefix adds key–value pairs to every softmax, and ReFT edits hidden states at chosen positions. Each time \(\Theta\) grows to \(\Theta\times\Theta'\) and \(f\) to a larger \(\tilde f\). A prefix always takes softmax mass, and on a head with \(W_oW_v\ne0\) no prefix leaves the output unchanged, so prefixes have no neutral setting there (Thm VI.5(f)).

Definition VI.1 (extensions; mergeability).
  • An extension is a function-preserving pointed embedding \(\iota:(\Theta,f,\theta_0)\to(\Theta\times\Theta',\tilde f,(\theta_0,\theta_0'))\), \(\iota(\theta)=(\theta,\theta_0')\), with \(\tilde f(\theta,\theta_0',-)=f(\theta,-)\). The point \(\theta_0'\) is the neutral point (“identity at initialisation”).
  • An unpointed extension has no neutral point.
  • A method on an extension is mergeable if there is \(\mu:Q\to\Theta\) with \(\Phi_{\tilde f}\circ\rho=\Phi_f\circ\mu\), a lift along the realisation.
  • It is box-locally mergeable if the modified box function stays in the box’s class: linear maps for a linear box, affine maps when the box has a bias slot. Theorem VI.3 gives sufficient conditions under which this yields a merge into the base network.

A merge is an arrow \(\mu\) that makes the square of realisations commute.

\[\begin{array}{ccc} Q & \hphantom{.}\xrightarrow{\qquad\mu\qquad}\hphantom{.} & \Theta\\[2pt] \llap{\scriptstyle\rho\ }\Big\downarrow & & \Big\downarrow\rlap{\scriptstyle\ \Phi_f}\\[2pt] \Theta\times\Theta' & \hphantom{.}\xrightarrow[\qquad\Phi_{\tilde f}\qquad]{}\hphantom{.} & C^\infty(X,Y) \end{array}\]

A weight-space method merges by \(\mu=\rho\), the canonical arrow to full fine-tuning (Obs I.4(a)), so the real questions live on extensions, in the slice of \(\mathbf{Para}\) over the pretrained model (Remark I.3). Box-local mergeability can be read off formulas, and the atlas of Exposé VIII records it as a merge type:

  • M1 the edit merges into its own box;
  • M1→ the rules move it into a neighbouring box, under side conditions;
  • Mq an exact merge exists but leaves the quantised type;
  • M∞ no merge: the rules are blocked box-locally, or the edit lies outside them.

“Zero inference latency” runs two questions together, whether the original architecture can compute the trained function at all and whether a local edit finds the weight. A zero up-projection, or the zero gate of LLaMA-Adapter, makes training start at the pretrained function, and by the function-level form of Lemma II.4 only \(U\), or the gate, moves at first.

CreditAdapters are Houlsby et al., 2019; prefix tuning is Li and Liang, 2021; prompt tuning is Lester et al., 2021; ReFT is Wu et al., 2024. He et al., 2022 first placed adapters, prefixes and LoRA in one frame, as modifications of hidden states. \(\mathbf{Para}\) is from Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras and Cruttwell et al., 2021.

The activation fixes the privileged basis

An edit moves through a network by sliding along wires, and a linear map slides through a box when it commutes with the box. Activations are the bottleneck. Permutations commute with every elementwise \(\sigma\), and for ReLU so do positive rescalings, since \(\mathrm{ReLU}(ct)=c\,\mathrm{ReLU}(t)\) for \(c\gt0\). The lemma says how little else does.

Lemma VI.2 (the activation fixes the privileged basis). Known Let \(\sigma:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) be an elementwise activation that is not affine, and let \(L\in GL_d\) commute with it as maps on \(\mathbb R^d\): \(L\,\sigma^{\times d}=\sigma^{\times d}L\).

(a) If \(\sigma\in C^2\), then \(L=PD\) is monomial.

(b) If \(\sigma\) is ReLU or leaky ReLU with slope \(\alpha\ne1\), then \(L=PD\) with \(D\) a positive diagonal.

(c) If moreover \(\sigma\) is real-analytic at \(0\) with \(\sigma(t)=\sum a_kt^k\), each diagonal entry \(c\) of \(D\) satisfies \(ca_k=c^ka_k\) for all \(k\). So \(c=1\) for GELU and SiLU, since \(\sigma''(0)\neq0\), and \(c=\pm1\) for tanh.

(expand)

(a) Fix row \(i\) of \(L\); its row vector \(\ell_i\) is nonzero because \(L\) is invertible. Coordinate \(i\) of \(L\,\sigma^{\times d}(x)\) is \(\sum_jL_{ij}\sigma(x_j)\), whose mixed partials \(\partial_j\partial_k\) vanish for \(j\ne k\). Coordinate \(i\) of \(\sigma^{\times d}(Lx)\) is \(\sigma(\ell_i\cdot x)\), with mixed partial \(L_{ij}L_{ik}\sigma''(\ell_i\cdot x)\). Since \(\sigma\) is not affine there is \(t_0\) with \(\sigma''(t_0)\ne0\); choose \(x\) with \(\ell_i\cdot x=t_0\). Then \(L_{ij}L_{ik}=0\) for \(j\ne k\), so each row has a single nonzero entry.

(b) ReLU is not \(C^2\), so argue by kinks instead. The left side of row \(i\) is non-differentiable exactly on \(\bigcup_{j:L_{ij}\ne0}\{x_j=0\}\), and the right side exactly on the hyperplane \(\{\ell_i\cdot x=0\}\). The two sets are equal, and a hyperplane equal to a finite union of coordinate hyperplanes is one of them. So row \(i\) has a single entry \(L_{ij}\), and \(L_{ij}\sigma(t)=\sigma(L_{ij}t)\) at \(t=1\) forces \(L_{ij}\gt0\). Leaky ReLU is identical.

(c) \(P\) commutes with \(\sigma^{\times d}\), so \(D\sigma=\sigma D\), that is \(c\,\sigma(t)=\sigma(ct)\) for each diagonal entry \(c\). Compare Taylor coefficients at \(0\). For GELU and SiLU \(a_2\ne0\), so \(c=c^2\) and \(c=1\). For tanh \(a_3\ne0\), so \(c=c^3\) and \(c=\pm1\). ∎

CreditThe lemma is classical. Sussmann (1992) and Chen, Lu and Hecht-Nielsen (1993) found the weight-space symmetries of tanh networks, and Godfrey et al. (NeurIPS 2022) computed the linear maps that commute with each common activation. Permutation symmetry is what Ainsworth et al., 2022 use to align whole models before merging them.

So rules R2 and R3 below exhaust the invertible linear maps that slide through \(\sigma\), permutations for GELU and SiLU and positive monomials for ReLU. In gated blocks such as SwiGLU every diagonal slides through the linear branch (rule R4), so their hidden-unit group is the full monomial group \(\mathrm{Mon}_{d_{\rm ff}}\).

More: symmetry groups by architecture

Ungated blocks (BERT, GPT-2, ViT) keep \(S_d\) under GELU and SiLU, \(B_d\) under tanh and the positive monomials under ReLU; gated blocks (LLaMA, Mistral, Qwen, Gemma) keep \(\mathrm{Mon}_{d_{\rm ff}}\). LayerNorm’s privileged basis comes from its diagonal gain; a gain-free RMSNorm is \(O(d)\)-equivariant, which SliceGPT and QuaRot (Ashkboos et al., 2024) use to rotate the residual stream (Obs II.15). Prediction X.3 turns the lemma into a permutation test.

Seven local rules

Draw a transformer block as a string diagram, with a copy node where a wire feeds several boxes and a product node in a gated block. An edit is a small box on a wire. To merge, slide it by equalities of maps until a trainable box absorbs it; where it gets stuck names the failing side condition. A positional rotation, a shared weight, a missing bias slot or a second reader of a wire can each block a slide, and all four occur in production models.

Analogy (what “string diagram” and “spider” mean here). The diagrams are string diagrams of a symmetric monoidal category, read literally. Copy is the cartesian diagonal \(x\mapsto(x,x)\), which commutes with every map, and the product node is \(\odot\). “Spider” is only a name for both; they fail the Frobenius law, which is never used.
CreditString diagrams for monoidal categories go back to Joyal and Street (1991); spiders are from Coecke, Pavlovic and Vicary, 2008.
Theorem VI.3 (the merge calculus). Known in part Work with activation-side diagrams in a symmetric monoidal category of continuous, piecewise-smooth maps with copy and discard spiders. A trainable box is a node whose weight ranges over all linear maps, or over all affine maps when it has a bias slot. R1 is closure of these classes; R2–R7 are equalities of maps.
rulestatementwhen it holds
R1 (fusion)composites and parallel sums of linear (affine) boxes are linear (affine)the operators involved are position-independent
R2 (permutation slide)\(P\sigma^{\times d}=\sigma^{\times d}P\)every elementwise \(\sigma\)
R3 (positive-diagonal slide)\(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\,\sigma^{\times d}=\sigma^{\times d}\,\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\) for all \(\ell\ge0\)iff \(\sigma\) is positively homogeneous (ReLU, leaky ReLU; not GELU, SiLU, tanh); such \(\sigma\) are piecewise linear
R4 (spider slide)\(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)(a\odot b)=(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)a)\odot b\)any \(\ell\)
R5 (cup slide)\(\langle Mq,k\rangle=\langle q,M^\top k\rangle\)always; under RoPE, \(\langle R_mMq,R_nk\rangle=\langle R_mq,R_nM^\top k\rangle\) for all occurring \(m,n\) iff \(M^\top\) commutes with every \(R_{n-m}\); for diagonal \(M\), iff \(\ell\) is constant on each rotary pair
R6 (norm absorption)\(W(\gamma\odot n(x)+\beta)\)
\(\quad=(W\,\mathrm{diag}\,\gamma)\,n(x)+W\beta\)		always; the shift \(W\beta\) needs a bias slot (RMSNorm has no \(\beta\))
R7 (copy slide)applying \(D\) and then copying equals copying and then applying \(D\) to every copyalways

Consequences.

(a) Affine edits. Let a method insert a position-uniform affine edit \(E_q\) on a wire \(w\) of the base network \(f\), for every \(q\in Q\). If every reader of \(w\) is a trainable box of \(f\), tied to no other weight, whose class is closed under precomposition with \(E_q\) (a shift needs a bias slot), the method merges by \(W_c\mapsto W_cE_q\) for all readers \(c\) at once. Dually, it merges into an untied trainable box that writes \(w\) whenever \(E_q\) composed with that box stays in its class. R1–R7 do not merge position-selective edits (LoReFT, Activated LoRA) or edits on a wire also read by a skip connection, a norm or the output.

(b) Diagonal methods. A diagonal method merges into a box reached by R1–R7 if the rules move every copy of \(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\) into trainable boxes for all \(\ell\) in the parameter domain (\(\ell\ge0\) when R3 is used); no position-dependent operator lies on the path (under RoPE, \(\ell\) is constant on rotary pairs); the target is unshared, or \(\ell\) is constant across the sharing group; and every shift has a bias slot. For generic frozen weights the RoPE condition is also necessary.

(c) Non-affine edits. For a linear or affine box, an edit whose modified box function is non-affine in the box input, for some parameter value, is not box-locally mergeable. For attention, see Thm VI.5(f).

(d) Type relativity. A generic high-precision update is off the quantisation grid, so an exact merge of a QLoRA or LoftQ update leaves the quantised type and a type-preserving merge is lossy. QA-LoRA’s group-constant adapters are the exception (Prop V.2(d)).

(expand)

R1 is closure of linear (affine) maps under composition and sums. R2, R4, R6 and R7 are identities; R7 is the naturality of the cartesian diagonal. For R3, \(\ell\sigma(t)=\sigma(\ell t)\) for all \(\ell\ge0\) and all \(t\) is positive homogeneity of degree one; such a \(\sigma\) is linear on each half-line, and linear if it is \(C^1\). For R5, \(\langle R_mMq,R_nk\rangle=\langle q,M^\top R_{n-m}k\rangle\) for every \(M\), since \(R_m^\top R_n=R_{n-m}\), while \(\langle R_mq,R_nM^\top k\rangle=\langle q,R_{n-m}M^\top k\rangle\). The two agree for all \(q,k\) iff \(M^\top\) commutes with \(R_{n-m}\), and a diagonal matrix commutes with a planar rotation through an angle that is not a multiple of \(\pi\) iff its two entries are equal.

(a) The closure and reader conditions put \(W_cE_q\) in each reader’s class, R7 distributes \(E_q\) to every reader, and position-uniformity lets one weight absorb the edit at every position. The dual case is R1 applied to \(E_q\circ W\). (b) is (a) applied at the box reached. For the converse under RoPE, suppose a query scaling \(D=\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\) merges into some \(W_K'\). Equal logits give \(W_Q^\top R_\Delta W_K'=W_Q^\top DR_\Delta W_K\) for every offset \(\Delta\) that occurs. If \(W_Q\) and \(W_K\) have full row rank, then \(W_K'=R_{-\Delta}DR_\Delta W_K\) for every such \(\Delta\). Causal attention includes \(\Delta=0\), so \(D\) commutes with every occurring \(R_\Delta\), and \(\ell\) is constant on each rotary pair whose angle at some occurring \(\Delta\) is not a multiple of \(\pi\). (c) is the definition of box-local mergeability. (d) is Prop V.2(d).

For the (IA)³ corollary under GELU, suppose \(\ell_i\,\mathrm{GELU}(w_i\cdot x)=\mathrm{GELU}(w_i'\cdot x)\) for all \(x\), with \(w_i\ne0\). On the hyperplane \(w_i\cdot x=0\) the right side vanishes, and GELU vanishes only at \(0\), so \(w_i'=cw_i\). Then \(\ell_i\,\mathrm{GELU}(t)=\mathrm{GELU}(ct)\) for all \(t\), and the Taylor coefficients at \(0\), \(\mathrm{GELU}(t)=t/2+t^2/\sqrt{2\pi}+\dots\), give \(c=\ell_i\) and \(\ell_i=\ell_i^2\). Under ReLU an entry \(\ell_i\lt0\) is impossible, since the left side is then \(\le0\) and somewhere negative, while the right side is \(\ge0\). ∎

Num Under RoPE, a query scaling slid onto \(W_K\) leaves a \(7.6\%\) least-squares residual unless \(\ell\) is pair-constant. Folding post-LN gains into \(W_1\) changes the output. Legal rewrites agree to \(10^{-15}\).

CreditThe individual merges are known: (IA)³ by Liu et al., 2022 (Sec. 3.3), SSF by re-parameterisation in Lian et al., 2022, and LoRA by linearity in Hu et al., 2021. Moving scales through ReLU, SwiGLU’s linear branch and norm gains, and the GELU obstruction, are in SmoothQuant (Xiao et al., 2023) and AWQ (Lin et al., 2024). New here is one calculus for PEFT with explicit side conditions. QA-LoRA is Xu et al., 2023; Activated LoRA is Greenewald et al., 2025.
ReviewResults VI.3–VI.5 were repaired after two rounds of review; see the referee record.

Under SwiGLU, R4 moves \(\ell\) onto the linear branch and R1 fuses it into \(W_{\rm up}\), for \(\ell\) of either sign:

\[\begin{aligned}\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\big(\mathrm{silu}(W_gx)\odot W_ux\big)&=\mathrm{silu}(W_gx)\odot\big(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\,W_ux\big)\\&=\mathrm{silu}(W_gx)\odot\big((\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\,W_u)\,x\big).\end{aligned}\]
Corollaries (scaling and reparametrisation methods). Weight-space reparametrisations merge, because their nonlinearities sit on the parameter side (Cayley in OFT, the norm in DoRA, \(u/\lVert u\rVert\) in HRA). HF PEFT merges an adapter on one side of a tied embedding and LM head unfaithfully. For the scaling methods:
  • (IA)³ on keys and values fuses into \(W_K,W_V\). HF applies the FFN vector to the input of \(W_{\rm down}\), where it fuses for every \(\sigma\). It also fuses into \(W_{\rm up}\) under SwiGLU (above) and under ReLU when \(\ell\ge0\), a restriction of the domain (IA)³ trains over; under GELU only entries \(\ell_i\in\{0,1\}\) pass (proof above).
  • A query scaling fuses into \(W_Q\). R5 moves it onto \(W_K\) without RoPE or with \(\ell\) constant on rotary pairs, and under GQA when \(\ell\) agrees across the group.
  • SSF fuses into the preceding box if it has a bias slot; bias-free LLaMA-type layers need a new bias. LN-tuning edits existing parameters. Folding \(\gamma,\beta\) forward works in pre-LN blocks, where the norm feeds only the next boxes; \(\beta\) needs a bias slot, an RMSNorm gain none. Never fold into a tied embedding or LM head.

Not box-locally mergeable: nonlinear Houlsby, Pfeiffer and parallel adapters, and AdapterFusion; routed mixtures of LoRA experts (MoLE, LoRAMoE, X-LoRA) unless the gate is constant; LST; prefix-type methods (Thm VI.5).

CreditNonlinear adapters: Houlsby et al., 2019, Pfeiffer et al., 2020 (with AdapterFusion), and the parallel adapter of He et al., 2022. Routed mixtures: MoLE, LoRAMoE, X-LoRA. LST is Sung et al., 2022; LN-tuning is Zhao et al., 2023. Masking (IA)³ to a few neurons connects to the sparse activation scaling of Stoehr et al., 2024.

The rules certify a merge and name the absorbing weight; a stuck rewrite certifies only a box-local obstruction. RoPE pins a query scaling to \(W_Q\) unless it is pair-constant. Bias-free layers leave a shift no slot, and a new bias, though nearly free at inference, changes the architecture. Post-LN feeds the normalised wire to the residual too, so R7 cannot carry its gains forward.

More: residual-stream edits that do merge

Some edits outside (a) merge anyway. A diagonal edit after a post-LN folds into that norm’s gain and shift; a shift after a pre-LN block with biases, into its FFN output bias; an orthogonal edit in an untied pre-RMSNorm model, globally, SliceGPT-style. No merge is known for LoReFT’s edit at interior positions.

Linear insertions, and ReFT

A linear inserted module fuses at once by R1, and the question is which weight-space method it becomes. ReFT, read one hidden vector at a time, becomes LoRA on a box the network lacks, the identity, written in homogeneous coordinates \((h,1)\) as \([I_d\mid0]\) so that its last column holds the bias.

Proposition VI.4 (linear insertions, and ReFT).

(a) A linear adapter in parallel with a single linear box, \(W_0x+sUDx\), is LoRA: it has the same \(\rho\).

(b) Let \(W_0\in\mathbb R^{m\times n}\) be bias-free. A serial linear adapter \((I+UD)W_0\), with \(U\in\mathbb R^{m\times r}\), \(D\in\mathbb R^{r\times m}\), \(r\ge1\) and the scale absorbed, has image \(W_0+\{Y\in\mathcal M_{\le r}:\mathrm{row}\,Y\subseteq\mathrm{row}\,W_0\}\). It is expressively equivalent to LoRA iff \(\operatorname{rank}W_0=n\), and isomorphic to LoRA, as unpointed methods, iff \(W_0\) is invertible, by \((U,D)\mapsto(U,DW_0)\). If \(r\lt m=n\) and LoRA is pointed at \((0,A_0)\) with \(\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\), a pointed isomorphism from the serial adapter at \((U_0,D_0)\) exists iff \(W_0\) is invertible and \(\mathrm{row}\,A_0=\mathrm{row}(D_0W_0)\); then \(U_0=0\).

(c) LoReFT is \(\Phi(h)=h+R^\top(Wh+b-Rh)\), with \(R\in\mathbb R^{r\times d}\) having orthonormal rows, applied at selected positions. On one hidden vector, \[\Phi(h)=\big(I+R^\top(W-R)\big)h+R^\top b,\] which is LoRA\(_r\) on the affine identity box \([I_d\mid0]\), with \(B=R^\top\) restricted to orthonormal columns and \(A=[W-R\mid b]\). Its image is \((I,0)+\mathcal M_{\le r}(d\times(d+1))\), of dimension \(r(2d+1-r)\). The bias is confined to \(\mathrm{col}\,B\), so for \(r\lt d\) this is smaller than LoRA\(_r\oplus\)BitFit, by \(d-r\). Its generic fibres are \(O(r)\)-orbits, \((R,W,b)\mapsto(OR,OW,Ob)\), so it is simulated by, and not isomorphic to, LoRA\(_r\) pointed at \((R_0^\top,0)\), a pointing with \(B_0\ne0\) and \(A_0=0\). It is pointed iff \(W=R\) and \(b=0\). The reference implementation, pyreft, initialises \(W\) and \(b\) by the default nn.Linear init, independently of \(R\), which leaves a pointing defect \(R^\top((W_0-R_0)h+b_0)\); at initialisation the \(R\)-component of \(h\) is replaced by a random affine function.

(expand)

(a) holds by definition.

(b) \(UDW_0\) ranges over the rank-\(\le r\) matrices whose rows lie in \(\mathrm{row}\,W_0\), which is all of \(\mathcal M_{\le r}\) iff \(\mathrm{row}\,W_0=\mathbb R^n\). If \(W_0\) is invertible, \((U,D)\mapsto(U,DW_0)\) is a linear bijection over \(\Theta\). Conversely, an isomorphism preserves \(\mathrm{Im}\) (Obs I.4(c)), forcing \(\operatorname{rank}W_0=n\), and is a diffeomorphism, forcing \(2mr=r(m+n)\), that is \(m=n\). Pointed case, “if”: \(U_0D_0W_0=0\) and \(W_0\) invertible give \(U_0D_0=0\), and \(\operatorname{rank}(D_0W_0)=\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\) gives \(D_0\) full row rank, so \(U_0=0\). Choose \(g\in GL_r\) with \(A_0=gD_0W_0\) and compose with the gauge \((B,A)\mapsto(Bg^{-1},gA)\). “Only if”: the unpointed part gives \(W_0\) invertible. \(S_1\) is invariant under pointed isomorphism (Obs I.6), and here \(S_1=\{XD_0W_0+U_0Y\}\) for the serial adapter and \(\{XA_0\}=\{Z:\mathrm{row}\,Z\subseteq\mathrm{row}\,A_0\}\) for LoRA. If \((U_0)_{ij}\ne0\), take \(Y=e_jv^\top\) with \(v\notin\mathrm{row}\,A_0\), possible since \(r\lt n\); row \(i\) of \(U_0Y\) is then a nonzero multiple of \(v\), a contradiction. So \(U_0=0\), and \(\mathrm{row}(D_0W_0)=\mathrm{row}\,A_0\).

(c) Expand \(\Phi\). Every \(d\times(d+1)\) matrix of rank \(\le r\) factors as \(R^\top V\) with \(R^\top\) having orthonormal columns (QR), so the image is as stated. \(R^\top\) is injective, so the edit vanishes iff \(W=R\) and \(b=0\). The \(O(r)\)-action preserves \(\Phi\), since \(R^\top O^\top(OW-OR)=R^\top(W-R)\) and \(R^\top O^\top Ob=R^\top b\). The parameter space has dimension \(rd-r(r+1)/2+rd+r\), short of LoRA\(_r\)’s \(2rd+r\) by \(r(r+1)/2\), so no isomorphism exists. The map \((R,W,b)\mapsto(R^\top,[W-R\mid b])\) is the simulation, and it sends the neutral point to \((R_0^\top,0)\). There \(S_1=\{R_0^\top V\}\) has dimension \(r(d+1)\), more than the \(rd\) of LoRA\(_r\) pointed at \((0,A_0)\), so that pointing does not simulate LoReFT (Obs I.6). For the defect, \(R\,\Phi(h)=Wh+b\) because \(RR^\top=I_r\), so the \(R\)-component of the output is the affine map \(h\mapsto Wh+b\). ∎

Num The Jacobian rank is \(22=r(2d+1-r)\) at \(d=6\), \(r=2\), against \(26\) for LoRA\(_r\oplus\)bias. The serial adapter’s image has dimension \(7\) at \((m,n,r)=(5,3,1)\).

Per hidden vector LoReFT, like DiReFT, adds no new geometric type; Wu et al. already read DiReFT as LoRA on hidden representations at certain positions, and name position as what separates ReFT from PEFT. Position also blocks a merge. LoReFT acts at selected prompt positions, outside Theorem VI.3(a), and no merge into the base weights is known.

More: biases, initialisations and whole-sublayer adapters

With a bias \(b_0\), the serial adapter of (b) also moves it by \(UDb_0\). Houlsby et al. initialise both projections near zero, so their adapters start with a small pointing defect. He et al.’s parallel adapter sits beside a whole sublayer, and its linear version edits the skip path.

CreditLoReFT, DiReFT and the reference implementation are Wu et al., 2024. Serial adapters are Houlsby et al., 2019; the parallel form, and the reading of LoRA as a linear adapter parallel to one box, are in He et al., 2022. LoRA is Hu et al., 2021.

Attention is a homomorphism followed by a perspective map

Fix a query \(q\). Attention over a context \(K\) of key–value pairs computes

\[\mathrm{Attn}_q(K)=\frac{\sum_{(k,v)\in K}e^{\langle q,k\rangle/\sqrt d}\,v}{\sum_{(k,v)\in K}e^{\langle q,k\rangle/\sqrt d}}.\]

The pair (total weight, weighted sum of values) is additive in the context, and only the division \((Z,S)\mapsto S/Z\), the perspective map of convex analysis, is nonlinear. With a prefix \(P\) before the content \(C\), the output averages what \(P\) alone and \(C\) alone would give, weighted by their shares of the mass, so the prefix is a parallel branch gated by its share.

Theorem VI.5 (attention is a homomorphism followed by a perspective map). Known in part Fix a query \(q\). Multisets of key–value pairs form a commutative monoid under \(\uplus\). Define
\[\Sigma_q(K)=\Big(\sum e^{\langle q,k\rangle/\sqrt d},\ \sum e^{\langle q,k\rangle/\sqrt d}\,v\Big).\]

(a) \(\Sigma_q\) is a monoid homomorphism into \((\mathbb R_{\ge0}\times\mathbb R^{d_v},+)\), landing in \(\{Z\gt0\}\cup\{(0,0)\}\). On nonempty multisets, \(\mathrm{Attn}_q=\pi\circ\Sigma_q\) with the partial map \(\pi(Z,S)=S/Z\), defined for \(Z\gt0\).

(b) Prefix is a gated parallel adapter. For nonempty \(P\) and \(C\), \(\mathrm{Attn}_q(P\uplus C)=(1-\lambda_q)\mathrm{Attn}_q(C)+\lambda_q\mathrm{Attn}_q(P)\), with \(\lambda_q=Z_q(P)/(Z_q(P)+Z_q(C))\).

(c) Per-layer pattern rigidity. Within one layer, for fixed \(q\) and \(C\), the content attention weights are multiplied by \(1-\lambda_q\), their ratios are unchanged, and the output lies on the segment from \(\mathrm{Attn}_q(C)\) to \(\mathrm{Attn}_q(P)\in\mathrm{conv}\,V_P\). Lower-layer prefixes change the queries, keys and values of higher layers, so attention patterns at the level of the network are not rigid.

(d) LLaMA-Adapter normalises \(P\) and \(C\) separately and adds a zero-initialised gate, \(\mathrm{Attn}_q(C)+g\,\mathrm{Attn}_q(P)\), with \(g=\tanh(g_l)\) per head in the paper and one raw scalar per layer in HF PEFT. It uses two perspective maps, leaves the content weights exactly unchanged, and is pointed at \(g=0\). Its prompt keys and values are the frozen \(W_k,W_v\) applied to a learned prompt in each adapted top layer.

(e) Prompt \(\to\) deep prefix in causal decoders. Assume that the soft prompt comes first, that attention is causal, and that content positions are offset by the prompt length in both methods (as HF PEFT does via cache_position). Then the states at prompt positions depend only on \(p\), and \(h:p\mapsto(K_j(p),V_j(p))_j\) satisfies \(\Phi\circ\rho_{\rm prefix}\circ h=\Phi\circ\rho_{\rm prompt}\) as functions of the content tokens, read at content positions. Without the offset, under RoPE, \(h\) must also rotate the prompt keys back by the prompt length. The methods live on different extensions, so this is an arrow of the unpointed slice over the realisation \(C^\infty(X_{\rm content},Y_{\rm content})\), not of \(\mathsf{Meth}\). It fails in bidirectional encoders of depth \(\ge2\).

(f) Obstructions. Call a head standard if it is vanilla softmax attention, without torch’s add_bias_kv or add_zero_attn, learned sinks or softmax-plus-one. On length-one contexts every weight setting of a standard head is affine in \(x\). For generic frozen head weights (in a bias-free head, \(W_o\ne0\) and \(W_q\ne0\) suffice) and generic prefixes, a prefixed head is not, because the gate \(\lambda(x)\) varies. So prefix tuning and P-tuning v2 are not box-locally mergeable. If \(W_oW_v\ne0\), no prefix is neutral for the head, so prompt and prefix tuning are generically unpointed.

(expand)

(a) Sums over a disjoint union split, and \(Z=0\) only for the empty multiset.

(b) \(\pi(Z_P+Z_C,S_P+S_C)=\dfrac{S_P+S_C}{Z_P+Z_C}=\lambda\dfrac{S_P}{Z_P}+(1-\lambda)\dfrac{S_C}{Z_C}\).

(c) Each content weight is \(e^{\langle q,k\rangle/\sqrt d}/(Z_P+Z_C)=(1-\lambda)\,e^{\langle q,k\rangle/\sqrt d}/Z_C\), and \(\mathrm{Attn}_q(P)\) is a convex combination of \(V_P\).

(d) This is the definition.

(e) Induction over layers. Under causal masking, prompt positions attend only to prompt positions, and with the stated offset their positional encodings agree in both methods. Without the offset, RoPE sees the content-to-prompt displacement shifted by the prompt length, which the counter-rotation restores.

(f) A softmax over one token equals \(1\), so the original head computes \(W_o(W_vx+b_v)+b_o\). The prefixed output is real-analytic in \((x,\text{prefix})\), and one explicit witness of a nonzero second derivative along a ray makes the prefixes with affine output a nowhere-dense analytic subset. If \(W_o=0\) the head is constant, and if \(W_q=0\) with \(W_k^\top b_q=0\) the gate is constant and the head stays affine, which is why genericity of the frozen weights is needed. For unpointedness, the prefixed output on one token is \(W_o\big[(1-\lambda(x))(W_vx+b_v)+\lambda(x)\,\mathrm{Attn}_{q(x)}(P)\big]+b_o\) with \(\lambda(x)\gt0\). It equals the frozen output iff \(W_o\mathrm{Attn}_{q(x)}(P)=W_o(W_vx+b_v)\) for all \(x\). The left side lies in the compact set \(W_o\,\mathrm{conv}\,V_P\), and the right side is unbounded when \(W_oW_v\ne0\). ∎

Num The identities (a)–(d) and the non-affinity in (f) are checked numerically; Figure 13 recomputes them in your browser.

CreditThe identity is standard. Part (b), prefix tuning as a gated parallel adapter, is He et al., 2022; see also Chen et al., 2022. The per-layer rigidity of (c) and the prompt-to-prefix construction behind (e) are in Petrov, Torr and Bibi, 2024 (§6, §2.2), informally already in Li and Liang, 2021 (§7.2); Petrov et al. also show that prefix tuning a pretrained transformer can be a universal approximator (2024). LLaMA-Adapter is Zhang et al., 2024; P-tuning v2 is Liu et al., 2022; on what prompts can express, see Wang et al., 2023. Ours are the homomorphism-and-perspective organisation, the explicit position hypotheses and the slice typing of (e).

At the lowest prefixed layer content weights keep their pretrained ratios. Higher layers change only through their modified inputs, to first order additively at each position (Petrov et al., §6), so a prefix cannot impose a new query–key interaction directly; no network-level impossibility follows. Petrov et al. and Li and Liang state that prompt tuning is strictly weaker than prefix tuning; a one-layer example with one virtual token shows it, and the general case is open.

Where each method lands

The verdicts are box-local, and the attention results are per layer. Two questions remain open. Is prefix tuning non-mergeable for the whole network? Is prompt tuning strictly weaker than P-tuning v2 in general causal decoders, and how do the two relate in encoders?

Exposé VII–VIII

The atlas

Fourteen methods read through the three invariants, seven coordinates that place every catalogued method in a periodic table, and five rules that draw the arrows between methods.

Do not attack the hard nut with a hammer. Let the sea rise around it until it opens of its own accord.

after Grothendieck
In one minute
  • Each of fourteen methods gets a card saying what it reaches, how training moves it, what a moved base point does and how it merges, every line linked to its result.
  • Seven coordinates read off the formula place all 382 catalogued methods in a periodic table. Some cells are empty by theorem; for instance, no linear method starts at an apex.
  • \(N\) simulates \(M\) when a pointed smooth map \(h\) gives \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\). Five rules produce every arrow (403 are recorded, each with its \(h\)), and three tests rule arrows out; PiSSA and LoRA, for instance, have no arrow either way.
  • Parameters are not reach. At 131,072 parameters on a \(4096\times4096\) matrix, LoRA\(_{16}\) reaches 130,816 directions and LoHa\(_{8,8}\) at most 122,753.
CreditBalancedness: Du, Hu and Lee, 2018 and Arora et al., 2018. Conserved charges: Zhao et al., 2023. The balanced slice: Kempf and Ness (1979). The crossover is the closed form of Tarmoun et al. and Min et al. (ICML 2021). Zero-\(B\) versus zero-\(A\) starts: Hayou et al., 2024. Scaled GD: Tong, Ma and Chi; Zhang and Pilanci, 2024.

A method is a pointed map \(\rho:(Q,q_0)\to(\Theta,\theta_0)\). Its image germ says what it can express, its fibres and the optimizer’s metric how it moves, base change what a moved \(\theta_0\) does, and extensions cover adapters and prompts. Exposé VII applies this toolkit to fourteen methods; Exposé VIII turns the answers into coordinates and arrows, mapped in Figure 14.

VII. Fourteen methods through the three invariants

CreditGaLore as one-sided LoRA with carried state: Torroba-Hennigen et al., 2025. Prefixes as gated adapters: He et al., 2022; rigid content ratios and the prompt-to-prefix map: Petrov, Torr and Bibi, 2024. The (IA)\(^3\) merge into \(W_{\rm down}\): Liu et al., 2022; scale migration through SwiGLU: AWQ. LoKr’s transport: Van Loan and Pitsianis, 1993. The \(2r\) bound underlies the PiSSA-to-LoRA conversion of Meng et al., 2024.

Each card gives \((Q,q_0,\rho)\) for \(y=Wx\), \(W\in\mathbb R^{m\times n}\), then the image germ (Exposé II), fibre and metric (III), base change (V) and merge (VI). The top rule’s colour is the modification kind, and the last line links each claim to its result.

LoRA

Hu et al., 2021
\[\rho(B,A)=W_0+sBA,\qquad q_0=(0,A_0)\]
Image
The cone \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), of dimension \(r(m+n-r)\) for \(r\lt\min(m,n)\). With \(\operatorname{rank}A_0=r\), \(\theta_0\) is an apex with deficit \(r(n-r)\).
Fibre
Gauge \(GL_r\). Gradient flow with per-block rates conserves \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\), and for isotropic \(\Phi\) is solved in closed form, one-sided well below a scale \(\sigma^*\), balanced well above. On the full-rank locus, with channel-uniform hyperparameters, SGD keeps \(O(r)\) of the gauge, Adam the signed permutations, undamped scaled GD all of it.
Base
\(K\) cycles reach \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le\min(Kr,m,n)}\). Full-rank pointings form three strata, as a set \(\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\sqcup\mathrm{Gr}(r,m)\sqcup\mathcal M_r\).
Merge
M1.

Thm II.2 · Thm III.5 · Thm III.9 · Thm III.12 · Thm V.3

DoRA

Liu et al., 2024
\[\rho(\mu,B,A)=\mathrm{diag}(\mu)\,N(W_0+sBA)\]
Image
\(N\) normalises rows. For \(W_0\) without zero rows and \(r\le\min(m,n)\) the image is \(\mathrm{Diag}_m\cdot(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r})\), exactly LoRA plus a free output scale, of dimension \(\min(mn,\,r(m+n-r)+m)\) for generic \(W_0\) [Num]. Its updates can exceed rank \(r\).
Fibre
