The gauge contains \(GL_r\), and gradient flow through the detached norm conserves \(\Phi\). Any gap to LoRA plus a scale, from the same start, comes from training dynamics.
Merge
M1.

Prop II.8 · Prop II.12 · Thm III.5

OFT / BOFT

Qiu et al., 2023; Liu et al., 2023
\[W_0R^\top\ \ \text{(OFT)},\qquad \mathrm{diag}(s)\,W_0R^\top\ \ \text{(BOFT)}\]
Image
\(R\) is block Cayley (OFT) or a butterfly product (BOFT). With exact Cayley maps the image is an orbit in the level set of \(K_{\rm out}=WW^\top\), and block OFT fixes \(n/b\) partial Grams too. BOFT keeps \(\lvert\cos\rvert\) between neurons if all \(s_i\ne0\), and the cosines if they share a sign. HF’s default Cayley–Neumann OFT is only approximately orthogonal. Regular base point (BOFT: disjoint factor planes).
Base
Exact block OFT on a fixed partition is idempotent up to closure; iterated BOFT with \(m'\) factors reaches \(\mathrm{diag}\cdot W_0\cdot P\big(\prod SO(b\,2^{m'-1})\big)P^\top\) for a fixed permutation \(P\), and, for injective \(W_0\), \(\mathrm{diag}\cdot W_0\cdot SO(n)\) only at full depth. Re-merging HF’s default with small generators can only shrink singular values.
Merge
M1.

Thm II.5 · Cor II.6 · Thm V.3

(IA)\(^3\)

Liu et al., 2022
\[\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\,W_0\ \ (W_K,W_V),\qquad W_0\,\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\ \ (W_{\rm down})\]
Image
Linear, the closure of a torus orbit, inside \(\mathrm{HiRA}_1\). Zeros of \(W_0\) stay zero, and row lines (column lines on \(W_{\rm down}\)) stay fixed while all \(\ell_i\ne0\). Covariance \(\mathrm{Mon}\times GL\), or \(GL\times\mathrm{Mon}\) on \(W_{\rm down}\).
Base
Idempotent.
Merge
Fuses into \(W_K\), \(W_V\), \(W_{\rm down}\) always; into \(W_{\rm up}\) under SwiGLU, under ReLU for \(\ell\ge0\), and under GELU only for entries \(0\) or \(1\).

Thm II.5 · Prop II.9 · Thm VI.3

VeRA

Kopiczko et al., 2023
\[\rho(b,d)=W_0+\Lambda_bB\Lambda_dA,\qquad b_0=0\]
Image
\(B\), \(A\) frozen and random. A toric cone of dimension \(m+r-1\) [Num] with apex at \(\theta_0\). At \(b_0=0\) only \(b\) gets a gradient.
Fibre
Gauge \(\mathbb R^\times\), \((cb,d/c)\). Gradient flow conserves \(\lVert b\rVert^2/\eta_b-\lVert d\rVert^2/\eta_d\).
Base
If \(B\) has no zero entry, rebasing reaches exactly LoRA-FA\((A)\), the target of its arrow \(h(b,d)=\Lambda_bB\Lambda_d\).
Merge
M1.

Lemma II.4 · Prop II.12 · Thm III.5 · Thm V.3

LoHa / LoKr

Hyeon-Woo et al., 2021; Yeh et al., 2023
\[(B_1A_1)\odot(B_2A_2)\ \ \text{(LoHa)},\qquad C\otimes BA\ \ \text{(LoKr)}\]
Image
LoHa\(_{r,r}\): rank up to \(\min(r^2,m,n)\), dimension at most \(2r(m+n)-2r^2-(m+n-1)\) (equal numerically below \(mn\)), gauge \(GL_r^2\) times a torus, apex at a zero factor. For \(r\le2\) it lies inside LoRA\(_{2r}\), at equal cost.
Image
LoKr is a constrained LoRA\(_1\) after the Van Loan–Pitsianis rearrangement. For \(C\in\mathbb R^{m_1\times n_1}\) and inner rank \(r'\le\min(m_2,n_2)\) its maximum rank is \(\min(m_1,n_1)r'\). It lies inside zero-init LoRA\(_r\) iff \(r\ge\min(m_1,n_1)r'\), and is otherwise incomparable with it when \(\min(m_1,m_2)\min(n_1,n_2)\gt1\).
Merge
M1.

Thm II.11 · Prop II.12 · Prop II.13

FourierFT

Gao et al., 2024
\[W_0+\alpha\,\mathrm{Re}\,F^{-1}(c),\qquad c\ \text{on }n_f\text{ fixed frequencies}\]
Image
A coordinate mask in the real cosine frame, inside the point-symmetric subspace. Started at \(\theta_0\) it is linear and regular, with a gauge dimension per sampled conjugate pair; HF’s default random spectrum starts elsewhere. Its covariance \(\Pi_{\rm cyc}\) excludes most neuron permutations, so permuting hidden units can change what it reaches.
Base
Idempotent with fixed indices.
Merge
M1.

Prop II.12 · Prop II.13 · Obs II.15 · Thm V.3

BitFit

Ben Zaken et al., 2021
\[b\mapsto b_0+\beta\ \text{on every bias},\qquad W=W_0\]
Image
A translation of the bias block; linear and regular.
Base
Idempotent.
Merge
Nothing to merge. Llama and Mistral have no biases, so it trains nothing there (Figure 17).

Prop II.12 · Thm V.3

Adapters

Houlsby et al., 2019
\[x\mapsto x+U\sigma(Dx),\qquad D\in\mathbb R^{r\times d},\ U\in\mathbb R^{d\times r}\]
Image
Function level, neutral at \(U=0\); Houlsby et al. start near it.
Fibre
Only hidden-unit permutations under Houlsby’s GELU; a ReLU would add positive rescalings. At \(U=0\) only \(U\) moves to first order.
Merge
Not box-locally mergeable for non-linear \(\sigma\). With \(\sigma=\mathrm{id}\), an adapter parallel to one linear layer is LoRA.

Lemma II.4 · Lemma VI.2 · Thm VI.3 · Prop VI.4

Prefix / prompt tuning

Li and Liang, 2021; Lester et al., 2021
\[\mathrm{Attn}_q(P\uplus C)=(1-\lambda_q)\,\mathrm{Attn}_q(C)+\lambda_q\,\mathrm{Attn}_q(P)\]
Image
Unpointed extensions on the sequence wire, since on a head with \(W_oW_v\ne0\) no prefix is neutral. A prefix is an input-gated parallel adapter on attention, rescaling its layer’s content attention weights but keeping their ratios.
Arrows
In causal decoders with content positions offset by the prompt length (as in HF PEFT), every soft prompt is a deep prefix. LLaMA-Adapter’s zero-initialised gate adds a neutral point.
Merge
Not box-mergeable, since on a softmax head with \(W_o\ne0\) and \(W_q\ne0\) a generic prefix makes the head non-affine, even on one token.

Thm VI.5 · Def VI.1

PiSSA / LoftQ / QLoRA

Meng et al., 2024; Li et al., 2023; Dettmers et al., 2023
\[W_{\rm res}+BA,\ \ B_0A_0=[W_0]_r\ \ \text{(PiSSA)}\]\[\kappa\theta_0+sBA,\ \ B_0=0\ \ \text{(QLoRA)}\]
Image
PiSSA is a split start, the cone with vertex \(W_0-[W_0]_r\) and \(\theta_0\) a smooth point. Its image and LoRA’s are incomparable, no exact pointing of LoRA\(_r\) passes rank \(2r\), and \(\Phi_0=0\) at equal rates.
Base
QLoRA is LoRA pointed at \(\kappa\theta_0\), with generically full-rank defect \(e=\kappa\theta_0-\theta_0\). QPiSSA splits, then quantises; one-step LoftQ quantises, then fits \(e\), leaving \(e-[e]_r\) only when \(\alpha/r=1\), as HF ignores the scale.
Merge
Exact merges of generic adapters leave the quantised type; type-preserving ones are lossy (Mq), QA-LoRA excepted.

Thm II.1 · Thm II.2 · Cor III.6 · Prop V.2

ReLoRA

Lialin et al., 2023
\[\theta_{k+1}=\theta_k+sB_kA_k,\quad\text{fresh }(B_k,A_k)\text{ each stage}\]
Base
Rebased LoRA. After \(K\) stages it reaches exactly \(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le\min(Kr,m,n)}\), so all of \(\Theta\) at \(K^*=\lceil\min(m,n)/r\rceil\). It is \(\mathrm{LoRA}_r^{\oplus K}\) unrolled in time.
Fibre
Per stage, an apex with gauge \(GL_r\).
Merge
Each stage merges (M1).

Obs I.7 · Thm V.3

GaLore

Zhao et al., 2024
\[W_{t+1}=W_t-\eta\,P_t\,\mathrm{opt}\big(P_t^\top G_t\big)\]
Fibre
A backward lens with compression \(P_t^\top\) and anchor \(P_t\), refreshed from a gradient SVD every \(T\) steps. Under Adam without weight decay or full-gradient clipping, each period is exactly one-sided LoRA \(W_k+P_kC\), with optimizer state carried over, not reset or transported.
Base
The anchor is fixed within a period, so integrability is trivial; as a set over its possible sequences of projectors, the moving shape reaches all of \(\Theta\).
Merge
Not applicable, since it writes \(W\) itself.

Def IV.1 · Prop IV.2 · Prop IV.3 · Thm V.3

Merging

Ilharco et al., 2022; Huang et al., 2023
\[\theta_0+\textstyle\sum_i\lambda_i\tau_i\ \ \text{(task arithmetic)}\]\[\big(\textstyle\sum_iw_iB_i\big)\big(\textstyle\sum_jw_jA_j\big)\ \ \text{(LoraHub)}\]
Fibre
Task arithmetic is \(GL(\Theta)\)-natural; TIES (global trimming, sign election by total mass) is natural under homotheties times signed permutations, DARE under the torus and, in law, permutations; neither under rotations.
Fibre
Factor-wise rules (LoraHub, HF’s linear) depend on each adapter’s gauge unless the adapters share a factor, such as a frozen \(A\).
Merge
Merging into a full-precision base is the canonical arrow to full fine-tuning.

Obs I.4 · Prop V.4

VIII.A Seven axes, each read off the formula

CreditHe et al., 2022 gave adapters four design axes (functional form, insertion form, modified representation, composition function), and Chen et al., 2023 searched a similar design space. The seven axes extend the idea to weight-space, backward and post-hoc methods, each tied to a result.
Definition (coordinates of a method). The coordinates of a method \(M=(Q,q_0,\rho)\) are its answers to the card questions on the seven axes below, each read off \(\rho\), \(q_0\) and the box \(\rho\) acts on, with no training.
AxisQuestionValuesLoRA(IA)\(^3\)Prefix tuning
1 · KindWhere does it act?R reparametrisation · E pointed extension · E° unpointed extension · B backward lens or schedule · P post-hoc operation · F\(_T\) family over tasks, inputs or tenantsRRE°
2 · ShapeWhat is the image germ?Lin (\(\rho\) affine) · Cone (multihomogeneous; Cone* when a split moves the vertex off \(\theta_0\)) · Orb (group orbit) · Sat (group acting on a cone) · Meet (orbit \(\cap\) cone) · Fun (function level)Cone, \(d=r(m+n-r)\)Lin, a torus-orbit closureFun
3 · Base pointIs \(\rho(q_0)=\theta_0\)? Is \(\dim S_1=d(M)\)?regular · apex (deficit \(\gt0\)) · neutral · defect · unpointedapex, deficit \(r(n-r)\)regularunpointed
4 · GaugeWhich reparametrisations fix \(\rho\)?none · scalar · torus · \(GL\) · \(GL\times\)torus · non-linear\(GL_r\)nonenon-linear (Li–Liang’s MLP)
5 · CovarianceFor which \((g,h)\) is \(\mathrm{Im}\,M(gW_0h^{-1})=g\,\mathrm{Im}\,M(W_0)\,h^{-1}\)?\(GL\times GL\) · \(CO\times CO\) · \(GL\times CO\) · \(\mathrm{Mon}\times GL\) · \(\mathrm{Mon}\times\mathrm{Mon}\) · \(\Pi_X\) (imposed structure) · frame (in law)\(GL\times GL\)\(\mathrm{Mon}\times GL\); \(GL\times\mathrm{Mon}\) on \(W_{\rm down}\)\(GL\times GL\), function level
6 · MergeDoes the update fuse into its box, or by R1–R7 into a neighbour?M1 (own box) · M1→ (a neighbour) · Mq (leaves the quantised type) · M∞ (no box-local merge) · n/aM1M1→M∞
7 · RebasingWhat does merge-and-restart reach?idempotent · →span · →group · schedule→span, all of \(\Theta\) at \(K=\lceil\min(m,n)/r\rceil\)idempotent–
(expand)

Each axis has a procedure that uses only \(\rho\), \(q_0\) and the box.

  • Kind. Check whether the trained model is \(f(\rho(q),-)\) with the original architecture (R), whether new boxes are inserted and some parameter value makes them the identity (E) or none does (E°), whether only the gradient path or the schedule changes (B), whether the inputs are trained checkpoints (P), and whether the adapter depends on a task, an input or a tenant (F\(_T\)). See Def I.2, Def IV.1 and Def VI.1.
  • Shape. Read the degree of \(\rho-\theta_0\) in \(q\). An affine map gives Lin and a multihomogeneous non-linear one gives Cone. A group acting on \(\theta_0\) gives Orb, a torus acting on another method’s image gives Sat (Prop II.8), and an intersection gives Meet (Thm II.7).
  • Base point. Evaluate \(\rho(q_0)\) and compute \(S_1=d\rho_{q_0}(T_{q_0}Q)\), then compare \(\dim S_1\) with the generic rank \(d(M)\) (Def I.5). For LoRA this is Thm II.2(b).
  • Gauge. Insert \(gg^{-1}\) on each internal wire between trainable boxes and slide diagonals through copy spiders, then confirm \(\lvert M\rvert-d(M)\) by a Jacobian rank at a random point (Prop II.12(a)).
  • Covariance. Test the identity of Def II.14 on generators of \(GL_m\times GL_n\), in distribution when frames are random or come from data.
  • Merge. Run the rewrite rules R1–R7 of Thm VI.3 with their side conditions, adding the type condition of Prop V.2(d) for a quantised base. The rules are sufficient conditions. They certify M1 and M1→, and M∞ records a box-local obstruction. Whether a whole network admits some other merge is left open.
  • Rebasing. Apply Thm V.3(a) to additive shapes and Thm V.3(b) and (c) to actions.
∎

Methods with different shapes or base-point types are never isomorphic, however alike their formulas (Obs I.4(c), Obs I.6).

VIII.B The periodic table

CreditThe colour key refines the additive, selective, reparametrisation-based and hybrid classes of Lialin et al., 2023; the survey of Han et al., 2024 uses a similar split.

Figure 14 splits reparametrisations by how they touch \(W_0\), additively, by a group action, by a Hadamard product or by selecting coordinates. Some of its cells are empty by theorem. An affine \(\rho\) has the same differential everywhere, so no linear method is an apex, and in the catalogue none is. Corollary 2 of Thm V.3 ties idempotent rebasing to shapes that are fixed subspaces, subgroups or submonoids, and of the catalogue’s 92 idempotent methods, 84 are linear shapes and 7 are orbits. The other, WASI, trains on the rank-\(K\) variety \(\mathcal M_{\le K}\) itself, a set that does not move with the base.

VIII.C When one method simulates another

“Generalises” and “is a special case of” get one reading here. \(N\) simulates \(M\) when a pointed smooth map \(h:Q_M\to Q_N\) satisfies \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\) (Def I.2), so \(N\) reaches every weight \(M\) reaches. Five rules produce every arrow in the atlas; three tests rule arrows out.

RuleStatementThe map \(h\), or the testExamples
A1 · intervalFrozen \(\to M\to\) Full FT for every weight-space \(M\).\(h=\mathrm{const}_{q_0}\); \(h=\rho_M\)Merging is the arrow to Full FT.
A2 · freezingFreezing coordinates at base values gives a sub-object.\(h(q')=(q',q_0'')\)LoRA-FA\((A_0)\hookrightarrow\) LoRA pointed at \((0,A_0)\); MiCA \(\hookrightarrow\) LoRA pointed at \((B_0,0)\).
A3 · factorisation\(\rho_M=\rho_N\circ h\) for an explicit pointed map \(h\), usually polynomial.VeRA \(\to\) LoRA-FA\((A)\): \(h(b,d)=\Lambda_bB\Lambda_d\). LoHa\(_{r_1,r_2}\to\) LoRA\(_{r_1r_2}\): face-splitting products. (IA)\(^3\to\mathrm{HiRA}_1\) pointed at \((0,\mathbf 1^\top)\): \(h(\ell)=(\ell-\mathbf 1,\mathbf 1^\top)\).Isomorphisms: rsLoRA \(\cong\) LoRA by \(h=(\sqrt r\,B,A)\); MiSS (balance mode) \(\cong\) LoRA-FA\((\mathbf 1^\top\otimes I_r)\); a linear adapter parallel to one layer \(\cong\) LoRA.
A4 · productsA product comes with its two projections.\(R\mapsto B_0R\), \(R\mapsto RA_0\)LoRA-XS \(\to\) FA\((A_0)\), FB\((B_0)\). HRA’s image lies in OFT\(_{SO}\)’s and LoRA\(_r\)’s, an image-level inclusion only.
A5 · sums\(M\to M\oplus N\), and \(K\) stages embed in \(K+1\).\(q\mapsto(q,q_0^N)\)LoRA\(_r\oplus\)LoRA\(_s\cong\) LoRA\(_{r+s}\) (unpointed); ReLoRA\(^K\to\) ReLoRA\(^{K+1}\).
T · transport\(T_*\) for \(T\in GL(\Theta)\) is an automorphism of the slice, giving one object in two frames.no arrow; record the frame \(T\)Sums of Kronecker products are \(\mathcal R^{-1}_*\mathrm{LoRA}\); HiRA is \((D_{W_0})_*\mathrm{LoRA}\) at \(\theta_0\) when \(W_0\) has no zero entry.
O1 · imageNo arrow if \(\mathrm{Im}\,M\not\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,N\), as when \(N\) keeps an invariant \(M\) breaks.compare imagesLoRA \(\not\to\) exact-Cayley OFT, which keeps \(K_{\rm out}\).
O2 · sizeNo arrow if \(d(M)\gt d(N)\) or \(\rho_{\max}(M)\gt\rho_{\max}(N)\).compare dimension and maximum rankLoRA\(_r\not\to\) LoRA-FA, since \(r(m+n-r)\gt mr\).
O3 · first orderNo arrow if \(S_1(M)\not\subseteq S_1(N)\), or if \(M\) and \(N\) lie in different strata of Thm II.2.compare \(S_1\) and strata\(\mathrm T_A\)- \(\not\to\) \(\mathrm T_B\)-pointed LoRA; PiSSA and LoRA, either way; LoRA \(\not\to\) AdaLoRA, though on one matrix their images agree.
(expand)

The rules produce arrows. In each case \(h\) is smooth and pointed, and \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\) is checked by multiplying out. A1 is Obs I.4(a), since \(\rho_M\circ\mathrm{const}_{q_0}=\theta_0\) and \(\mathrm{id}\circ\rho_M=\rho_M\). A2 holds because freezing \(q''\) at \(q_0''\) leaves the rest of the formula unchanged. For A3, take VeRA, where \(W_0+\Lambda_bB\Lambda_dA=W_0+(\Lambda_bB\Lambda_d)A\) and \(h\) sends \(b_0=0\) to LoRA-FA’s base point \(0\). A4 is the universal property of the fibre product (Obs I.4(b), Prop I.8(a)); for a meet such as HRA only the inclusion of images holds (Thm II.7). A5 is the identity below, from Obs I.7.

\[\rho_{M\oplus N}(q,q_0^N)=\theta_0+\delta_M(q)+\delta_N(q_0^N)=\rho_M(q).\]

The tests rule arrows out. An arrow gives \(\mathrm{Im}\,M=\rho_N(h(Q_M))\subseteq\mathrm{Im}\,N\), which is O1. Images are semialgebraic, or definable when softmax enters, so dimension is monotone under inclusion; and every update in \(\mathrm{Im}\,M-\theta_0\) also lies in \(\mathrm{Im}\,N-\theta_0\), so its rank is bounded by \(\rho_{\max}(N)\). Together these give O2. The chain rule at \(q_0\) gives \(S_1(M)\subseteq S_1(N)\) (Obs I.6), the first half of O3. For the strata, the images of \(\mathrm T\)-type and \(\mathrm S\)-type objects are incomparable (Thm II.2(d)). Inside the \(\mathrm T\) strata, \(S_1(\mathrm T_A)=\{XA_0\}\) contains matrices whose column space leaves \(\mathrm{col}\,B_0'\), so it does not lie in \(S_1(\mathrm T_B)=\{B_0'Y\}\), and symmetrically.

Transport. \(T_*\) is an automorphism of the slice with inverse \((T^{-1})_*\) (Prop II.13). It identifies \(M\) and \(T_*M\) as one object in two frames and does not by itself give an arrow between them.

∎
More: which published relations survive

Some become exact. VeRA is LoRA-FA\((A)\) with a constrained factor, and rsLoRA and a linear parallel adapter are LoRA. Others are weaker than they sound. HRA’s arrow to LoRA\(_r\) lands on a degenerate pointing (\(B_0A_0=0\)) outside the strata of Thm II.2, and LoRA is a special case of AdaLoRA only unpointed, their first-order images having dimensions \(mr\) and \(r\) (Lemma II.4).

The literature in time

Figure 16 dates each method by its first arXiv submission. Landmarks come in rough order, with bottleneck adapters in 2019, prefixes and LoRA’s cone in 2021, the torus of (IA)\(^3\) in 2022, base change with QLoRA in 2023, and GaLore’s moving frame in 2024, the year of 113 of the 382 methods.

What a budget buys

Proposition II.12 separates the budget \(\lvert M\rvert\), what a method trains, from the expressive dimension \(d(M)\), the directions it can move the weights in. The gap is gauge waste (Def I.5), per matrix \(r^2\) for LoRA, one for VeRA (numerically), zero for injective linear methods, at least \(2r^2+m+n-1\) for LoHa\(_{r,r}\) (Prop II.12). At 131,072 parameters on a \(4096\times4096\) matrix, LoRA\(_{16}\) reaches 130,816 directions at rank up to 16, LoHa\(_{8,8}\) at most 122,753 at rank up to 64. Rankings at matched budget and at matched \(d(M)\) can differ (Prediction X.10).

The catalogue

CreditEarlier maps made this one possible: the surveys of Lialin et al., 2023, Xu et al., 2023, Han et al., 2024 and Mao et al., 2024, the delta-tuning study of Ding et al., 2023, and the libraries AdapterHub, Adapters and Hugging Face PEFT, whose source settled many details.

Everything above rests on a catalogue of 382 methods and 138 papers, checked against arXiv and, for library claims, Hugging Face PEFT as of 30 September 2026; 82 methods ship in that library. Each method entry gives formula, budget, locus, kind, merge verdict, initialisation, relations, sources, coordinates and a categorical reading, most with arrows and obstructions. Every entry has BibTeX and an address such as #m-lora.

The atlas is meant to grow. A new method enters by answering the seven questions and exhibiting its arrows, each with its map \(h\); a disputed comparison is settled by an explicit \(h\) or one of the three tests.

Exposé IX

Rosetta stone

A dictionary from fine-tuning phrases to the objects of the relative theory, with the result behind each pairing that needs one.

A half-seen analogy between two theories is the most fertile thing a mathematician can hold. One day the hunch turns into certainty, and the twin theories show the source they share.

after Weil’s Rosetta-stone letter of 1940
In one minute
  • LoRA+ is \(\alpha\) in disguise. Under Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\), no weight decay or clipping and one schedule, LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) trains exactly like LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\), given the same data order and dropout masks (Schulman et al., 2025).
  • Under Adam without weight decay or full-gradient clipping, GaLore is one-sided LoRA, restarted every period with its optimizer state kept (Torroba-Hennigen et al., 2025). QLoRA is LoRA attached at the quantised weight, off \(\theta_0\) by a generically full-rank error.
  • Read from the mathematical side (Figure 19), some constructions are known methods. In the SVD frames of \(W_0\), when \(\operatorname{rank}W_0\ge r\), the product of the two one-sided LoRAs is LoRA-XS, and for injective \(W_0\) and even \(r\le n-2\) LoRA’s image meets the rotation orbit of \(W_0\) in the image of HRA (without Gram–Schmidt, the HF default).

A fine-tuning paper says that LoRA+ gives \(B\) a larger learning rate than \(A\). The relative theory says that, under the hypotheses of Theorem III.10, LoRA+ lies on the torus orbit of plain LoRA with a larger \(\alpha\). Passing between such sentences lets each side borrow the other’s theorems, and Figure 19 is a dictionary for the passage.

How to read an entry

An entry claims that a phrase names a definite object, on which claims about the phrase can be checked. There are three strengths.

CreditThe language of parametrised maps and lenses on our right-hand side comes from Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras, 2017 and Cruttwell et al., 2021. Inside PEFT, He et al., 2022 wrote an earlier dictionary of this kind, reading adapters, prefix tuning and LoRA as modifications of one hidden state.
StrengthWhat the entry assertsExample
DefinitionThe phrase gets a precise, computable meaning.“Effective number of parameters” is \(d(M)\), the generic rank of \(d\rho\) (Def I.5).
TheoremThe phrase hides a claim, proved under stated hypotheses.“No initialisation buys rank above \(2r\)” means that on one \(m\times n\) box the exact pointings of LoRA\(_r\) together reach \(\theta_0+\mathcal M_{\le\min(2r,m,n)}\) (Thm II.1).
AnalogyA borrowed word names a pattern and adds nothing to the theorem behind it.“Noether charge”, “gauge”, “Erlangen”, “moduli space”.

The box fixes each borrowed word’s sense; Exposé XI says more (Analogy vs theorem).

Analogy (the borrowed words).
  • Noether: conserved charges from symmetries, proved for continuous-time flows with constant rates (Thm III.5); Adam and momentum break them.
  • Gauge: the symmetry group of a parametrisation, with no connection.
  • Erlangen: a theorem for group-type methods (Thm II.5), and a name for the shape of cones such as LoRA’s image.
  • Moduli space: LoRA initialisations in three full-rank strata, classified only as a set (Thm II.2).
  • Lens duality: exact only at degree one, for optimizers that read neither the parameters nor the full gradient (Prop IV.2).

Most of the right-hand column concerns images, which measure expressivity rather than learnability; entries with dynamical claims state their optimizer.

Six entries, one from each exposé

I. “Method \(N\) can do whatever \(M\) can”

Entries 3 and 4 (Exposé I).
PEFT “method \(N\) can do whatever \(M\) can”; the frozen model and full fine-tuning.
Category theory · geometry a morphism \(h:M\to N\) of the pointed slice, a pointed smooth map with \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\); the initial and terminal objects.

The weak reading asks that \(N\) reach every weight \(M\) reaches; the useful one asks that \(N\) also follow \(M\)’s parameter paths, through a map \(h\) with \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\). Methods at \(\theta_0\) with such arrows form the pointed slice \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\) (Def I.2). The frozen model is initial, full fine-tuning is terminal, and the arrow from \(M\) to full fine-tuning is \(\rho_M\) itself (Obs I.4(a)). Image inclusion gives only a map of sets over \(\Theta\), which need not be smooth (Obs I.4(c)).

(expand)

The only pointed map from the one-point space into \(Q\) is \(*\mapsto q_0\), and it lies over \(\Theta\) because \(\rho(q_0)=\theta_0\). So the frozen model is initial. An arrow \(h\) from \(M\) into \((\Theta,\theta_0,\mathrm{id})\) must satisfy \(\mathrm{id}\circ h=\rho_M\), so it exists and is unique. For the gap between sets and smooth maps, take \(\rho_M(t)=t\) and \(\rho_N(u)=u^3\) on \(\mathbb R\), both pointed at \(0\). The images agree, and the only map with \(\rho_N\circ h=\rho_M\) is the cube root, which is not differentiable at \(0\).

∎

Every weight-space method pointed at \(\theta_0\) lies between the frozen model and full fine-tuning, and in exact arithmetic on a full-precision base its merge is that canonical arrow. Merging questions remain for extensions such as adapters, prefixes and ReFT (reading VI), for quantised bases (Prop V.2) and for combinations of adapters (Prop V.4).

More: a first-order test, and defaults that start elsewhere

The first-order image \(S_1\) detects part of this gap. In the cube-root example \(S_1(M)=\mathbb R\) is not inside \(S_1(N)=0\), so no smooth simulation exists (Obs I.6). Several initialisations start away from \(\theta_0\) (HF’s default FourierFT spectrum and VB-LoRA bank, init_lora_weights=False, LoRA-XS’s small random \(R\), quantised bases) and carry a pointing defect (Exposé V).

II. “The effective number of parameters”

Entries 6 and 11 (Exposé II).
PEFT expressivity, the effective number of parameters; redundant parameters.
Category theory · geometry the image germ of \(\rho\) at \(\theta_0\) and its dimension \(d(M)\); the gauge group (automorphisms of \(Q\) over \(\Theta\)) and the generic fibre.

A parameter count measures \(Q\), while expressivity lives in the image. The difference is spent on the fibres of \(\rho\), along which the gauge group moves. LoRA spends \(r^2\) parameters there, since \((Bg^{-1},gA)\) and \((B,A)\) give the same weights for every \(g\in GL_r\).

With \(\lvert M\rvert=\dim Q\) and \(d(M)=\dim\operatorname{Im}M\) (Def I.5), \(d(M)\le\lvert M\rvert-\dim(\text{generic gauge orbit})\), with equality when the gauge is locally transitive on generic fibres (Prop II.12(a)). For LoRA\(_r\) on an \(m\times n\) box with \(r\le\min(m,n)\), \(d=r(m+n-r)\). Compare LoHa\(_{r,r}\) with LoRA\(_{2r}\) at their shared budget \(2r(m+n)\), with \(2r\le\min(m,n)\). If \(2r^2\lt m+n-1\), LoHa reaches at least \((m+n-1)-2r^2\) fewer dimensions; if moreover \(r^2\le\min(m,n)\), it trades them for rank up to \(r^2\) when \(r\ge3\), and lies strictly inside LoRA\(_{2r}\) when \(r\le2\) (Prop II.12(c)). If \(2r^2\ge m+n-1\), as for \(r=32\) on layers 320 to 640 wide, LoHa matches or beats LoRA\(_{2r}\) in rank, and in dimension by its numerically checked dimension formula. Rank, in turn, is bounded by a minimum cut of the method’s tensor network (Thm II.10).

(expand)

The generic fibre contains the generic gauge orbit, and \(d=\lvert M\rvert-\dim(\text{generic fibre})\). For LoRA, the full-rank factorisations of a rank-\(r\) matrix form a single \(GL_r\)-orbit on which \(GL_r\) acts freely, so the generic fibre has dimension \(r^2\). For LoHa\(_{r_1,r_2}\), the product \(B_1A_1\odot B_2A_2\) is preserved by \(GL_{r_1}\times GL_{r_2}\) and by the diagonal torus \((B_1,A_1,B_2,A_2)\mapsto(D_1B_1,A_1D_2,D_1^{-1}B_2,A_2D_2^{-1})\), since \((D_1XD_2)\odot(D_1^{-1}YD_2^{-1})=X\odot Y\). At generic parameters the joint stabiliser is one-dimensional. Indeed \(D_1B_1g_1^{-1}=B_1\) makes every row of \(B_1\) a left eigenvector of \(g_1\), and \(m\ge r_1+1\) rows in general position force \(g_1=cI\) and \(D_1=cI\); the other three equations then give \(D_2=g_2=c^{-1}I\). So the orbits have dimension \(r_1^2+r_2^2+m+n-1\). At \(r_1=r_2=r\) this bounds \(d(\mathrm{LoHa}_{r,r})\) by \(2r(m+n)-2r^2-(m+n-1)\), and subtracting it from \(d(\mathrm{LoRA}_{2r})=2r(m+n)-4r^2\) gives the gap. The rank bound \(r^2\) comes from \(\operatorname{rank}(X\odot Y)\le\operatorname{rank}X\cdot\operatorname{rank}Y\), and it is attained when \(r^2\le\min(m,n)\). For \(r\le2\), LoHa\(_{r,r}\) maps into LoRA\(_{r^2}\), whose image lies in that of LoRA\(_{2r}\); when the gap is positive, the containment is strict.

∎

Report \(d(M)\) beside \(\lvert M\rvert\) in every comparison (Prediction X.10).

CreditLoHa is the Hadamard-product parametrisation of FedPara (Hyeon-Woo et al., 2021). Its dimension bound is proved, and equality is checked numerically (Exposé XI).

III. “The LoRA+ ratio, \(\alpha\), the init scale, rsLoRA”

Entry 23 (Exposé III).
PEFT the LoRA+ ratio, \(\alpha\), the initial scale, rsLoRA.
Category theory · geometry coordinates on the orbit space of a hyperparameter torus.

Multiplying \(B\) by \(c\gt0\) and dividing the scale \(s\) by \(c\) leaves the weights unchanged, and Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\) ignores a positive rescaling of a block’s gradient history. Rescaling the block’s learning rate too then reproduces the run step for step, and the per-block factors form a torus acting on the hyperparameters.

Theorem III.10 makes this exact for any multihomogeneous method, assuming no parameter-dependent regulariser, no global clipping, the same data order and dropout masks, and exact arithmetic. For LoRA (Corollary III.11(b)), under Adam with \(\varepsilon=0\), no weight decay or clipping and one schedule for both groups, LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) at scale \(s\) produces exactly the trajectory of plain LoRA at scale \(\lambda s\), with the same \(\eta_A\) and \(A_0\). For \(\varepsilon\gt0\) this needs \(\varepsilon_B'=\lambda\varepsilon\), and with AdamW a decay \(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{wd}\) on \(B\). Under SGD the scale is \(\sqrt\lambda\,s\).

(expand)

Take \(c_B=1/\lambda\) and \(c_A=1\), so that \(B'=c_BB\) and \(s'=s/c_B=\lambda s\). LoRA+ has \(\eta_B=\lambda\eta_A\), and the rescaled run is asked to use \(\eta_B'=c_B\eta_B=\eta_A\), which is plain LoRA. The start \(B_0=0\) stays \(0\). At corresponding points \(\nabla_{B'}=c_B^{-1}\nabla_B\). With \(\varepsilon=0\), Adam’s step does not change when a block’s whole gradient history is multiplied by a positive constant, so the step in \(B'\) is \(\eta_B'/\eta_B\) times the step in \(B\), and \(\Delta B'=c_B\Delta B\) holds exactly when \(\eta_B'=c_B\eta_B\). The weights agree at step \(0\), and induction over steps carries the agreement along the whole run. With \(\varepsilon\gt0\) the same computation asks for \(\varepsilon_B'=\varepsilon/c_B\), and decoupled decay for \(\mathrm{wd}_B'=\mathrm{wd}/c_B\). Under SGD the gradient factor \(c_B^{-1}\) and the required \(c_B\) combine into \(\eta_B'=c_B^2\eta_B\), which gives \(c_B=\lambda^{-1/2}\) and scale \(\sqrt\lambda\,s\).

∎

Only the torus orbit of \((s,\eta_A,\eta_B,\sigma_A)\) matters. Under Adam it is fixed by \(s\eta_A\eta_B\) and \(\sigma_A/\eta_A\) (Cor III.11(a)), so at a fixed rank LoRA+, rsLoRA and \(\alpha\)-tuning are one family. A gain of LoRA+ over LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\) needs something that breaks the torus, such as global-norm clipping (a Hugging Face Trainer default), an \(\varepsilon\) or decay shared across groups, or different schedules (Prediction X.1). Figure 7 runs both trainings.

CreditThe LoRA and Adam case, with its \(\varepsilon\gt0\) extension and the two effective hyperparameters, is due to Schulman et al., 2025, for the method of Hayou et al., 2024. The torus for any multihomogeneous method and the \(c^2\) law for SGD are added here.
More: the same fibres under gradient flow

Entry 22 reads the same fibres under gradient flow. With block-scalar rates the gauge yields the conserved charge \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\) (Thm III.5), after Du, Hu and Lee, 2018, Arora et al., 2018, Zhao et al., 2023 and Marcotte, Gribonval and Peyré, 2023. Balanced points \(B^\top B=AA^\top\) form the Kempf–Ness slice (Prop III.7; Kempf and Ness, 1979; Richardson and Slodowy, 1990).

IV. “GaLore versus LoRA”

Entry 25 (Exposé IV).
PEFT GaLore versus LoRA.
Category theory · geometry cotangent-side and tangent-side lens 2-cells, which coincide for linear charts (the degree-one collapse).

Backpropagation makes each map a lens, running forward from parameters to outputs and backward from output gradients to parameter gradients. LoRA restricts the forward side. GaLore compresses the gradient before the optimizer and expands its output after.

Let \(\rho_k(C)=W_k+\mathcal LC\) with \(\mathcal L\) linear and \(C_0=0\). Let the optimizer’s state and output depend only on the gradients fed to it, with one implementation on both sides (SGD, heavy-ball or Nesterov momentum, Adam without weight decay, Adafactor without parameter scaling). Then training \(C\) and merging at the end of a period gives the same \(W\)-trajectory as the backward method with compression \(\mathcal L^*\) and anchor \(\mathcal L\), started at \(W_k\) from the same optimizer state (Prop IV.2). Decoupled weight decay, LAMB and LARS trust ratios, parameter-scaled Adafactor and full-gradient clipping break the identity.

(expand)

By induction on the step, the weights of the second procedure equal \(W_k+\mathcal LC_t\), where \(C_t\) is the state of the first. Both procedures feed the optimizer the same sequence \(\mathcal L^*\nabla L(W_t)\) from the same state, so they receive the same outputs \(U_t\). One sets \(W\leftarrow W-\eta\,\mathcal LU_t\), and the other sets \(C\leftarrow C-\eta\,U_t\), which moves \(W_k+\mathcal LC\) by the same amount. The excluded ingredients see different tensors in the two procedures. Decoupled decay acts on \(C\) in one and on \(W\) in the other, parameter-scaled steps read the size of \(C\) in one and of \(W\) in the other, and full-gradient clipping uses \(\lVert G\rVert\) in one and \(\lVert\mathcal L^*G\rVert\) in the other.

∎

Under these hypotheses, GaLore with a projector \(P_k\) fixed on each period is one-sided LoRA \(W_k+P_kC\), merged and restarted every period with \(C\)’s optimizer state carried over unchanged. It adds two things to one-sided LoRA, a frame from the gradient’s SVD and that carry-over, which reads moments written for \(P_{k-1}\) as if written for \(P_k\). LDAdam transports them instead.

CreditThe identity for GaLore (Zhao et al., 2024) is due to Torroba-Hennigen, Lang, Guo and Kim, 2025, and its SGD case with a frozen random factor to Hao, Cao and Mou, 2024.
More: the rest of the cotangent family

Under the same hypotheses the identity (Prop IV.2, Prop IV.3) covers LoRA-FA and GaLore with random projectors, MeZO up to its finite-difference estimate, GaLore with gradient SVDs, and BitFit and LISA (without weight decay) with coordinate blocks. Flora as published, APOLLO and Fira update outside their projected subspace and are not covered.

V. “QLoRA and LoftQ”

Entry 28 (Exposé V).
PEFT QLoRA, LoftQ.
Category theory · geometry base change along a map that does not preserve the function; a pointing defect; an approximate section.

A quantiser \(\kappa\) moves the pretrained point to \(\kappa\theta_0\) and changes the function. Additive methods commute with translation, \(M(\theta+v)=\tau_v\circ M(\theta)\), so QLoRA is LoRA attached at \(\kappa\theta_0\), with pointing defect \(e=\kappa\theta_0-\theta_0\) (Def V.1), and \(\theta_0\) lies outside its image unless \(\operatorname{rank}e\le r\) (Prop V.2(a)). Splitting initialisations come in three orders with three different defects (Prop V.2(b)). Write \(W\) for the full-precision weight, \(\delta_0\) for the initial adapter, \(W_{\rm res}=W-\delta_0\) and \(s=\alpha/r\).

OrderUsed byPointing defect
split, then quantise the residualQPiSSA; HF’s PiSSA and CorDA workflow\(\kappa(W_{\rm res})-W_{\rm res}\)
quantise, then fit the errorLoftQ with one iteration (HF default loftq_iter=1)\(e-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\), as HF does not divide the fit by \(s\); at \(s=1\), \(e\) minus its best rank-\(r\) approximation
quantise, split, re-quantiseHF olora_init on bitsandbytes weights\(e+\kappa(r')-r'\), with \(r'=\kappa W-\delta_0\)
(expand)

Translation is the definition of additivity. The image of LoRA\(_r\) pointed at \(\kappa\theta_0\) is \(\kappa\theta_0+\mathcal M_{\le r}\), which contains \(\theta_0\) iff \(-e\in\mathcal M_{\le r}\), that is, iff \(\operatorname{rank}e\le r\). For the table, compute \(\rho(q_0)-W\) in each pipeline. Splitting first gives \(\kappa(W_{\rm res})+\delta_0-W=\kappa(W_{\rm res})-W_{\rm res}\). Fitting the error stores the factors of \(-\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\), which the forward pass multiplies by \(s\), so \(\delta_0=-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\) and the defect is \(\kappa W-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)-W=e-s\,\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\). Re-quantising the split residual gives \(\kappa(r')+\delta_0-W=e+\kappa(r')-r'\).

∎

Measured from its own start, QLoRA obeys the rank bound of any LoRA (Thm II.1(c)); measured from \(\theta_0\), its update also contains the generically full-rank defect. Since \(e-\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\) is orthogonal to \(\mathrm{SVD}_r(e)\), one-step LoftQ in HF beats QLoRA in Frobenius norm only for \(0\lt s\lt2\). Further LoftQ iterations need not decrease their objective, and at \(s=1\) the output is an approximate section, pointed up to its attained residual (Prop V.2(c)).

CreditQLoRA, Dettmers et al., 2023; LoftQ, Li et al., 2023; PiSSA and QPiSSA, Meng et al., 2024.

VI. “Merging an adapter into the weights”

Entry 5 (Exposé VI).
PEFT merging an adapter into the weights; zero inference latency.
Category theory · geometry for weight-space methods, the canonical arrow to the terminal object; for extensions, a lift along the realisation \(\Phi_f\), certified box by box by rewriting.

Reading I settled merging for weight-space methods on a full-precision base. Adapters, prefixes and (IA)\(^3\) scalings insert new boxes and live on an extension of \(f\), so merging one is a lifting problem along the realisation \(\theta\mapsto f(\theta,-)\).

A method \(\rho\) on an extension \(\tilde f\) is mergeable if some \(\mu:Q\to\Theta\) has \(\Phi_{\tilde f}\circ\rho=\Phi_f\circ\mu\) (Def VI.1). Theorem VI.3 gives sufficient conditions box by box, through seven rewrite rules with side conditions such as position-uniform edits, unshared absorbing weights and bias slots for shifts. For instance, HF applies the feed-forward vector \(\ell\) of (IA)\(^3\) to the input of \(W_{\rm down}\), where it always fuses. It fuses into \(W_{\rm up}\) instead under SwiGLU or GeGLU for every \(\ell\), and under ReLU when \(\ell\ge0\). Under GELU, an entry \(\ell_i\) on a nonzero row of \(W_{\rm up}\) fuses there only if it is \(0\) or \(1\).

(expand)

The scaling of the input of \(W_{\rm down}\) is absorbed as \(W_{\rm down}\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\) by fusion (R1), whatever the activation. In a gated block the hidden activation is \(\mathrm{silu}(W_gx)\odot W_ux\), and the spider slide (R4), \(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)(a\odot b)=a\odot(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)b)\), moves the scaling onto the linear branch, so \(W_u\mapsto\mathrm{diag}(\ell)W_u\) for every \(\ell\). Under ReLU the positive-diagonal slide (R3), \(\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\sigma^{\times d}=\sigma^{\times d}\mathrm{diag}(\ell)\) for \(\ell\ge0\), does the same; this slide holds exactly for positively homogeneous \(\sigma\). GELU is not positively homogeneous. Absorbing \(\ell_i\ne0\) into a nonzero row \(w\) of \(W_{\rm up}\) would need a row \(w'\) with \(\ell_i\,\mathrm{GELU}(w\cdot x)=\mathrm{GELU}(w'\cdot x)\) for all \(x\). The left side is constant along directions orthogonal to \(w\), and the right side is not unless \(w'\) is parallel to \(w\), so \(w'=cw\), and then \(\ell_i\,\mathrm{GELU}(t)=\mathrm{GELU}(ct)\) for all \(t\). Since \(\mathrm{GELU}(t)=t/2+t^2/\sqrt{2\pi}+O(t^4)\), the coefficients of \(t\) and \(t^2\) give \(c=\ell_i\) and \(\ell_i=\ell_i^2\). With biases the same argument gives \(\ell_i\,\mathrm{GELU}(u)=\mathrm{GELU}(cu+\beta)\), and \(u=0\) forces \(\beta=0\), because GELU vanishes only at \(0\). An entry \(\ell_i=0\) is absorbed by zeroing its row, since \(\mathrm{GELU}(0)=0\).

∎

“Zero inference latency” is certified by local rewriting, a sufficient test that also names the absorbing weight; the \(W_{\rm up}\) route is idle in practice, as \(W_{\rm down}\) always works. Nonlinear adapters, input-routed mixtures and prefixes do not merge box-locally. At its layer a prefix is a gated parallel adapter (Thm VI.5(b)), not box-locally mergeable for generic frozen weights and prefixes (Thm VI.5(f)); whole-network merging of prefixes is open.

CreditThe (IA)\(^3\) merge is noted by Liu et al., 2022, and the gated-adapter reading of prefix tuning is due to He et al., 2022. Scale migration through SwiGLU, ReLU and norm layers, with the GELU obstruction, already appears in SmoothQuant (Xiao et al., 2023) and AWQ (Lin et al., 2024). The activation symmetries behind slides R2 and R3 (Lemma VI.2) are classical (Sussmann, 1992; Chen, Lu and Hecht-Nielsen, 1993; Godfrey et al., 2022).

Reading from the right

Some mathematical constructions are methods already in use.

  • The product \(\mathrm{FA}(A_0)\times\mathrm{FB}(B_0)\) of the two one-sided LoRAs is the sandwich \(R\mapsto W_0+B_0RA_0\). In the SVD frames of \(W_0\), with \(\sigma_r\gt0\), it is LoRA-XS pointed at \(R=0\) (Prop I.8(a); Bałazy et al., 2024).
  • For injective \(W_0\) and even \(r\) with \(2\le r\le n-2\), LoRA’s image meets the rotation orbit \(W_0\cdot SO(n)\) exactly in the image of HRA without Gram–Schmidt, the HF default (Thm II.7; Yuan et al., 2024). This is a meet of sets, and HRA is not the categorical product.
  • Transport along a fixed \(T\in GL(\Theta)\) is an automorphism of the slice. It carries coordinate masks to FourierFT (with no conjugate frequency pair sampled), WaveFT (when its padded inverse wavelet transform is invertible) and C3A, each started at zero, and to LoRA-XS and SVFT in the SVD frame of \(W_0\), which depends on \(\theta_0\). The inverse of the rearrangement of Van Loan and Pitsianis, 1993 carries LoRA\(_k\) to unconstrained sums of \(k\) Kronecker products (Prop II.13).
  • The monoidal sum \(\oplus\) adds updates, so its image is a Minkowski sum. RoSA, GraLoRA and the stages of ReLoRA, unrolled in time, are words in \(\oplus\) (Obs I.7).

Products and meets can be tried on other pairs too. Meets of images always exist as sets of weights, while products can fail to exist (Obs I.4(b)). Which meets admit a polynomial chart, as LoRA-XS and HRA’s image do, is an open problem.

Exposé X–XI

Predictions, analogies and open problems

Eleven experiments that could show the framework wrong, each stated for a named optimizer, then a plain account of which borrowed words carry theorems, what is open, whose results are re-derived here, and what the referees changed.

Say what you proved, say what you only named, and say what would show you wrong.

a working maxim
In one minute
  • A fair LoRA+ baseline multiplies \(\alpha\) by the ratio \(\lambda\). Under Adam, with \(\varepsilon\), decay and per-block clipping thresholds rescaled and one shared schedule, the two runs give identical weights (after Schulman et al., 2025).
  • LoRA’s charge \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\) is constant under gradient flow and, when a step size \(\eta\) multiplies fixed block rates, moves by \(O(\eta^2)\) per SGD step, a cheap diagnostic of what the optimizer adds.
  • Some claims need no training run. Generic rank-one targets separate LoRA from Kronecker adapters, exact block OFT re-merged on a fixed partition never leaves \(W_0\cdot\prod SO(b)\), and LoraHub’s merge depends on gauge choices unless modules share a factor.
  • Referees repaired 27 of the 41 results, and a second review narrowed 19 of those again.

Why the theory should risk something

A framework that only re-describes known methods cannot be wrong, and so it earns little trust. The claims below say what a training run will do. Each inherits the optimizer scope of the result it rests on; a claim about images needs none, because no run can leave the image.

№PredictionOptimizer scopeRests onKind
X.1LoRA+ against \(\alpha\) re-tuned by \(\lambda\)Adam, AdamWThm III.10, Cor III.11theorem
X.2DoRA against LoRA + output gainAdamW, shared by all armsProp II.8, Conj Dconjecture
X.3Hidden-unit permutationscoordinatewise (SGD, Adam)Lemma VI.2, Obs II.15theorem + data
X.4Residual-stream rotationsreach: any; outcomes: depend on optimizer and initObs II.15, Conj Aconjecture
X.5Rank-one targetsany (image claim)Thm II.11theorem
X.6Re-based OFTany (reach); exact Cayley mapThm V.3theorem
X.7The crossover scale \(\sigma^*\)gradient flow, SGDThm III.9theorem + data
X.8Drift of the charge \(\Phi\)gradient flow, SGD, AdamThm III.5theorem + data
X.9LoraHub with shared or independent seedspost hoc; regime set by trainingProp V.4theorem + data
X.10Rankings at equal effective dimensionany (bookkeeping)Prop II.12theorem + data
X.11Whitened data-aware splitssplit: any; training is not covariantProp II.16, Conj Cconjecture

Read the kind column as a warning label. Theorem means the sharp half cannot fail inside its hypotheses. Theorem + data pairs a proved mechanism with an empirical claim about its size. Conjecture means we expect it and have not proved it. The toys check mechanisms in float64 on small matrices; the experiments on real models are still to be run.

Predictions about optimizers

LoRA has four knobs, the scale \(s=\alpha/r\), the rates \(\eta_A,\eta_B\) and the initial size \(\sigma_A\) of \(A\). A two-dimensional torus of rescalings moves all four without changing any weight. LoRA+ turns one knob, the ratio \(\lambda=\eta_B/\eta_A\), and under Adam that turn lands on the orbit of plain LoRA with a larger \(\alpha\).

Prediction X.1 (LoRA+ against a re-tuned \(\alpha\)). Any gain of LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) over plain LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by \(\lambda\) (same \(\eta_A\), same \(A_0\)) comes from the ingredients that break the extended torus: global-norm clipping, an \(\varepsilon\) or weight decay shared by the two parameter groups, and schedules that differ between the groups. With all of them off, or rescaled per block, the two runs produce the same weights at every step in exact arithmetic.
Scope
Adam and AdamW (\(\beta_1\in[0,1)\), \(\beta_2\in(0,1)\), bias correction allowed); linear LoRA modules from \(B_0=0\); no regulariser on the factors; common data order and dropout masks. A shared schedule, warm-up included, preserves the torus. HF PEFT’s LoRA+ optimizer treats embeddings and 1-D parameters separately, so only linear modules are covered.
Support
Thm III.10 and Cor III.11(b), both verified. For \(\varepsilon\gt0\) the LoRA run needs \(\varepsilon_B=\lambda\varepsilon\) on \(B\), and with AdamW a decay \(\lambda\cdot\mathrm{wd}\) on \(B\). Under SGD the matching scale is \(\sqrt\lambda\,s\).
Test
Train LoRA+ with \(\lambda=16\) against LoRA with \(\alpha\) multiplied by 16, \(\varepsilon\) on \(A\) and \(16\varepsilon\) on \(B\), no decay and no clipping; the weights agree bit for bit, since 16 is a power of two. Then switch on one torus breaker at a time (shared \(\varepsilon\), shared AdamW decay, max_grad_norm=1.0, group-specific schedules) and record the gap each opens.
Would fail if
a gap beyond rounding appeared with every hypothesis met. A standard HF run clips by default and its LoRA+ optimizer shares \(\varepsilon\) and decay, so there the experiment measures how much of the gain each breaker carries.
Toy
Orbit explorer: two float64 runs of an \(8\times6\) regression, whose gap \(\max_t\lVert W_t-W'_t\rVert_F\) reads exactly 0 on the orbit and turns nonzero when \(\varepsilon\), decay or clipping is switched on.
More: why the test avoids \(\varepsilon=0\)

Literal \(\varepsilon=0\) needs the convention \(0/0=0\). At \(B_0=0\) the gradient of \(A\) vanishes, and a stock Adam divides zero by zero there.

CreditThe LoRA/Adam case, its extension to \(\varepsilon\gt0\) and the two effective hyperparameters are due to Schulman et al., 2025, for the method of Hayou et al., 2024. The torus for any multihomogeneous method, and the \(c^2\) law for SGD, are added here.

Under gradient flow LoRA conserves the charge \(\Phi=B^\top B/\eta_B-AA^\top/\eta_A\), and when \(\Phi\) is isotropic the dynamics have a closed form. Small singular values of the update grow through \(B\) alone, as in LoRA-FA; large ones grow through both factors in balance.

Prediction X.7 (the crossover scale). Under SGD, the singular values of \(\Delta W\) switch from one-sided to two-sided growth as they cross \(\sigma^*=\mu\lvert\kappa\rvert/2\), where \(\mu=s\sqrt{\eta_A\eta_B}\) and \(\Phi=-\kappa I_r\). For default LoRA with \(B_0=0\) and \(A_0A_0^\top=cI\), \(\kappa=c/\eta_A\) and \(\sigma^*=\tfrac{sc}2\sqrt{\eta_B/\eta_A}\). LoRA+ with ratio \(\lambda\) multiplies \(\sigma^*\) by \(\sqrt\lambda\).
Scope
Gradient flow exactly; SGD without momentum up to the \(O(\eta^2)\) drift of the charge per step. For Kaiming initialisations \(A_0A_0^\top\) is only close to \(cI\) (HF’s default has \(c\approx1/3\)), and the crossover becomes a band of relative width \(O(\sqrt{r/n})\). Adam is not covered.
Support
Thm III.9, verified; known from the linear-network literature, with the PEFT reading ours.
Test
Train with SGD from orthonormal rows of \(A_0\) scaled by \(\sqrt c\), so that the charge is exactly isotropic. Take the SVD \(\Delta W=U\Sigma V^\top\) at intervals and read off \(f_i=u_i^\top(BB^\top/\eta_B)u_i\) and \(g_i=v_i^\top(A^\top A/\eta_A)v_i\). Below \(\sigma^*\), \(f_i\approx0\) and \(g_i\approx\kappa\); above it, \(f_i\approx g_i\approx\sigma_i/\mu\). Repeat at ratio \(\lambda\).
Would fail if
the gains departed from the closed-form values \(f_i=f_\kappa(\sigma_i^2/\mu^2)\) and \(g_i=g_\kappa(\sigma_i^2/\mu^2)\) of Thm III.9, with \(f_\kappa(x)=\sqrt{x+\kappa^2/4}-\kappa/2\) and \(g_\kappa=f_\kappa+\kappa\), while \(\Phi\) stayed isotropic. If \(\Phi\) has drifted, the test says nothing.
Toy
Noether hyperbolas, panel B: \(f_\kappa\), \(g_\kappa\) and \(\sigma^*\) for \(m=n=6\), \(r=2\), with singular values from the integrated flow.
CreditThe closed form is due to Tarmoun, França, Haeffele and Vidal (ICML 2021), who call the charge “\(\lambda\)-balanced”, and to Min, Tarmoun, Vidal and Mallada (ICML 2021); see also Dominé et al., 2024 and Kunin et al., 2024. The balanced regime above \(\sigma^*\) is the dynamics of Arora, Cohen and Hazan, 2018.

LoRA+ moves \(\sigma^*\) up by \(\sqrt\lambda\), so more of the update spends longer in the LoRA-FA regime. PiSSA at equal rates has \(\kappa=0\), and under gradient flow it is balanced from the first step.

Part of LoRA’s gauge is a symmetry of \(\rho\), and gradient flow conserves its charge whatever signal drives it. A discrete step conserves it up to second order in the step size; nothing forces Adam’s sign-like step to.

Prediction X.8 (drift of the charge). \(\Phi\) is constant under gradient flow, moves by \(O(\eta^2)\) per SGD step at fixed block rates \(\eta_A,\eta_B\), and drifts under Adam. We expect its drift under Adam to track other measures of how far Adam breaks LoRA’s gauge.
Scope
Gradient flow driven by any signal \(\Lambda D\rho^\top\gamma\), which covers minibatches, LoRA dropout, global-norm clipping and DoRA’s detached norm; SGD without momentum or weight decay. Momentum, Adam and AdamW fall outside Thm III.5.
Support
Thm III.5 (repaired; known after Zhao et al., 2023); weight decay moves the charge on purpose (Cor III.6). A step \(B\leftarrow B-\eta\eta_B\nabla_B\), \(A\leftarrow A-\eta\eta_A\nabla_A\) changes the charge by exactly \[\Delta\Phi=\eta^2\big(\eta_B\,\nabla_B^\top\nabla_B-\eta_A\,\nabla_A\nabla_A^\top\big).\]
Test
Log \(\Phi\) during training. Under SGD at step sizes \(\eta\), \(\eta/2\), \(\eta/4\), the per-step change should match the formula to rounding and shrink about fourfold each time. Under Adam, compare \(\lVert\Phi_t-\Phi_0\rVert\) (our suggestion) with the gap between runs started at \(A_0\) and \(gA_0\), \(g\notin B_r\), which by Cor III.13 can differ.
Would fail if
the SGD drift departed from the formula, so that the update was not a plain gradient step, or \(\Phi\)-drift under Adam showed no relation to any other measure of gauge breaking.
Toy
Noether hyperbolas, panel A: scalar LoRA rides its hyperbola under the flow, wobbles under GD and drifts under Adam.
(expand)

Write \(g_B=\nabla_B=sGA^\top\) and \(g_A=\nabla_A=sB^\top G\) for one weight-space gradient \(G\). Expanding the step,

\[\tfrac1{\eta_B}B^{+\top}B^+=\tfrac1{\eta_B}B^\top B-\eta\big(g_B^\top B+B^\top g_B\big)+\eta^2\eta_B\,g_B^\top g_B,\] \[\tfrac1{\eta_A}A^+A^{+\top}=\tfrac1{\eta_A}AA^\top-\eta\big(g_AA^\top+Ag_A^\top\big)+\eta^2\eta_A\,g_Ag_A^\top.\]

The first-order terms agree, since \(g_B^\top B=sAG^\top B=Ag_A^\top\) and \(B^\top g_B=sB^\top GA^\top=g_AA^\top\). They cancel in the difference, which leaves \(\Delta\Phi=\eta^2(\eta_Bg_B^\top g_B-\eta_Ag_Ag_A^\top)\). The argument uses only that both gradients come from one \(G\), so it covers minibatches, LoRA dropout on the input of \(A\), and global clipping when \(g_A,g_B\) denote the gradients actually applied.

∎
CreditThe conservation law, with its symmetric and antisymmetric halves, is Zhao et al., 2023, Prop. 5.1; laws independent of loss and data are in Marcotte, Gribonval and Peyré, 2023. The principle goes back to Du, Hu and Lee, 2018, Arora, Cohen and Hazan, 2018 and Kunin et al., 2021.

Predictions about images

DoRA splits each output neuron into a length and a direction. At the level of images that adds exactly one free gain per output neuron to LoRA, so anything else that separates the two happens in training, where Adam notices the coordinates a parameter is written in.

Prediction X.2 (DoRA against LoRA + output gain). Conj D. Started from the same weights and trained with AdamW at the same settings, LoRA followed by a trainable output gain matches DoRA once the gain is trained in DoRA’s coordinates (\(\mu_i\) starting at \(\lVert w_{0,i}\rVert\) in place of \(\ell_i\) starting at 1).
Scope
AdamW, as in the DoRA paper and HF PEFT, with one learning rate, decay and data order for every arm. Images refer to the merged weight, with \(r\le\min(m,n)\) and \(W_0\) free of zero rows. Every arm trains \(r(m+n)+m\) parameters.
Support
Prop II.8 (repaired): \(\operatorname{Im}\mathrm{DoRA}_r=\mathrm{Diag}_m\cdot(W_0+\mathcal M_{\le r})\), the image of LoRA\(_r\) followed by an output gain. The first Conj D, that any gap comes from the parametrisation, followed from this equality and could not fail.
Test
Start four arms at \(W_0\) with the same \(A_0\): (i) LoRA with an output gain, \(\ell_0=\mathbf 1\); (ii) the gain in DoRA’s coordinates, \(W=\mathrm{diag}(\mu_i/\lVert w_{0,i}\rVert)(W_0+sBA)\), \(\mu_{0,i}=\lVert w_{0,i}\rVert\); (iii) DoRA with the exact gradient of its normalisation; (iv) DoRA with the detached norm of the DoRA paper (Sec. 4.3) and HF PEFT (weight_norm.detach()). This design is ours.
Would fail if
arm (ii) stayed clearly behind arm (iv). Comparing (iii) with (iv) then shows how much of the gap the detached backward carries.
Toy
None for training. The Gram lab shows the image-level half: at \(B=0\) DoRA acts as an output torus, and its update escapes rank \(r\).

A rank-one update writes one new feature into many neurons, and LoRA does this with a single rank. A Kronecker sum measures rank across a different cut of the same four-leg tensor, where, for the splits HF PEFT uses, a generic rank-one update is as complex as any matrix can be.

Prediction X.5 (rank-one targets). On targets \(W_0+uv^\top\) with generic \(u,v\), and a loss whose only minimiser is the target, LoRA\(_1\) can reach zero loss while no sum of fewer than \(\kappa_1=\min(m_1,m_2)\min(n_1,n_2)\) Kronecker products can. For HF PEFT’s splits \(\kappa_1\) is the largest possible Kronecker rank: \(d\) for its square splits of \(d=1024\) or \(4096\), 576 for its \(24\times32\) split of 768. For LoRA ranks below \(\min(m_1,n_1)\min(m_2,n_2)\) and fewer than \(\kappa_1\) terms, neither image contains the other.
Scope
The negative half concerns images and holds for every optimizer; the positive half asks the optimizer to find a point that exists.
Support
Thm II.11 (repaired), for unconstrained Kronecker sums and for LoKr, since \(C\otimes BA\) is one Kronecker term. With Schmidt decompositions \(u=\sum_i\sigma_ix_i\otimes y_i\), \(v=\sum_j\tau_jz_j\otimes w_j\), the best \(k\)-term sum leaves the squared error \(\sum\sigma_i^2\tau_j^2\) over all but the \(k\) largest products.
Test
On one linear layer with whitened inputs and more samples than parameters (or the loss \(\tfrac12\lVert W-W_0-uv^\top\rVert_F^2\)), train LoRA\(_1\) and sums of \(k=1,\dots,\kappa_1\) unconstrained Kronecker products, each with its own learning-rate sweep, and plot the final loss against \(k\) beside the floor.
Would fail if
a sum of fewer than \(\kappa_1\) terms went below the floor. LoRA\(_1\) stalling above zero would be an optimization failure, against which the framework claims nothing.
Toy
Cut the tensor: the \(uv^\top\) preset on \(16\times16\) lights one ordinary singular value and \(\kappa_1\) Kronecker singular values.
(expand)

The Van Loan–Pitsianis rearrangement \(\mathcal R\) permutes the entries of a matrix. It is therefore a linear isometry for the Frobenius norm, and by Thm II.11(a) it carries the set of \(k\)-term Kronecker sums onto the matrices of rank at most \(k\). The distance from \(uv^\top\) to that set equals the distance from \(\mathcal R(uv^\top)\) to the rank-\(\le k\) matrices, which by Eckart–Young is the root of the sum of the squared singular values beyond the \(k\)-th. The proof of Thm II.11(c) shows that the singular values of \(\mathcal R(uv^\top)\) are the products \(\sigma_i\tau_j\).

∎
CreditThe rearrangement and the nearest-Kronecker-product problem are Van Loan and Pitsianis, 1993. The PEFT reading, that Kronecker sums are LoRA across another cut, is ours. Kronecker adapters in practice: KronA, LoKr.

Parameter count measures what a method pays, expressive dimension what it can reach. The difference is gauge, parameters that move without moving the weights. It ranges from one parameter for VeRA through \(r^2\) for LoRA\(_r\) to at least \(2r^2+m+n-1\) for LoHa\(_{r,r}\), which has the budget of LoRA\(_{2r}\).

Prediction X.10 (effective dimension). Benchmarks should report the expressive dimension \(d(M)\) beside the budget \(\lvert M\rvert\). At budgets of a few thousand parameters, rankings at equal effective dimension can differ from rankings at equal parameter count.
Scope
Optimizer-free bookkeeping; the change of ranking is the empirical claim.
Support
Prop II.12 (repaired). LoRA\(_r\) wastes \(r^2\) parameters, VeRA one (numerically; for \(r\le n\) and generic frozen factors also by C.14) and FourierFT one per sampled conjugate pair. At the budget of LoRA\(_{2r}\), with \(2r\le\min(m,n)\), \(r^2\le\min(m,n)\) and \(2r^2\lt m+n-1\), LoHa\(_{r,r}\) reaches at least \((m+n-1)-2r^2\) fewer dimensions, and is strictly dominated for \(r\le2\). When \(2r^2\ge m+n-1\), as for \(r=32\) on 320- to 640-wide layers, LoHa matches or beats LoRA\(_{2r}\) in dimension too, by its numerically checked dimension formula.
Test
On a small-budget benchmark, compare methods at matched \(\lvert M\rvert\) and at matched \(d(M)\), computing \(d\) by a float64 Jacobian rank where no formula is proved. Report the maximum rank too, and name its cut.
Would fail if
the two rankings agreed at every budget tested, so that gauge is too small a share of the budget to matter there.
Toy
The budget calculator sets \(\lvert M\rvert\) beside \(d(M)\) on real base models; rank is not volume places one budget’s methods by maximum rank and dimension, with a live Jacobian check.

Predictions about symmetry

The hidden units of a feed-forward block carry no order. Permute them with the matching rows and columns, and the network computes the same function. A method that never looks at unit order should not notice; a method built on a frame, a reshape or a block partition of the hidden coordinates should.

Prediction X.3 (the permutation test). Over random function-preserving permutations of hidden units, LoRA’s outcome variance equals its seed variance. FourierFT, LoKr and MoRA show larger variance, and so do BOFT and OFT on modules whose input side is the hidden layer (down_proj). On up_proj and gate_proj the permutation only reorders BOFT’s output rows, so BOFT is exactly equivariant there.
Scope
The LoRA half holds for any coordinatewise optimizer (SGD, Adam, AdamW), which commutes with coordinate permutations of \(Q\) (the third item of Prop II.13), and an initialisation of \(A\) with i.i.d. entries. The rest is empirical.
Support
Lemma VI.2 (classical): the linear maps that commute with GELU or SiLU are the permutations, with ReLU the positive monomials. Gated SwiGLU or GeGLU blocks admit the full monomial group through their linear branch (R4 of Thm VI.3). Obs II.15 turns covariance under those maps into equal reachable function sets.
Test
Apply \(K\) random permutations to the rows of up_proj and gate_proj and the columns of down_proj, and confirm that outputs agree to rounding. Fine-tune each method once per permuted copy with a fresh seed, and \(K\) times on the original with \(K\) seeds; compare the two variances, method by method.
Would fail if
LoRA’s permutation variance exceeded its seed variance, pointing to an implementation tied to unit order, or the listed methods showed no excess.
Toy
No variance test. The merge game runs the rule behind the lemma: a positive diagonal slides through ReLU and stops at GELU.
CreditLemma VI.2 is classical: Sussmann (1992); Chen, Lu and Hecht-Nielsen (1993); Godfrey et al. (NeurIPS 2022). The methods under test: FourierFT, LoKr, MoRA, BOFT, OFT.

If the prediction holds, seed variance understates the variance of methods tied to unit order across equally good checkpoints.

SliceGPT rotates the residual stream of a model without changing what it computes. A method whose image rotates with the weights reaches the same functions afterwards; a method built on the stream’s coordinates may lose or gain reach.

Prediction X.4 (the rotation test). Take a pre-norm transformer with its norm gains fused into the linear maps they feed and LayerNorm converted to RMSNorm, and rotate its residual stream by one global orthogonal matrix. This provably leaves unchanged the reachable function sets of LoRA, of (IA)\(^3\) with HF defaults, and of DoRA on the input-rotated boxes (\(q\), \(k\), \(v\), gate, up). Conj A. Against the gain-fused, unrotated model, the rotation measurably changes the fine-tuning results of block OFT/BOFT on residual-input projections, LoHa (\(r\ge2\)), HiRA and coordinate masks, because LLM residual streams have a privileged basis.
Scope
The proved half concerns reachable function sets, for every optimizer and for box-wise methods with no shared parameters or random frames. Training outcomes are exactly unchanged only for (IA)\(^3\). For LoRA and DoRA, HF’s Kaiming-uniform \(A\) is not rotation-invariant in law, and Adam is only \(B_r\)-equivariant (Thm III.12). Sandwich and post-norm models (Gemma-2/3, OLMo-2) and SliceGPT’s per-block rotations are excluded.
Support
Obs II.15 (repaired), a sufficient condition. The conjectured methods fail it, which by itself proves nothing.
Test
Keep the gain-fused model as control, rotate a copy, and confirm that outputs agree to rounding. Fine-tune every method on both over several seeds. LoRA’s reach provably does not change, so its change in results measures the share of the optimizer and the initialisation.
Would fail if
the listed methods changed no more than LoRA does.
CreditThe rotation is that of SliceGPT. In QuaRot, the per-head Hadamard fused offline into \(W_v\) and \(W_{\rm out}\) is a value–output head gauge, which Obs II.15 covers; the online Hadamards preserve the function but insert new operations, outside its hypothesis.

PiSSA and CorDA choose their split from a truncated SVD, which follows the coordinates under rotations and uniform scalings and changes when they are stretched unevenly. Whitening the input first removes that dependence from the split.

Prediction X.11 (whitened splits). Conj C. Whitened CorDA or PiSSA is more robust to function-preserving input reparametrisations, and no worse otherwise.
Scope
The covariance of the split is optimizer-free; the robustness of trained results is the empirical part. Absolute damping \(C+\lambda I\) cuts the covariance to \(O(n)\), while CorDA’s trace-relative damping keeps \(CO(n)\). The released CorDA code divides each calibration sample by its largest entry, which breaks even rotations, so only the published formulas are covered.
Support
Prop II.16 (repaired), for \(C=\mathbb E[xx^\top]\succ0\), \(1\le r\lt\min(m,n)\) and spectral gaps. The whitened split \(\Pi_rW_0\), with \(\Pi_r\) the top-\(r\) eigenprojector of \(W_0CW_0^\top\), is \(GL_n\)-covariant on the input side. The PiSSA and MiLoRA splits and the paper-form CorDA splits have input-side group exactly \(CO(n)\), so norm-gain folding or SmoothQuant rescalings change them.
Test
Prepare function-preserving versions of one checkpoint that differ by non-conformal input maps (folded RMSNorm gains, or a per-channel rescaling with its inverse folded into the producer). Initialise PiSSA, paper-form CorDA and the whitened split on each, fine-tune with one optimizer, and compare the spread across versions; compare all three on the unmodified checkpoint for the “no worse” half.
Would fail if
PiSSA and CorDA varied no more than the whitened split across versions, or the whitened split did worse on the unmodified checkpoint.
CreditThe splits are PiSSA, MiLoRA and CorDA; the whitened form is the truncation-aware whitening of SVD-LLM. EVA starts at \(B_0=0\) and splits nothing.

Predictions about base change and merging

Merging and restarting lets a method compose its own moves. For an additive cone such as ReLoRA’s, composition keeps buying rank; for block OFT with a fixed partition, a product of block rotations is again a block rotation.

Prediction X.6 (re-based OFT). With an exact Cayley map and a fixed partition, merge-and-restart OFT reaches nothing beyond the closure of one cycle, \(W_0\cdot\prod SO(b)\). Partitions whose hypergraph is connected generate \(SO(n)\), so re-based exact OFT gains reach only if its partition changes between cycles or its butterflies reach full depth. LoRA-type cones gain from rebasing with a fixed shape; fixed linear shapes (masks, BitFit, (IA)\(^3\)) gain nothing.
Scope
Reachable sets, hence optimizer-free, and silent on trained performance. HF PEFT’s default Cayley–Neumann OFT is not orthogonal; with small generators (\(\lVert Q\rVert_{\rm op}\le2^{1/4}\)) each merge is a contraction, so repeated merging can only shrink neuron norms and singular values. For injective \(W_0\), iterated exact BOFT with \(m'\) butterfly factors reaches \(W_0\cdot SO(n)\), up to its output scale, only at full depth \(b\,2^{m'-1}=n\).
Support
Thm V.3 (repaired), corollaries 2 and 4.
Test
Run \(K\) cycles of merge-and-restart OFT with use_cayley_neumann=False and a fixed partition, logging the partial Grams \(W_{:,J}W_{:,J}^\top\) and the loss. Repeat with a fresh random partition each cycle, and with ReLoRA at the same per-cycle budget. Only the fixed-partition run keeps its partial Grams fixed to rounding.
Would fail if
a partial Gram moved beyond rounding under the fixed partition, so that the map was not exactly orthogonal. Gains in loss from restarts alone are outside the claim.
Toy
Rebasing reach, tab B: block OFT never connects its hypergraph, while the “rotating” scheme connects it and reaches \(SO(16)\).
CreditOFT and BOFT introduced the block and butterfly parametrisations; ReLoRA introduced merge-and-restart for LoRA.

LoraHub mixes adapters factor by factor. A factor belongs to no adapter in particular, since \((B,A)\) and \((cB,A/c)\) give the same update, so the mix depends on choices the adapters do not record unless the modules share a factor.

Prediction X.9 (LoraHub seeds). LoraHub’s factor-wise merge \((\sum_iw_iB_i)(\sum_jw_jA_j)\) behaves like update arithmetic, up to the factor \(\sum_jw_j\), when its modules share the \(A\)-seed and \(A\) stays close to its initialisation. It degrades when the modules come from independent seeds.
Scope
The algebra is post hoc and optimizer-free. Whether \(A\) stays near \(A_0\) is empirical; a large \(\eta_B/\eta_A\), as in LoRA+, favours it.
Support
Prop V.4 (repaired). Replacing \((B_2,A_2)\) by \((cB_2,A_2/c)\) changes the merge by \(\sum_{l\ne2}w_lw_2\big[(c-1)B_2A_l+(c^{-1}-1)B_lA_2\big]\), so it is not a function of the adapters. With one shared frozen \(A\) it returns \((\sum_jw_j)\sum_iw_iB_iA\) exactly. HF’s add_weighted_adapter with linear, and its non-SVD ties and dare_* variants, are factor-wise rules of the same kind.
Test
Train \(N\) modules on \(N\) tasks from a shared \(A\)-seed and from independent seeds. Merge each set by LoraHub’s rule at fixed weights and compare with \((\sum_jw_j)\sum_iw_iB_iA_i\), as matrices and on the downstream metric, logging \(\lVert A_i-A_0\rVert/\lVert A_0\rVert\).
Would fail if
independent seeds merged as well as shared ones, so that the cross terms \(B_iA_j\), \(i\ne j\), cost nothing in practice.
Toy
Gauge lab, tab B: re-gauging one module moves the LoraHub merge and leaves task arithmetic still; “share frozen A” makes the two agree up to \(\sum_jw_j\).
CreditLoraHub (Huang et al., 2023). FFA-LoRA freezes a shared factor; KnOTS re-factorises the modules jointly through a shared \(U\Sigma\). Task arithmetic combines updates and has no gauge to break.

A safe rule combines LoRAs on the products \(B_iA_i\), or on factors only when the modules share one.

One more prediction, and how to read a failure

One expectation is not on the list. By Prop II.13, unconstrained Kronecker sums are LoRA after a rearrangement of entries, and FourierFT (with no sampled conjugate pair), WaveFT (when HF’s pad–IDWT–crop map is invertible) and C3A are coordinate masks in a fixed frame; an optimizer that sees the loss only through the trainable parameters runs each exactly as its source on a transported loss. At matched learning-rate sweeps we therefore expect the gap between a transported method and its source to follow how well each image aligns with the task’s update, measured by projecting a full fine-tuning \(\Delta W\) onto each image.

A failure inside the hypotheses of X.1, X.5, X.6, or of a proved half, would mean an error in a proof, and we would mark that result refuted; a failure outside them shows which hypothesis matters. A failure of X.2, X.4 or X.11 would refute Conjecture D, A or C.

What the borrowed words mean

Words borrowed from geometry and physics compress a great deal, and each also carries connotations no theorem here supports.

Analogy vs theorem (seven borrowed words). For each word, its sense here, the results behind it, and what it leaves out.

Grothendieck

Meaning here
The relative point of view: a method is a map \(\rho:(Q,q_0)\to(\Theta,\theta_0)\), methods form the pointed slice \(\mathsf{Meth}(\Theta,\theta_0)\), and changing \(\theta_0\) is base change.
Proved
Frozen model initial and full fine-tuning terminal; products are fibre products in \(\mathbf{Diff}\) and can fail to exist (Obs I.4); a symmetric monoidal sum (Obs I.7); automorphisms by transport (Prop II.13); pushforward along function-preserving maps (Def V.1(a)).
Not claimed
No schemes, sheaves, descent or stacks. Quantisation and rebasing borrow only the word “base change”, and idle constructions such as \(\int\mathsf{Meth}\to\mathbf{PNet}\) are left out. “Exposé” and the rising sea are homage.

Erlangen

Meaning here
After Klein, a method’s geometry is its covariance group \(\mathcal G_M\) (Def II.14); what a group-type method can never change is a complete invariant of the action.
Proved
Complete invariants of the orthogonal, torus and Hadamard actions (Thm II.5); exactly orthogonal input-side methods keep \(K_{\rm out}\) and the spectrum through any number of merges (Cor II.6, Thm V.3); covariance groups of named methods (Prop II.9(e), Prop II.16).
Not claimed
For cones such as LoRA’s image the word only names the shape and its covariance. Keeping an invariant concerns reach; that it causes knowledge retention is not shown.

Noether

Meaning here
The pattern that turns a continuous symmetry of \(\rho\) into a conserved quantity.
Proved
Thm III.5, for gradient flow driven by any signal \(\Lambda D\rho^\top\gamma\), a mild extension of Zhao et al. (2023); for LoRA the charges are the entries of \(\Phi\).
Not claimed
No Lagrangian. Discrete steps drift by \(O(\eta^2)\) relative to the charge, and Adam and momentum break the law.

Gauge

Meaning here
The symmetry group of a parametrisation (Def I.5); for LoRA, \(GL_r\) acting by \((Bg^{-1},gA)\).
Proved
Generic gauges and fibre dimensions (Prop II.12); the part each optimizer respects, \(B_r\subset O(r)\subset GL_r\) (Thm III.12); the gauge dependence of factor-wise merging (Prop V.4).
Not claimed
No connection or curvature. Fibres are gauge orbits only on the full-rank stratum.

Moduli

Meaning here
A set of isomorphism classes.
Proved
Three strata of full-rank LoRA pointings are classified, as a set, by \(\mathrm{Gr}(r,n)\sqcup\mathrm{Gr}(r,m)\sqcup\mathcal M_r\) (Thm II.2).
Not claimed
No topology (\(\mathrm T_A\) lies in the closure of \(\mathrm S\)) and no stack, and the strata miss some pointings (HF’s orthogonal init, defect pointings).

Fibre

Meaning here
The preimage \(\rho^{-1}(\theta)\), the parameters that give the same weights, as in the site’s title.
Proved
Fibre dimensions (Prop II.12(a)); which points of the apex fibre \(\{BA=0\}\) an optimizer can tell apart (Cor III.13).
Not claimed
\(\rho\) is no fibre bundle; the fibre dimension jumps between strata.

Lens

Meaning here
Backpropagation as the 2-functor \(\mathbf{Para}(\mathsf R)\), which sorts tangent-side from cotangent-side methods.
Proved
The degree-one collapse: GaLore with weight decay 0 is one-sided LoRA with a moving frame and carried optimizer state (Prop IV.2).
Not claimed
The duality is exact only at degree one, for optimizers that do not read the parameter tensor; decoupled decay, default Adafactor and full-gradient clipping break it.
Credit\(\mathbf{Para}\) and the lens reading of backpropagation are due to Fong, Spivak and Tuyéras and Cruttwell et al., 2021. The ETHER control study that tempers the knowledge-retention reading is Bini et al., 2024.

Three scopes travel with every claim. Expressivity is a different property from learnability. Only Exposé III is dynamical, and each of its results names its optimizer. Mergeability is proved box by box under explicit side conditions, and whole-network statements are open. Most of the mathematics is real algebraic geometry, invariant theory, Lie generation and gradient-flow ODEs; the category theory does its own work in seven places: the interval [frozen, full FT] and simulation; invariants finer than the image; a product that yields LoRA-XS, and meets that organise others; base change along maps that change the function; the lens; naturality of gradient descent and of combination rules; and rank as a min-cut.

Some statements rest on less than a full proof. The equalities in the dimensions of LoHa (Prop II.12) and VeRA are checked numerically; the bounds are proved. For VeRA with \(r\le n\) and generic frozen factors the equality also follows from the toric-cone description of C.14, and the generic equality for DoRA (Prop II.8) is proved. Prop V.4(a) in its continuous case and the converse in Cor III.13 have complete proofs, and Prop IV.3 is argued around Frobenius’ theorem. theory/framework_checks.py re-runs the main numbers in about a minute (see Reproducing the numbers).

Open problems

Each grew out of a proof that stopped short, and each is stated so that a solution would be recognisable.

  1. LoHa’s dimension. Prop II.12 proves \(d(\mathrm{LoHa}_{r_1,r_2})\le\min\big(mn,\,r_1(m+n-r_1)+r_2(m+n-r_2)-(m+n-1)\big)\), with equality checked only numerically. Prove it in general, and decide whether the Hadamard variety is closed.
  2. Initialisations, dynamically. Thm II.2 classifies three strata by expressivity, and Cor III.13 shows that Adam sees \(A_0\) only up to signed row permutations. Classify the apex fibre modulo \(B_r\), stratified by the charge \(\Phi_0\) (Cor III.6), degenerate pointings included.
  3. HRA’s apex in practice. HRA’s paired initialisation, HF PEFT’s default, starts at an apex (Thm II.7(b), for injective \(W_0\) and even \(r\le n-2\)), with \(\dim S_1=kn-\tfrac{k(k+1)}2\), \(k=r/2\), against \(d=rn-\tfrac{r(r+1)}2\). Does it slow early training against a regular start at matched budget?
  4. Charts for meets. LoRA-XS (Prop I.8(a)) and HRA’s image (Thm II.7(a)) are meets with a polynomial chart; LoRA meet output scaling (Prop I.8(b)) is a union of coordinate planes. When does a meet of two named methods admit one?
  5. The frontier. Incomparable pairs are common: split against zero-init LoRA (Thm II.2(d)), Kronecker sums or GraLoRA against LoRA (Thm II.11, Prop II.12(c)). Find the size-\(k\) frontier of \(\mathsf{Meth}\), whose maximal elements are pairwise incomparable.
  6. An Adam analogue of the crossover. Thm III.5 and Thm III.9 hold for gradient flow. Find a conserved or monotone quantity for sign-like updates, whose symmetry is only \(B_r\) (Thm III.12).
  7. Prefixes and prompts. For a vanilla softmax head with \(W_o\ne0\) and \(W_q\ne0\), a generic prefix is not box-locally mergeable (Thm VI.5(f)); settle the whole network. In causal decoders every soft prompt is a deep prefix (Thm VI.5(e)); Petrov, Torr and Bibi and Li and Liang assert that the inclusion is strict, as it is in a one-layer, one-token example. Prove the generic case, and settle encoders.

Conjectures A, C and D are open as well; they appear above as Predictions X.4, X.11 and X.2.

Debts

Much of what this site proves was known in another language or setting. A re-derivation earns its place only if the new language adds something, so the box states what we add next to each debt.

The referee record

Every numbered result went to two independent model referees, who checked it against its proof, the literature and the Hugging Face PEFT source. Of the 41 results, 14 stood as first written, with wording fixes, and 27 were repaired by an added hypothesis, a narrowed claim, a replaced proof or a withdrawn reading. A second review attacked the repaired statements, found a false or overreaching clause in 19 of them, and those were narrowed again. Only the final statements appear on this site; the notes are in theory/propositions.json and theory/round2_verdicts.json.
